# Look Up requirements checklist — part 1: S1 Foundations and S2 Station side (basic)

Built on 5 October 2026 from the extraction slices in `c:\tmp\lookup-workflows\requirements\` (every line tagged `S1` or `S2`, plus lines tagged elsewhere that belong here) and from `status-built.md` (snapshot of commit `318fa2c`, about 05:15 on 5 October 2026).

## How to read this file

- Line shape: `- [ ] **<PHASE>-<AREA>-<number>** Requirement. *Source:* where it is stated [slice lines]. *Verified by:* the test or check that proves it.`
- `- [x]` is used only where `status-built.md` shows the behaviour is committed **and** a named test proves it; the test is given after *Evidence:*. A behaviour that exists in committed code with no test is left `- [ ]` and marked *(built, not yet tested)*. **Status note (review round 1, 5 October 2026).** `status-built.md` is a snapshot of commit `318fa2c`. Six commits have landed on `main` since: `4e9409f` (portal sign-in), `9c310e1` (station access, station profile, clients, audit trail), `5dfd86a` (ads with direct audio uploads), `ce8a7a7` (action cards, schedules, publishing, development seed), `8c69b85` (portal shell, sign-in screen, account page, client error reporting) and `e291d01` (personal values kept out of logs; process failures logged as one JSON line). They added the API test files `authentication.test.ts`, `authentication-when-key-value-store-is-down.test.ts`, `stations-and-clients.test.ts`, `ads-and-uploads.test.ts`, `cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts`, `campaign-time-windows-database.test.ts`, `development-seed.test.ts`, `client-errors.test.ts`, `log-privacy.test.ts` and `process-failures.test.ts`, the contracts test `schedule-schemas.test.ts`, and the portal tests (`error-reporting.test.ts`, `portal-routes.test.tsx`, `api-client.test.ts`, `design-tokens.test.ts`, `change-password-page.test.tsx`). That work is committed but is not in `status-built.md`, so nothing here is ticked on it. Items this file used to call uncommitted, and the items moved here from P6, are marked *(committed after the status snapshot)* with the commit and the test that should prove them; the other S2 items have not yet been re-judged against the new tests. Before the S2 gate, `status-built.md` must be regenerated from `main` with a fresh test run, and every S2 item re-judged against the named tests.
- Square brackets after a source list the slice lines merged into the item, so a reviewer can check that nothing was dropped: `B` = `slice-blueprint.md`, `X` = `slice-plan-cross-cutting.md`, `D` = `slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md`, `P` = `slice-plan-portal.md`, `M` = `slice-plan-mobile.md`, `R` = `slice-plan-recognition.md`, `T` = `status-built.md`; the number is the line number in that file as read on 5 October 2026.
- `plan` = `C:\Users\Luckson\.claude\plans\c-users-luckson-downloads-system-archit-virtual-steele.md`; `blueprint` = `system_architecture_blueprint.md`; `baseline` = `docs/security-baseline.md`; `conventions` = `docs/conventions.md`; `global rules` = the "Error handling, logging & tests" rules in the user's CLAUDE.md; `memory/<file>` = the user's standing directives; `<Name>` wireframe = `c:\tmp\lookup-workflows\wireframes\<Name>.dc.html`; `design S2-Dn` = decision n in `docs/design/step-2-station-side.md` (the Step 2 build specification, recorded as a built document in `status-built.md`).
- Names follow the readable-names standing requirement of 4 October 2026, which is newer than the master plan's short names (`fp`, `recog`, `app_api`, `TenantTx`, `t_off` and so on). The plan's name is given in brackets where it helps tracing.
- Retired ids. An item moved to another phase or merged into another item during review is replaced by a line `- ~~<id>~~ — Retired …` that names where it went. A retired id is never reused.
- *Awaiting user confirmation* marks built behaviour that departs from the approved sources; it stays unticked until the user accepts the departure, and the acceptance date is then added as a source.
- Items whose source is only `status-built.md` record behaviour that is built (usually from a Step 2 design decision or the Phase 0 tools) but that no plan sentence states. They are kept so the built and tested behaviour stays covered; they are marked "status-built.md only".

## Conflicts between sources, and what this file chose

1. **API test runner.** Plan Pinned stack and Step 1 say jest 30.5.2 + ts-jest 29.4.14; the newer Execution gate (4 Oct 2026) says "API vitest", and the repository uses vitest 5.0.3 + supertest 7.3.1. Chosen: vitest (S1-STACK-15).
2. **Request validation library.** Plan Pinned stack and "Reused from Tonse-hub" say class-validator / class-transformer with a `ValidationPipe` (`whitelist`, `forbidNonWhitelisted`); `security-baseline.md` §2 and the built code use a strict zod validation pipe that refuses unknown fields. Chosen: the zod pipe, refusing (not stripping) unknown fields (S1-REQUESTS-16, S2-STACK-02).
3. **Action card validation.** Plan Step 2, G10 and the data model say cards are validated at run time by ajv against `packages/contracts/action-card.schema.json`; design S2-D8 (newer, built) writes the contract once in zod, emits the JSON Schema for the app and keeps ajv as a test-time cross-check. Chosen: S2-D8, as the newest dated decision; the requirement keeps the plan's substance ("one shared contract, unknown fields refused") and needs the user's confirmation (S2-CARDS-01, S2-STACK-12). Review round 1: S2-STACK-04 now ticks only the proven part (the JSON Schema compiles in ajv and agrees with zod); the supersession itself is the open item S2-STACK-12.
4. **Action type spellings.** Blueprint §5.1 and plan G10 say `ACTION_LINK`, `ACTION_DIAL`, `ACTION_WHATSAPP`, `ACTION_MAP`; the built contract (design S2-D9) says `LINK`, `CALL`, `WHATSAPP`, `MAP`. Chosen: the requirement names the four kinds; the contract's spelling is the one every client shares (S2-CARDS-03). Needs confirmation before Step 4 builds against it.
5. **Local object storage.** Plan Pinned stack says MinIO; design S2-D1 (newer) replaces it with versitygw v1.8.0 because MinIO community images can no longer be pulled. Chosen: S2-D1 for the product; the plan's ports and private bucket are kept (S2-INFRASTRUCTURE-01).
6. **Enqueueing `ingest` at upload completion.** Plan Step 2 says `POST /ads/:id/complete` enqueues `ingest`; design S2-D12 (newer) leaves the queue producer to Step 3. Chosen: the item stays in S2 as the plan says, marked "deferral proposed by S2-D12"; it may move to S3 only with the user's acknowledgement (S2-ADS-07, S2-LOGGING-04).
7. **Refresh-token reuse.** Baseline §3 and the plan say a reused refresh token revokes the whole family; the built database layer (design S2-D3) allows a used token to be presented up to three times within 30 seconds (lost responses, two tabs). Chosen: the baseline requirement is kept unticked (S2-SESSIONS-11); the grace is recorded as built behaviour that needs the user's acceptance (S2-SESSIONS-24, unticked in review round 1 and marked awaiting user confirmation; baseline §3 must be amended if the user accepts it).
8. **Token library.** Plan pins `@nestjs/jwt` 12.0.2 and says Tonse-hub's JWT pattern is reused; design S2-D3 uses `jose` 6.2.12. Kept as the plan states, unticked, flagged (S2-STACK-01, S2-SIGN-IN-14).
9. **NestJS version.** Repo layout says NestJS 11; Pinned stack says 12.1.2. Chosen: 12.1.2.
10. **Node version.** Target architecture says "Node 22/24"; Pinned stack says 24; the root `package.json` allows `>=22.12`. Chosen: Node 24 (pinned).
11. **Partitioning.** G12 says "monthly partitions later"; the standing requirement and conventions say now (built). Chosen: now.
12. **Idempotency uniqueness.** Master data model puts `UNIQUE(install_id, idempotency_key)` on `recognition_events`; the standing requirement moves it to a separate unpartitioned table (built). Chosen: the separate table.
13. **API folder layout.** Repo layout says `src/common/{errors,logging,context,db}`; the standing requirement says `config/`, `infrastructure/`, `common/`, `features/<feature>/`. Chosen: the standing requirement (newer).
14. **Debug-log flag.** Plan says `LOG_DEBUG=false`; baseline and code say `LOG_REQUEST_DETAILS`. Chosen: `LOG_REQUEST_DETAILS`.
15. **Password change revokes sessions.** `slice-plan-portal.md` line 44 tags it P5A; baseline §3 marks it "Next" (Step 2) and the Step 2 database layer builds it. Chosen: S2 (earliest phase that delivers it) (S2-SESSIONS-14).
16. **Non-ACTIVE station guard.** The plan tests it in Phase 5a; the routes it protects ship in Step 2 and a new station defaults to `PENDING_VERIFICATION`. Chosen: S2 (S2-AUTHORIZATION-04).
17. **Error filter never logs a raw user id** (`slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md` line 195, tagged S1). No request carries a user before sign-in exists. Chosen: merged into S2-LOGGING-01.
18. **Recognition event id as UUIDv7** (`slice-blueprint.md` line 317, tagged S1). The id is generated by `POST /recognize` (Step 3), as the data-model slice says. Chosen: S1 keeps only the table (S1-RECOGNITION-EVENTS-08); the UUIDv7 public id stays with S3.
19. **SECURITY DEFINER functions `mark_ingested`, `resolve`, `record_recognition`.** Plan Step 1 lists them in the Foundation migration; they were not built, and the baseline and the slices place them in Step 3. Chosen: left to the S3 section (not repeated here); recorded in the gaps.
20. **Leak patterns.** The plan's regex lacks "Fatal error", which the global rules require; the built pattern file lacks `Traceback` and "Fatal error". Chosen: all of them are required (S1-TESTS-08, S1-TESTS-09).
21. **Station-scoped route paths.** The plan writes `/ads`, `/ads/:id/...`, `/stations/me`; design S2-D10 puts station work under `/api/v1/stations/{stationId}/...`. Requirements below use the plan's route names; the path shape follows S2-D10 once the user confirms it.
22. **Touch targets.** Plan §Mobile D asks for 44 px minimum; several wireframes draw smaller controls. Chosen: 44 px (S2-ACCESSIBILITY-02).
23. **Client error reports (review round 1).** Part 3 placed `POST /api/v1/client-errors` and the portal's reporting in P6 because plan Phases row 6 lists "global error reporting". Both were built in Step 2 (commit `8c69b85`), and the global rules require client crashes to reach the developer from the first build that has error handlers. Chosen: **S2** (S2-CLIENT-ERRORS-01 to -07; the old P6 ids are retired). The listener app's sending moves to S4 in part 3; only offline queueing of app reports stays in P6.
24. **Map button form (review round 1).** The built shared builder makes a Google Maps search address; the plan's slice verification ("Map opens directions"), blueprint §5.1 ("Navigation Directions … with destination coordinates") and the Result wireframe ("Get directions") ask for directions. Chosen: **directions** (S2-BUTTON-ADDRESSES-04, unticked until the builder and its fixtures change).
25. **Definition-of-done step 9 (review round 1).** S1-ISOLATION-01 claimed MVP step 9 on a test that covers only clients and stations. Chosen: the S1 items are narrowed to what the test proves, and step 9 moves to P8-MVP-DONE-04 in part 5.

- [ ] **S1-REPOSITORY-05** `.gitignore` covers dependency folders and build output (for example `node_modules`, `dist`), so no generated file is committed; field audio in the spike corpus and benchmark reports are ignored too. *Source:* plan §Step 1 Monorepo (line 103) [D606, T387]. *Verified by:* `git check-ignore node_modules dist` succeeds. *(built, not yet tested)*

- [ ] **S1-REPOSITORY-07** The lockfile (`package-lock.json`) is committed. *Source:* baseline §9 row 2 [X362, T390]. *Verified by:* the lockfile is tracked in git and `npm ci` succeeds. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-REPOSITORY-08** Workspace `package.json` files pin exact dependency versions (no `^` or `~` ranges), the only exception being the plan's TypeScript `~6.0.3` pin. *Source:* baseline §9 row 2 [X361, T390]. *Verified by:* a script check of every workspace dependency list. *(built, not yet tested)*

- [ ] **S1-INFRASTRUCTURE-06** Local Valkey listens on 127.0.0.1 only. *Source:* baseline §9 row 1 [X359, T385]. *Verified by:* the compose file publishes port 56379 bound to 127.0.0.1. *(built, not yet tested)*

### Configuration and secrets

- [x] **S1-CONFIGURATION-01** Configuration is one zod schema, validated once at start-up, and the API refuses to start on an unsafe value (fail closed). *Source:* plan §Step 1 Config (line 107); plan §Reused from Tonse-hub (line 83); baseline §6 row 1 [D96, X329, T165, T170]. *Verified by:* start-up test: an unsafe configuration stops start-up before any request is served. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/startup-checks.test.ts :: production configuration fails closed > refuses an unencrypted database connection.
- [ ] **S1-CONFIGURATION-02** The API refuses to start when a required configuration value is missing. *Source:* baseline §6 row 1 [X330, T170]. *Verified by:* start-up test with each required variable unset in turn. *(built, not yet tested: `apps/api/src/config/app-config.ts` refuses missing values, but `startup-checks.test.ts` unsets only `AUTHENTICATION_SECRET` and, in production, `DATABASE_TLS_MODE`, not each required variable in turn)*
- [x] **S1-CONFIGURATION-03** The API refuses to start on a weak or placeholder secret. *Source:* plan §Reused from Tonse-hub (line 83); baseline §6 row 1 [D96, X330]. *Verified by:* start-up test: a short secret and a placeholder such as `change-me` each stop start-up. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/startup-checks.test.ts :: production configuration fails closed > refuses a missing, short or placeholder authentication secret, in any environment.
- [x] **S1-CONFIGURATION-04** A configuration error names the setting and the reason and never prints the value. *Source:* baseline §6 row 1 [X331, D96, T169]. *Verified by:* start-up test: the error output contains the setting name but not the bad value. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/startup-checks.test.ts :: configuration errors never print secret values > names the bad variable without echoing values.
- [ ] **S1-CONFIGURATION-05** The database configuration has no default credentials: with the database user or password unset, both the API and the migration script refuse to start. *Source:* plan §Reused from Tonse-hub, Do differently (line 95) [D116, T25, T170]. *Verified by:* start-up test and a migration-script test with the credentials unset. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-CONFIGURATION-06** The database setup address (`DATABASE_SETUP_URL`) is read only by the setup scripts; the running API's configuration has no such setting and no migrator address. *Source:* baseline §6 row 3; plan §Do differently (line 95); plan §Roles and RLS (line 378) [X334, D572, T26]. *Verified by:* configuration test: the API's schema has no setup address; setting it does not change the API's connection. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [x] **S1-CONFIGURATION-07** In production the database connection must be encrypted, or the API refuses to start. *Source:* baseline §1 row 11; blueprint §3.1 line 67 (kept, extended to the database link) [X240, B157, T166]. *Verified by:* start-up test: production with encryption mode "disable" or unset fails. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/startup-checks.test.ts :: production configuration fails closed > refuses an unencrypted database connection.
- [x] **S1-CONFIGURATION-08** In production every allowed browser origin must be https, or the API refuses to start. *Source:* baseline §2 row 4 [X259, B156, T167]. *Verified by:* start-up test with an `http://` origin in a production configuration. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/startup-checks.test.ts :: production configuration fails closed > refuses non-https browser origins.
- [x] **S1-CONFIGURATION-09** In every environment, allowed browser origins must be exact bare origins (no path, no wildcard), read as a list. *Source:* baseline §2 row 4 ("exact allowed origins") [B156, T165, T168]. *Verified by:* start-up test with a path and a wildcard entry. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/startup-checks.test.ts :: production configuration fails closed > refuses entries that are not bare origins, in any environment ; apps/api/test/startup-checks.test.ts :: production configuration fails closed > accepts a sound production configuration.
- [ ] **S1-CONFIGURATION-10** Configuration refuses a database address that is not postgres, a key-value store address that is not redis, a port outside 1 to 65,535 (default 3100) and a log level outside a fixed list (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §5 [T170]. *Verified by:* start-up test per bad value. *(built, not yet tested: `apps/api/src/config/app-config.ts`; the only bad value tested is a key-value store address that is not a URL at all, in `startup-checks.test.ts` :: configuration errors never print secret values)*
- [ ] **S1-CONFIGURATION-11** The database setup configuration refuses role passwords shorter than 16 characters and names any missing setup variable (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §1 [T25]. *Verified by:* setup-script test with a 15-character password and with a variable unset. *(built, not yet tested)*

### API structure

- [ ] **S1-STRUCTURE-01** Every API route sits under the global prefix `/api/v1` (kept from blueprint §7 path `/api/v1/recognize`). *Source:* plan §Step 1 Startup (line 106); blueprint §7 line 224 [D607, B339, T121]. *Verified by:* API test that walks the router's registered routes and asserts each path starts with `/api/v1`; the same path without the prefix gives the 404 envelope. *(built, not yet tested: the cited test, apps/api/test/health-and-request-ids.test.ts :: health and request ids > reports ok when the database and key-value store answer, reaches only the health route, so it cannot prove "every route"; unticked in review round 1)*
- [x] **S1-STRUCTURE-02** The API skeleton has its error, logging and request-context layers, used by the database access too (the plan's `src/common/{errors,logging,context,db}`, laid out under the standing structure in `common/` and `infrastructure/`). *Source:* plan §Phases row 1 (line 643); plan §Repo layout, apps/api (line 336); plan §Standing requirements (line 20) [X482, D328]. *Verified by:* `error-responses.test.ts` and `health-and-request-ids.test.ts`; structure check. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > (every case, through expectErrorEnvelope) ; apps/api/test/health-and-request-ids.test.ts :: health and request ids > echoes a well-formed request id from the caller.
- [x] **S1-STRUCTURE-03** `apps/api/src/main.ts` is the process entry: it starts the server and does last-resort error logging. *Source:* conventions §Structure (API) [X390, X117, T130]. *Verified by:* structure check plus the process tests of S1-ERRORS-25 and S1-ERRORS-26. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/process-failures.test.ts :: process failures are logged as one structured line, then the process exits > an unreachable database at start-up: one fatal JSON line with the stack, no password, nothing else, exit 1 ; apps/api/test/process-failures.test.ts :: process failures are logged as one structured line, then the process exits > a normal start writes only JSON lines, including the messages Nest printed while starting.
- [ ] **S1-STRUCTURE-04** `app.module.ts` wires infrastructure first, then features. *Source:* conventions §Structure [X391]. *Verified by:* code review of the import order. *(built, not yet tested: `apps/api/src/app.module.ts` imports configuration, logging, request context, database, key-value store, object storage and the common modules before the features; code review only)*
- [ ] **S1-STRUCTURE-05** `configure-http-application.ts` holds the HTTP pipeline in order and is shared with the tests, so tests run the same pipeline as production. *Source:* conventions §Structure [X392, T131, T391]. *Verified by:* the test support file (`test/support/test-application.ts`) builds the application through it. *(built, not yet tested: `main.ts` and `test/support/test-application.ts` both build the application through `configureHttpApplication`, which meets the Verified-by as a structure check, but no test asserts it)*
- [x] **S1-STRUCTURE-06** `config/` holds validated configuration that fails closed. *Source:* conventions §Structure; plan §Standing requirements (line 20) [X393]. *Verified by:* structure check plus `startup-checks.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/startup-checks.test.ts :: production configuration fails closed > refuses an unencrypted database connection ; apps/api/test/startup-checks.test.ts :: production configuration fails closed > refuses a missing, short or placeholder authentication secret, in any environment.
- [ ] **S1-STRUCTURE-07** `infrastructure/` holds what features rely on: database, key-value store and logging. *Source:* conventions §Structure [X394]. *Verified by:* structure check. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-STRUCTURE-08** `common/` holds cross-cutting rules: errors, HTTP guards, rate limiting and validation. *Source:* conventions §Structure [X395]. *Verified by:* structure check. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-STRUCTURE-09** `apps/api/scripts/` holds database setup only: roles and migrations. *Source:* conventions §Structure [X397]. *Verified by:* structure check (the Step 2 seed script will join it; see S2-SEED-05). *Built differently: `apps/api/scripts/` also holds the development seed (`seed-development-data.ts`, `sample-ads.ts`, `sample-audio.ts`) and the object storage bucket set-up (`prepare-object-storage.ts`), as conventions §Structure (API) now lists, decision conventions §Structure (API) with S2-D1; awaiting user confirmation.*
- [ ] **S1-STRUCTURE-10** The API enables shutdown hooks, so a stop signal closes the server, the database pool and the key-value connection cleanly. *Source:* plan §Step 1 Startup (line 106) [D610, T163]. *Verified by:* integration test: SIGTERM ends the process with exit code 0, no open handles and no lost in-flight request. *(built, not yet tested)*

### Names

- [x] **S1-NAMING-01** The schema holding the fingerprint store is named `fingerprints`; the earlier name `fp` is used nowhere. *Source:* plan §Standing requirements (line 18) [D21, T24]. *Verified by:* database test: schema `fingerprints` exists and no schema `fp` exists. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > names > S1-NAMING-01, S1-NAMING-02, S1-MIGRATIONS-06: schemas app, fingerprints and recognition exist, owned by the schema owner; fp and recog do not.
- [x] **S1-NAMING-02** The schema holding the matching functions is named `recognition`; the earlier name `recog` is used nowhere. *Source:* conventions §Names in the database; plan §Standing requirements (line 18) [D22, T24]. *Verified by:* database test: schema `recognition` exists and no schema `recog` exists. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > names > S1-NAMING-01, S1-NAMING-02, S1-MIGRATIONS-06: schemas app, fingerprints and recognition exist, owned by the schema owner; fp and recog do not.
- [x] **S1-NAMING-03** The database roles are named `lookup_api`, `lookup_admin_api`, `lookup_fingerprint_worker` and `lookup_schema_owner`; the earlier names `app_api`, `app_admin`, `app_fp` and `lookup_owner` are not used. *Source:* plan §Standing requirements (line 18); conventions §Names in the database [D23, T22]. *Verified by:* database test: exactly these four application roles exist and none of the earlier names does. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > names > S1-NAMING-03: exactly the four Look Up roles exist; the earlier names do not.
- [x] **S1-NAMING-04** The helper that opens a station-scoped transaction is `StationScopedTransaction` in `station-scoped-transaction.ts`; the text `TenantTx` appears nowhere in `apps/`. *Source:* plan §Standing requirements (line 18); conventions §Names in code [D24, T51]. *Verified by:* code scan. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/source-scan.test.ts :: source scan > S1-NAMING-04, S1-NAMING-05: no TenantTx and no "tenant" anywhere in production source ; apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > StationScopedTransaction on pooled connections > S1-ISOLATION-09, S1-ISOLATION-10: the station context lasts only for its transaction; the next request on the same connection sees nothing.
- [x] **S1-NAMING-05** Tables use the project's domain words: `stations` (not `tenants`), `ads` (not `media_items`), `action_cards` (not `actionable_payloads`), `campaigns` and `campaign_time_windows` (not `campaign_schedules`), `recognition_events` (not `recognition_logs`); no `tenant` appears in production code (supersedes blueprint §6 table names). *Source:* blueprint §6 lines 142–216; conventions §Names in code and §Names in the database [B261]. *Verified by:* database test lists the table names; code search for `tenant`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > names > S1-NAMING-05, S1-MIGRATIONS-10: the slice tables exist under their domain names; the blueprint names do not ; apps/api/test/source-scan.test.ts :: source scan > S1-NAMING-04, S1-NAMING-05: no TenantTx and no "tenant" anywhere in production source.
- [x] **S1-NAMING-06** No column is named with a Postgres reserved word such as `offset`. *Source:* plan §Verified facts, Postgres (line 260); plan G1 (line 283) [D181]. *Verified by:* all migrations run on a clean Postgres 16 without quoting any identifier. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > names > S1-NAMING-06: no column is named with a reserved SQL word.
- [x] **S1-NAMING-07** Every database constraint has an explicit name following the conventions (no system-generated `_pkey`, `_fkey` or `_check` names). *Source:* plan §Reused from Tonse-hub (line 84); conventions §Names in the database [D97]. *Verified by:* database catalogue test of constraint names. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > names > S1-NAMING-07: every constraint has a readable name ending in its kind (no generated _pkey, _fkey or PK_ names).
- [ ] **S1-NAMING-08** The same naming rules apply to the Phase 0 tools in `spike/` and `services/fingerprinter/bench`. *Source:* conventions §Names in code; plan §Execution "Capture-app rename" (line 30) [X384]. *Verified by:* naming scan over `spike/` and `services/fingerprinter/bench`. *(built, not yet tested: commit `8ba386b` spelled out the names in `spike/capture_app` and `services/fingerprinter/bench`; `source-scan.test.ts` reads only `apps/api`, `apps/portal` and `packages/contracts`)*
- [x] **S1-NAMING-09** Manifests already on disk keep loading: old short manifest column names load with every value intact. *Source:* conventions §Names in code [X385, T346, T348]. *Verified by:* pytest with an old-format manifest. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_legacy_column_names.py :: test_load_manifest_reads_old_column_names ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_legacy_column_names.py :: test_append_rows_accepts_rows_with_old_column_names.
- [ ] **S1-NAMING-10** Capture-app data already on disk keeps its format: old clip file names (for example `…_neg_…_8s_…_t1.m4a`) and recorded values such as `mic/ns-off/agc-off` still parse. *Source:* conventions §Names in code [X385]. *Verified by:* pytest: old-format clip names and recorded values still parse. *(built, not yet tested: `services/fingerprinter/tests/test_import_captures.py` :: test_import_merges_session_and_widens_old_header reads `mic/ns-off/agc-off`, and `spike/capture_app/test/widget_test.dart` :: file names are safe and carry every label the benchmark groups by shows the clip-name format unchanged, but no test parses an old clip name)*
- [x] **S1-NAMING-11** The capture importer renames old manifest columns to the readable names when it reads them, and appending to an old manifest rewrites it in the current names. *Source:* conventions §Names in code ("which the importer renames on read") [X386, T345, T347]. *Verified by:* pytest with an old manifest. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_legacy_column_names.py :: test_current_header_is_the_old_header_with_full_names ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_legacy_column_names.py :: test_append_rows_rewrites_an_old_corpus_manifest_in_current_names.

### Database roles

- [ ] **S1-ROLES-01** Each service connects to the database as its own role: the API as `lookup_api`, the super-admin API as `lookup_admin_api`, the fingerprint worker as `lookup_fingerprint_worker`. *Source:* baseline §1 row 1 [X211, T22]. *Verified by:* database test: the three roles exist; integration test: `current_user` of each service's connection. *(built, not yet tested: the three login roles exist with their attributes (`database-catalogue.test.ts`) and the API is accepted as `lookup_api` at start-up (`startup-checks.test.ts`), but only the API connects today; the super-admin API and the fingerprint worker are not built yet)*
- [x] **S1-ROLES-02** The owner role `lookup_schema_owner` (plan name `lookup_owner`) cannot log in and owns every database object: every schema, table and function. *Source:* plan §Roles and RLS (line 378); plan G2 (line 284); baseline §1 row 1 [D571, D219, X213, B145, T22]. *Verified by:* database test: `rolcanlogin` is false; every table, function and schema in `app`, `fingerprints` and `recognition` is owned by it. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > roles > S1-ROLES-02, S1-ROLES-05, S1-ROLES-06, S1-ROLES-07, S1-ROLES-04: the owner cannot sign in; service roles can, with no superuser, no BYPASSRLS, no role or database creation ; apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > roles > S1-ROLES-02, S1-MIGRATIONS-01, S1-MIGRATIONS-03: the schema owner owns every table, index, sequence and function (apart from extension members, which Postgres gives to the installing superuser) ; apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > names > S1-NAMING-01, S1-NAMING-02, S1-MIGRATIONS-06: schemas app, fingerprints and recognition exist, owned by the schema owner; fp and recog do not.
- [x] **S1-ROLES-03** No application ever connects as the owner role, because table owners bypass row-level security; the API's and the worker's connection strings never name it. *Source:* plan §Verified facts, Postgres (line 262); plan G2 (line 284) [D183, D219, B145]. *Verified by:* configuration review plus the start-up check of S1-STARTUP-03. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > roles > S1-ROLES-02, S1-ROLES-05, S1-ROLES-06, S1-ROLES-07, S1-ROLES-04: the owner cannot sign in; service roles can, with no superuser, no BYPASSRLS, no role or database creation ; apps/api/test/startup-checks.test.ts :: startup refuses an unsafe database role > rejects a superuser connection (row-level security would not apply).
- [x] **S1-ROLES-04** `lookup_api` (plan name `app_api`) can log in, is not a superuser and cannot bypass row-level security. *Source:* plan §Roles and RLS (line 379); baseline §1 row 1 [D573, X212, B147, T23]. *Verified by:* database test of `rolsuper` and `rolbypassrls`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/startup-checks.test.ts :: startup refuses an unsafe database role > accepts the restricted lookup_api role.
- [x] **S1-ROLES-05** `lookup_admin_api` (plan name `app_admin`) can log in, is not a superuser, cannot bypass row-level security and owns nothing; it sees all stations' rows only through explicit full-access policies (`USING (true)`), and without a policy it sees nothing. *Source:* plan §Roles and RLS (line 380); baseline §1 row 1 [D575, X212, B147, T52]. *Verified by:* database test of the role attributes; each admin policy exists by name. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > roles > S1-ROLES-02, S1-ROLES-05, S1-ROLES-06, S1-ROLES-07, S1-ROLES-04: the owner cannot sign in; service roles can, with no superuser, no BYPASSRLS, no role or database creation ; apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > roles > S1-ROLES-05: service roles own nothing at all ; apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > roles > S1-ROLES-05: every station table has an explicit full-access policy for the super-admin role ; apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > what each role may do > S1-PRIVILEGES-23, S1-ROLES-05: the super-admin role reads every station, changes only statuses, and never sees password hashes.
- [x] **S1-ROLES-06** `lookup_fingerprint_worker` (plan name `app_fp`) can log in, is not a superuser and cannot bypass row-level security. *Source:* plan §Roles and RLS (line 381); baseline §1 row 1 [D577, X212, B147]. *Verified by:* database test of the role attributes. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > roles > S1-ROLES-02, S1-ROLES-05, S1-ROLES-06, S1-ROLES-07, S1-ROLES-04: the owner cannot sign in; service roles can, with no superuser, no BYPASSRLS, no role or database creation.
- [x] **S1-ROLES-07** The design does not depend on `BYPASSRLS`: no Look Up role has it, and super-admin access is granted through explicit policies instead. *Source:* plan §Verified facts, Postgres (line 263); plan §Roles and RLS (lines 379–380) [D185]. *Verified by:* database test: `rolbypassrls` is false for every Look Up role; the admin role reads all stations through its policy. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > roles > S1-ROLES-02, S1-ROLES-05, S1-ROLES-06, S1-ROLES-07, S1-ROLES-04: the owner cannot sign in; service roles can, with no superuser, no BYPASSRLS, no role or database creation ; apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > what each role may do > S1-PRIVILEGES-23, S1-ROLES-05: the super-admin role reads every station, changes only statuses, and never sees password hashes.
- [ ] **S1-ROLES-08** The roles script (`apps/api/scripts/create-database-roles.ts`; plan name `create-roles.ts`) creates the owner role and the three login roles through a setup connection that may create roles, takes every password from the environment, and can be re-run (it updates roles in place). *Source:* plan §Step 1 DB roles script (line 116) [D622, T22]. *Verified by:* script test on a clean database: the four roles exist afterwards; a second run is harmless; no password literal is in the source. *(built, not yet tested: `apps/api/test/setup/prepare-test-database.ts` runs it before every API test run, so a re-run is exercised, but no test asserts its result or scans it for password literals)*
- [x] **S1-ROLES-09** Each application role has a fixed search path: `app` for the two API roles, `fingerprints, app` for the fingerprint worker (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §1 [T28]. *Verified by:* database test of each role's `search_path` setting. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > roles > S1-ROLES-09, S1-ROLES-10: lookup_api has a fixed search path and server-side time limits ; apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > roles > S1-ROLES-09, S1-ROLES-11: lookup_admin_api has a fixed search path and server-side time limits ; apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > roles > S1-ROLES-09, S1-ROLES-12: lookup_fingerprint_worker has a fixed search path and server-side time limits.
- [x] **S1-ROLES-10** `lookup_api` has server-side limits that apply even if the application forgets its own: statement timeout 5 seconds, lock wait 3 seconds, idle in transaction 15 seconds. *Source:* baseline §1 row 9 [X236, X237, X238, T29]. *Verified by:* database test: `pg_sleep` past the limit is cancelled; the role's settings include `lock_timeout` and `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts :: database privileges of the API role > database-wide > server-side timeouts apply to the API role even if the application forgets its own.
- [x] **S1-ROLES-11** `lookup_admin_api` has server-side limits: statement timeout 15 seconds, lock wait 5 seconds, idle in transaction 30 seconds. *Source:* baseline §1 row 9 [X236, X237, X238, T30]. *Verified by:* database test of the role's settings. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > roles > S1-ROLES-09, S1-ROLES-11: lookup_admin_api has a fixed search path and server-side time limits.
- [x] **S1-ROLES-12** `lookup_fingerprint_worker` has server-side limits: statement timeout 60 seconds, lock wait 10 seconds, idle in transaction 60 seconds. *Source:* baseline §1 row 9 [X236, X237, X238, T30]. *Verified by:* database test of the role's settings. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > roles > S1-ROLES-09, S1-ROLES-12: lookup_fingerprint_worker has a fixed search path and server-side time limits.
- [x] **S1-ROLES-13** PUBLIC cannot connect to the database. *Source:* baseline §1 row 8 [X232, B150, T31]. *Verified by:* database test: a fresh role without grants cannot connect. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts :: database privileges of the API role > database-wide > PUBLIC can neither connect nor use the public schema.
- [x] **S1-ROLES-14** PUBLIC cannot create temporary tables, and neither can the API role. *Source:* baseline §1 row 8 [X233, B150, T31, T33]. *Verified by:* database test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts :: database privileges of the API role > database-wide > PUBLIC can neither connect nor use the public schema ; apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts :: database privileges of the API role > database-wide > cannot create temporary tables.
- [x] **S1-ROLES-15** PUBLIC has no use of the `public` schema. *Source:* baseline §1 row 8; plan §Step 1 Migration (line 118) [X234, D624, B150, T31]. *Verified by:* database test: `CREATE TABLE public.x` as a service role fails. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts :: database privileges of the API role > database-wide > PUBLIC can neither connect nor use the public schema.
- [x] **S1-ROLES-16** PUBLIC cannot execute any function in the `app`, `fingerprints` or `recognition` schemas unless granted (default privileges revoke function execution). *Source:* baseline §1 row 8 [X235, B150, T32]. *Verified by:* database catalogue test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts :: database privileges of the API role > database-wide > no function of ours is executable by PUBLIC (protects future SECURITY DEFINER functions).
- [x] **S1-ROLES-17** Every SECURITY DEFINER function pins its `search_path`, with `pg_catalog` first and `pg_temp` last. *Source:* baseline §1 row 13 [X242, T177]. *Verified by:* catalogue test: every `prosecdef` function has a `search_path` in `proconfig`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > privileges > every SECURITY DEFINER function pins its search path with pg_catalog first and pg_temp last.
- [ ] **S1-ROLES-18** Every SECURITY DEFINER function returns only the columns its caller needs. *Source:* baseline §1 row 13 [X243, T179]. *Verified by:* per-function test: the result columns equal the documented list (tested so far only for the hash-settings lookup, S2-SIGN-IN-09). *(built, not yet tested)*

### Migrations and schemas

- [x] **S1-MIGRATIONS-01** Schema changes are raw-SQL TypeORM migrations; the Foundation migration is raw SQL run as the owner role. *Source:* plan §Reused from Tonse-hub (line 84); plan §Step 1 Migration (line 117) [D97, D623, T27]. *Verified by:* migration test: after it runs, every created object is owned by `lookup_schema_owner`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > roles > S1-ROLES-02, S1-MIGRATIONS-01, S1-MIGRATIONS-03: the schema owner owns every table, index, sequence and function (apart from extension members, which Postgres gives to the installing superuser).
- [x] **S1-MIGRATIONS-02** Migrations run as a separate step before deployment, never at application start; the running API never synchronises or changes the schema, and `lookup_api` has no `CREATE` on any schema. *Source:* plan §Do differently (line 94); plan §Render topology (line 329); baseline §1 row 12 [D114, X241, B405, T27]. *Verified by:* start-up test: starting the API against an un-migrated database creates no tables; privilege test: `lookup_api` lacks `CREATE` (the baseline lists no proving test — see gaps). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > roles > S1-MIGRATIONS-02: no service role can create anything in any schema or in the database ; apps/api/test/source-scan.test.ts :: source scan > S1-MIGRATIONS-02: the application never synchronises or migrates the schema itself.
- [x] **S1-MIGRATIONS-03** Migrations run as the owner role, reached through a separate migrator login that the running API never uses. *Source:* plan §Roles and RLS (line 378); baseline §1 and §6 [D572, T27]. *Verified by:* migration-script test: created objects are owned by `lookup_schema_owner`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > roles > S1-ROLES-02, S1-MIGRATIONS-01, S1-MIGRATIONS-03: the schema owner owns every table, index, sequence and function (apart from extension members, which Postgres gives to the installing superuser).
- [x] **S1-MIGRATIONS-04** One shared database configuration serves both the migration command line and the running API. *Source:* plan §Do differently (line 95); plan §Step 1 Database (line 112) [D115]. *Verified by:* code review: exactly one data-source definition, imported by both the migration script and the API module. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/source-scan.test.ts :: source scan > S1-MIGRATIONS-04: one data-source definition, used by both the API and the migration script.
- [ ] **S1-MIGRATIONS-05** Each migration runs in its own transaction, and the latest migration can be reverted (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §1 [T27]. *Verified by:* migration test: apply, revert and re-apply the latest migration on a scratch database. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [x] **S1-MIGRATIONS-06** Three schemas exist, all owned by the owner role: `app` (business data), `fingerprints` (plan name `fp`) and `recognition` (plan name `recog`). *Source:* plan §Step 1 Migration (line 118); conventions §Names in the database [D624, T24]. *Verified by:* database test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > names > S1-NAMING-01, S1-NAMING-02, S1-MIGRATIONS-06: schemas app, fingerprints and recognition exist, owned by the schema owner; fp and recog do not.
- [x] **S1-MIGRATIONS-07** The `citext` extension is installed, for case-insensitive email. *Source:* plan §Data model intro (line 351) [D364, T76]. *Verified by:* database test: `pg_extension` lists `citext`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > extensions and ids > S1-MIGRATIONS-07, S1-MIGRATIONS-08, S1-MIGRATIONS-09: citext and btree_gist are installed, uuid-ossp is not, and ids default to gen_random_uuid().
- [x] **S1-MIGRATIONS-08** The `btree_gist` extension is installed, for the campaign exclusion constraint. *Source:* plan §Data model intro (line 351) [D365, T83]. *Verified by:* database test: `pg_extension` lists `btree_gist`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > extensions and ids > S1-MIGRATIONS-07, S1-MIGRATIONS-08, S1-MIGRATIONS-09: citext and btree_gist are installed, uuid-ossp is not, and ids default to gen_random_uuid().
- [x] **S1-MIGRATIONS-09** Ids are generated with the built-in `gen_random_uuid()`; the `uuid-ossp` extension is not installed (supersedes blueprint §6 `CREATE EXTENSION "uuid-ossp"`). *Source:* plan §Data model intro (line 351); blueprint §6 lines 139, 143 [D363, B262]. *Verified by:* database test: `pg_extension` has no `uuid-ossp`; low-volume tables default their id to `gen_random_uuid()`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > extensions and ids > S1-MIGRATIONS-07, S1-MIGRATIONS-08, S1-MIGRATIONS-09: citext and btree_gist are installed, uuid-ossp is not, and ids default to gen_random_uuid().
- [x] **S1-MIGRATIONS-10** The Foundation migration creates the slice tables: `stations`, `portal_users`, `station_memberships`, `refresh_tokens`, `clients`, `action_cards`, `ads`, `campaigns`, `campaign_time_windows`, `fingerprints.audio_assets`, `fingerprints.hashes_version_1`, `listener_installs`, `recognition_events` and `audit_events`. *Source:* plan §Step 1 Migration (line 119) [D625, T75–T92]. *Verified by:* database catalogue test: all fourteen tables exist after migration, and reverting the migration removes them. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > names > S1-NAMING-05, S1-MIGRATIONS-10: the slice tables exist under their domain names; the blueprint names do not.
- [x] **S1-MIGRATIONS-11** Phase 1 foundations exist: the database roles, the schemas and the row-level-security migration. *Source:* plan §Phases row 1 (line 643) [X481]. *Verified by:* `startup-checks.test.ts` and `station-isolation.test.ts` pass. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/startup-checks.test.ts :: startup refuses an unsafe database role > accepts the restricted lookup_api role ; apps/api/test/station-isolation.test.ts :: row-level security between stations > a station sees only its own clients and itself.

### Station isolation (row-level security)

- [x] **S1-ISOLATION-01** Many independent radio stations share one deployment, and with station A's context the API role reads none of station B's clients and does not see station B's station row. *Source:* blueprint §1 line 9 and §3 line 24 (kept); plan §Phases row 1 Verify (line 643); plan §Step 1 tests (line 129) [B38, X489, X658, T41]. *Verified by:* `station-isolation.test.ts` (clients and stations only; every other station-owned table is S1-TESTS-13, and MVP definition-of-done step 9 is P8-MVP-DONE-04, both open). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-isolation.test.ts :: row-level security between stations > a station sees only its own clients and itself.
- [x] **S1-ISOLATION-02** With station A's context, an update or delete of one of station B's clients affects zero rows. *Source:* plan §Phases row 1 Verify (line 643) [X490, T44]. *Verified by:* `station-isolation.test.ts` (clients only; the other station-owned tables are S1-TESTS-13). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-isolation.test.ts :: row-level security between stations > a station cannot update or delete another station's rows.
- [x] **S1-ISOLATION-03** With station A's context, the API role cannot insert a client carrying station B's id (row-level-security violation). *Source:* plan §Phases row 1 Verify (line 643); plan §Step 1 tests (line 129) [X491, T43]. *Verified by:* `station-isolation.test.ts` (clients only; the other station-owned tables are S1-TESTS-13). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-isolation.test.ts :: row-level security between stations > a station cannot insert rows into another station.
- [x] **S1-ISOLATION-04** Every station-owned table has row-level security enabled, enforced by PostgreSQL itself (not only by application code), with station-isolation policies. *Source:* baseline §1 row 3; blueprint §3 line 43 (kept); plan G2 [X217, B95]. *Verified by:* `station-isolation.test.ts` with direct SQL; a catalogue query finds no station-owned table with row-level security off. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > row-level security > S1-ISOLATION-04: row-level security is on for every table except the listed global ones, and every table with a station has a station policy.
- [x] **S1-ISOLATION-05** Each station-staff policy uses both `USING` and `WITH CHECK` set to `station_id = app.current_station_id()`, so an update that would move a row to another station fails (supersedes blueprint §6 "ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY" with no policies). *Source:* plan §Roles and RLS (line 386); plan §Step 1 Migration (line 121); blueprint §3.1 line 63 and §6 line 151 [D582, B144]. *Verified by:* isolation test: changing a row's `station_id` to station B fails the `WITH CHECK`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > row-level security (lookup_api) > S1-ISOLATION-05: an update cannot move station A's row to station B (WITH CHECK) ; apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > row-level security (lookup_api) > S1-TESTS-13: station A's context cannot insert rows carrying station B's id.
- [x] **S1-ISOLATION-06** The function `app.current_station_id()` (plan name `app.station()`) returns the setting `app.current_station_id` (plan name `app.station_id`) as a UUID, is `STABLE`, and returns NULL when the setting is unset or empty. *Source:* plan §Roles and RLS (lines 382–383) [D579]. *Verified by:* database test: the set value inside a transaction, NULL in a fresh one. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > row-level security (lookup_api) > S1-ISOLATION-06: app.current_station_id() is STABLE, returns the setting in its transaction and NULL without one.
- [x] **S1-ISOLATION-07** With no station context set, station-owned rows are invisible: NULL context means deny, never "all stations". *Source:* plan §Roles and RLS (line 383); baseline §1 row 3 [D580, X219, B92, T40]. *Verified by:* `station-isolation.test.ts`: a query with no context returns zero rows. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-isolation.test.ts :: row-level security between stations > with no station context, station data is invisible (fails closed).
- [x] **S1-ISOLATION-08** With no station context set, inserts into station-owned tables fail. *Source:* plan §Roles and RLS (line 383) [D580]. *Verified by:* isolation test: an insert with no context is refused. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > row-level security (lookup_api) > S1-ISOLATION-08: with no station context, inserts into station-owned tables are refused.
- [x] **S1-ISOLATION-09** `StationScopedTransaction` (plan name `TenantTx`) opens a transaction and sets the request's station and user context with `set_config(..., true)`, so the values last only for that transaction (supersedes blueprint §3 "Tenant Context Router" and "Tenant Isolation Middleware"). *Source:* plan §Roles and RLS, Context in NestJS (line 393); baseline §1 row 3; blueprint §3 lines 29 and 42 [D590, X218, B90, T51]. *Verified by:* integration test: the setting is present inside the helper and empty on the same connection after commit. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > StationScopedTransaction on pooled connections > S1-ISOLATION-09, S1-ISOLATION-10: the station context lasts only for its transaction; the next request on the same connection sees nothing.
- [x] **S1-ISOLATION-10** Because the context is transaction-local, a pooled connection never carries one request's station into another, and the rule holds behind a transaction-mode connection pooler. *Source:* plan §Roles and RLS, Context in NestJS (line 393); conventions §Scaling rules [D591, B91]. *Verified by:* integration test with a pool of one connection: a request for station A followed by a request with no context reads zero rows; two interleaved transactions never see each other's station. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > StationScopedTransaction on pooled connections > S1-ISOLATION-09, S1-ISOLATION-10: the station context lasts only for its transaction; the next request on the same connection sees nothing.
- [x] **S1-ISOLATION-11** Every reference between station-owned tables is a composite foreign key that includes `station_id` (`(id, station_id)`), because foreign-key checks bypass row-level security. *Source:* plan §Verified facts, Postgres (line 262); plan G2 (line 284); baseline §1 row 4 [D184, X220, B143, T46]. *Verified by:* isolation tests per reference: ad → client, campaign → ad, campaign → card, window → campaign, client → default card. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > composite keys (enforced even where row-level security is bypassed) > S1-ISOLATION-11: station A's <…> cannot reference station B's row ; apps/api/test/station-isolation.test.ts :: row-level security between stations > composite keys stop a station's action card from pointing at another station's client.
- [x] **S1-ISOLATION-12** An action card cannot point at another station's client. *Source:* plan §Data model, row action_cards (line 361) [D423, T45]. *Verified by:* `station-isolation.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-isolation.test.ts :: row-level security between stations > composite keys stop a station's action card from pointing at another station's client.
- [x] **S1-ISOLATION-13** The `stations` table has explicit policies: with a station context, a station reads only its own station row (supersedes blueprint §6 line 151, row-level security with zero policies). *Source:* blueprint §6 line 151; plan G2 [B270, T42]. *Verified by:* `station-isolation.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-isolation.test.ts :: row-level security between stations > a station sees only its own clients and itself.
- [x] **S1-ISOLATION-14** A signed-in user without a station context sees the stations they are a member of (to choose one after sign-in); memberships are visible to their station and to the member (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §2 [T50]. *Verified by:* database test with a user context and no station context. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > row-level security (lookup_api) > S1-ISOLATION-14: a signed-in user with no station chosen sees only their own stations and memberships ; apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > row-level security (lookup_api) > S1-TESTS-13, S1-ISOLATION-16: station A's context sees its own station_memberships rows and none of station B's.
- [x] **S1-ISOLATION-15** Every station-owned table carries a mandatory `station_id` column (named for the station, not `tenant_id`), including the tables the blueprint left without one: `clients`, `action_cards`, `ads`, `campaigns` and `campaign_time_windows` (supersedes blueprint §3.1 "mandatory tenant_id column"). *Source:* blueprint §3.1 line 61 and §6 lines 177–195; plan G2; conventions "Domain words" [B141]. *Verified by:* database test: each table has `station_id NOT NULL`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > row-level security > S1-ISOLATION-15: every station-owned table has a mandatory station_id.
- [x] **S1-ISOLATION-16** A station can read only the recognition events resolved to it; an event's station may be empty (unresolved or no match) (replaces blueprint §6 `tenant_id … ON DELETE SET NULL`). *Source:* plan §Data model, row recognition_events (line 370); plan §Roles and RLS; blueprint §6 line 212 [D525, B330, T49]. *Verified by:* row-level-security test: station A's context returns only rows whose `station_id` is A. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > row-level security (lookup_api) > S1-TESTS-13, S1-ISOLATION-16: station A's context sees its own recognition_events rows and none of station B's ; apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > row-level security (lookup_api) > S1-ISOLATION-16: unresolved recognition events (no station) are visible to no station.

### Table and column privileges

- [x] **S1-PRIVILEGES-01** The API role cannot read `portal_users.password_hash`, not even its own. *Source:* plan §Data model, row portal_users (line 356); baseline §1 row 5; blueprint §6 line 158 [D399, X221, B279, B149, T53]. *Verified by:* `database-privileges.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts :: database privileges of the API role > portal users > cannot read password hashes, even its own.
- [x] **S1-PRIVILEGES-02** The API role cannot set anyone's `platform_role` (no promotion to super admin). *Source:* baseline §1 row 5; plan G7 [X222, B105, B149, T55]. *Verified by:* `database-privileges.test.ts`: `UPDATE … SET platform_role` is denied. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts :: database privileges of the API role > portal users > cannot promote anyone to super admin or re-enable an account.
- [x] **S1-PRIVILEGES-03** The API role cannot change a portal user's account status (cannot re-enable a disabled account). *Source:* baseline §1 row 5 [X223, B149, T56]. *Verified by:* `database-privileges.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts :: database privileges of the API role > portal users > cannot promote anyone to super admin or re-enable an account.
- [x] **S1-PRIVILEGES-04** The API role cannot change a station's approval status (for example, lift a suspension); only the super-admin role can. *Source:* baseline §1 row 5 [X224, B267, B149, T61]. *Verified by:* `database-privileges.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts :: database privileges of the API role > stations > a station cannot change its own approval status (for example, lift a suspension).
- [x] **S1-PRIVILEGES-05** The API role cannot link an ad to an audio asset, neither on insert nor on update; only ingestion sets `ads.audio_asset_id`. *Source:* plan §Data model, row ads (line 363); baseline §1 row 5 [D453, X225, B292, B149, T65]. *Verified by:* `database-privileges.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts :: database privileges of the API role > ads and action cards > cannot point an ad at an audio asset (that would hijack another station's audio).
- [x] **S1-PRIVILEGES-06** Action cards are immutable versions: the API role can insert and read them but can neither update nor delete one. *Source:* plan §Data model, row action_cards (line 361); baseline §1 row 6 [D424, X227, B308, T67]. *Verified by:* `database-privileges.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts :: database privileges of the API role > ads and action cards > action cards are immutable versions: no update, no delete.
- [x] **S1-PRIVILEGES-07** The audit trail is append-only for the API role: insert allowed, update and delete refused. *Source:* baseline §1 row 6 [X226, B334, T68]. *Verified by:* `database-privileges.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts :: database privileges of the API role > audit trail > is append-only: insert yes, update and delete no.
- [x] **S1-PRIVILEGES-08** The audit trail is append-only for every role that can log in (`lookup_admin_api` and `lookup_fingerprint_worker` included): insert and read at most, never update or delete. *Source:* baseline §1; plan §Data model, row audit_events (line 374) [D567]. *Verified by:* database test per login role. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > what each role may do > S1-PRIVILEGES-08: the audit trail is append-only for every login role.
- [x] **S1-PRIVILEGES-09** The API role has no direct access to refresh tokens: as `lookup_api`, a select on `app.refresh_tokens` is refused with "permission denied". The table-level rule (row-level security on with no policy, and no direct access for any login role) is S1-PRIVILEGES-26. *Source:* baseline §1 row 7; plan §Step 1 Migration (line 119); plan §Verified facts, Postgres (line 261) [X228, D626, D182, T60, T71]. *Verified by:* `database-privileges.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts :: database privileges of the API role > portal users > has no direct access to refresh tokens.
- [x] **S1-PRIVILEGES-10** Recognition idempotency keys have row-level security switched on with no policy: the API role reads zero rows and cannot insert, and only narrow SECURITY DEFINER functions will touch the table. *Source:* baseline §1 row 7; plan §Verified facts, Postgres (line 261) [X230, D182, B320, T71]. *Verified by:* database test as `lookup_api`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > what each role may do > S1-PRIVILEGES-10: the API role can neither read nor write recognition idempotency keys directly ; apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > row-level security > S1-ISOLATION-04: row-level security is on for every table except the listed global ones, and every table with a station has a station policy.
- [x] **S1-PRIVILEGES-11** The API role has no grant of any kind on the `fingerprints` schema or any table in it (`audio_assets`, `hashes_version_1`): SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE are all denied (supersedes blueprint §3, "row-level security hides fingerprints across tenants"). *Source:* plan §Roles and RLS (line 389); plan G3 (line 285); plan §Step 1 tests (line 130); plan §Phases row 1 Verify (line 643) [D586, D222, D638, X494, B148]. *Verified by:* database catalogue test (no privilege rows for `lookup_api`) and a denial test on both tables. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > row-level security > S1-PRIVILEGES-11: the API roles hold no privilege of any kind on the fingerprints schema or its tables ; apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > what each role may do > S1-PRIVILEGES-11: the API role is refused on both fingerprint tables.
- [x] **S1-PRIVILEGES-12** The API role cannot read `fingerprints.audio_assets`. *Source:* plan §Data model, row fp.audio_assets (line 362) [D442, T47]. *Verified by:* `station-isolation.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-isolation.test.ts :: row-level security between stations > the API role has no access to the fingerprint store.
- [x] **S1-PRIVILEGES-13** The API role cannot delete recognition events directly: as `lookup_api` with a station context, `DELETE FROM app.recognition_events` is refused with "permission denied". The wider rule that analytics tables are read-only for the API role is S1-PRIVILEGES-25 (insert and update), S3-RECORDING-01 (writes only through a SECURITY DEFINER function), P4-DATABASE-02 and P7-ROLLUPS-08 (the later analytics tables). *Source:* plan §Roles and RLS (line 388); baseline §1 [D584, B331, T48]. *Verified by:* `station-isolation.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-isolation.test.ts :: row-level security between stations > the API role cannot write recognition events directly.
- [x] **S1-PRIVILEGES-14** Partitions of partitioned tables are reachable only through the parent table and its policies. *Source:* baseline §1 row 10 [X239, B319, T96]. *Verified by:* `database-scaling.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-scaling.test.ts :: recognition event partitions > the API role cannot reach a partition directly, around the parent table and its policies.
- [x] **S1-PRIVILEGES-15** With a station context the API role sees the signed-in user and that station's team; without one it sees only the user; never another station's users (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §2 [T54]. *Verified by:* `database-privileges.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts :: database privileges of the API role > portal users > sees itself and its station's team, never other stations' users.
- [x] **S1-PRIVILEGES-16** The API role cannot write any password hash, not even its own; only the sign-in functions can (status-built.md only; design S2-D4). *Source:* status-built.md §2 [T57]. *Verified by:* `database-privileges.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts :: database privileges of the API role > portal users > cannot write any password hash, even its own (only the sign-in functions can).
- [x] **S1-PRIVILEGES-17** A user can change their own full name; changing a teammate's name affects zero rows (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §2 [T58]. *Verified by:* `database-privileges.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts :: database privileges of the API role > portal users > can edit its own name, never a teammate's.
- [x] **S1-PRIVILEGES-18** The API role cannot create or delete portal users directly (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §2 [T59]. *Verified by:* `database-privileges.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts :: database privileges of the API role > portal users > cannot create or delete users directly.
- [x] **S1-PRIVILEGES-19** A station edits only its own profile; editing another station's profile affects zero rows. *Source:* blueprint §6 line 151 (explicit policies replace "RLS with no policies"); plan G2 [B270, T62]. *Verified by:* `database-privileges.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts :: database privileges of the API role > stations > can edit its own profile, never another station's.
- [x] **S1-PRIVILEGES-20** The station profile columns the API role may edit are only province, city, time zone, logo and "listed in app" (never name or frequency) (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §2 [T63]. *Verified by:* privilege test per column. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > what each role may do > S1-PRIVILEGES-20: the API role may edit only province, city, time zone, logo and listing of its station.
- [x] **S1-PRIVILEGES-21** The API role cannot create or delete stations directly (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §2 [T64]. *Verified by:* `database-privileges.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts :: database privileges of the API role > stations > cannot create or delete stations directly.
- [x] **S1-PRIVILEGES-22** The API role can create ads for its own station (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §2 [T66]. *Verified by:* `database-privileges.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts :: database privileges of the API role > ads and action cards > can create ads for its own station.
- [ ] **S1-PRIVILEGES-23** `lookup_admin_api` cannot read password hashes; on portal users, ads, campaigns and listener installs it may change only the status; it may create and update stations and manage memberships; it may only read clients, action cards and recognition events (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §2 [T69]. *Verified by:* privilege tests as `lookup_admin_api`. *(built, not yet tested: `station-isolation-matrix.test.ts` (the S1-PRIVILEGES-23 test) proves every part except that the role cannot insert clients, action cards or recognition events)*
- [ ] **S1-PRIVILEGES-24** `lookup_fingerprint_worker` has table privileges only in the `fingerprints` schema (read, insert and delete hashes; read and insert audio assets) and cannot read app tables. *Source:* baseline §8 row 3 (Built: grants) [X352, T70]. *Verified by:* `database-privileges.test.ts` as the worker role. *(built, not yet tested: `station-isolation-matrix.test.ts` (the S1-PRIVILEGES-24 test) proves reading both fingerprint tables, refused update and delete of audio assets and no access to app tables; inserting and deleting hashes and inserting audio assets are not exercised)*
- [ ] **S1-PRIVILEGES-25** The API role cannot insert or update recognition events directly: as `lookup_api` with a station context, an `INSERT` and an `UPDATE` on `app.recognition_events` are each refused with "permission denied" (analytics are read-only for the API role). *Source:* plan §Roles and RLS "Analytics : read-only for app_api; writes only via SECURITY DEFINER recog.* functions" (line 388); baseline §1 [D584, T48]. *Verified by:* `database-privileges.test.ts`: one insert case and one update case, each expecting "permission denied". *(built, not yet tested)* *Built in:* Foundation migration (only `SELECT` on `app.recognition_events` is granted to `lookup_api`). Split from S1-PRIVILEGES-13 in review round 1.
- [ ] **S1-PRIVILEGES-26** `app.refresh_tokens` has row-level security switched on with no policy, and no login role (`lookup_api`, `lookup_admin_api`, `lookup_fingerprint_worker`) reads or writes it directly; only the sign-in SECURITY DEFINER functions reach it. *Source:* baseline §1 row 7; plan §Step 1 Migration (line 119); plan §Verified facts, Postgres (line 261) [X228, D626, D182]. *Verified by:* catalogue test: `relrowsecurity` is true for `app.refresh_tokens` and `pg_policies` holds no row for it; a denial test for each of the three login roles. *(built, not yet tested: row-level security on is proven by `database-catalogue.test.ts` (the S1-ISOLATION-04 test) and `lookup_api`'s denial by `database-privileges.test.ts` and `portal-sessions-database.test.ts`; no test checks that no policy exists or denies `lookup_admin_api` and `lookup_fingerprint_worker`)* *Built in:* Foundation migration (`ALTER TABLE app.refresh_tokens ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY`, no policy, no grant). Split from S1-PRIVILEGES-09 in review round 1.

### Database schema: general, stations, users, clients and action cards

- [x] **S1-SCHEMA-01** Station data, ads, action cards, recognition events and fingerprint hashes all live in PostgreSQL (engagement events join them in P4 and daily statistics in P7). *Source:* blueprint §2 line 19 (kept); plan §Data model [B69]. *Verified by:* database test lists the expected tables in schemas `app`, `fingerprints` and `recognition`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > names > S1-NAMING-05, S1-MIGRATIONS-10: the slice tables exist under their domain names; the blueprint names do not.
- [ ] **S1-SCHEMA-02** Every timestamp column is `timestamptz`, NOT NULL wherever a value is always known. *Source:* blueprint §6 lines 146–147; plan §Data model intro (line 351) [B263]. *Verified by:* database catalogue test: no `timestamp without time zone`; `created_at` and `updated_at` are NOT NULL. *(built, not yet tested: every timestamp column in the migrations is `timestamptz`; no catalogue test)*
- [ ] **S1-SCHEMA-03** A shared trigger keeps `updated_at` current on update for stations, portal users, clients, ads and campaigns. *Source:* blueprint §6 lines 146–147; plan §Data model intro (line 351) [B263, T91]. *Verified by:* database test: updating each kind of row moves `updated_at` forward without the caller setting it. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [x] **S1-SCHEMA-04** `stations` is the tenant table, one row per radio station; there is no separate tenant or business table (supersedes blueprint §6 `tenants`). *Source:* plan §Data model, row stations (line 355) [D373, T75]. *Verified by:* schema test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > names > S1-NAMING-05, S1-MIGRATIONS-10: the slice tables exist under their domain names; the blueprint names do not.
- [ ] **S1-SCHEMA-05** `stations.name` is required (the blueprint's `company_name`). *Source:* plan §Data model, row stations (line 355); blueprint §6 line 144 [D374, B264, T75]. *Verified by:* database test: an insert without a name fails. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-SCHEMA-06** `stations.frequency_label` is required and holds the frequency as shown to people, for example "89.5 FM". *Source:* plan §Data model, row stations (line 355) [D375, B272, T75]. *Verified by:* schema test: column exists and is NOT NULL. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-SCHEMA-07** `stations` has a `province` column. *Source:* plan §Data model, row stations (line 355) [D376, B272, T75]. *Verified by:* schema test. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-SCHEMA-08** `stations` has a `city` column. *Source:* plan §Data model, row stations (line 355) [D377, B272, T75]. *Verified by:* schema test. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [x] **S1-SCHEMA-09** G6: each station has a mandatory time zone, `stations.time_zone` (plan name `timezone`), defaulting to `Africa/Lusaka`. *Source:* plan G6 (line 288); plan §Data model, row stations (line 355) [D230, D379, B210, T75]. *Verified by:* database test: a station inserted without a time zone gets `Africa/Lusaka`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > who can reach a station > a member reads the station profile, with their role (the test station is inserted without a time zone).
- [ ] **S1-SCHEMA-10** `stations.logo_object_key` (plan name `logo_key`) holds the storage key of the logo, not a URL. *Source:* plan §Data model, row stations (line 355) [D380, B272, T75]. *Verified by:* schema test. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-SCHEMA-11** `stations.is_listed_in_app` (plan name `listed_in_app`) is a NOT NULL boolean, default true. *Source:* plan §Data model, row stations (line 355) [D394, B272, T75]. *Verified by:* schema test. *(built, not yet tested: the default true is shown by `stations-and-clients.test.ts` :: who can reach a station > a member reads the station profile, with their role (station inserted without the column); NOT NULL is not tested)*
- [ ] **S1-SCHEMA-12** `stations.status` is one of `PENDING_VERIFICATION`, `PENDING_REVIEW`, `ACTIVE`, `REJECTED` or `SUSPENDED`, and nothing else (supersedes blueprint §6 `account_status` 'ACTIVE', 'SUSPENDED', 'PENDING'). *Source:* plan §Data model, row stations (line 355); blueprint §6 line 145 [D389, B265, T75]. *Verified by:* database test of `stations_status_check`. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-SCHEMA-13** D6: a new station never starts as `ACTIVE`: the default status is `PENDING_VERIFICATION` (supersedes blueprint §6 `DEFAULT 'ACTIVE'`). *Source:* plan D6 (line 236); plan §Data model, row stations (line 355); blueprint §6 line 145 [D139, B266, T75]. *Verified by:* database test: a station inserted without a status is `PENDING_VERIFICATION`. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-SCHEMA-14** Deleting a station that still has dependent rows is refused (`ON DELETE RESTRICT`); nothing cascades from a station, so analytics are never deleted with it (supersedes blueprint §6 `ON DELETE CASCADE`). *Source:* blueprint §6 lines 156, 166, 179, 191, 201; plan §Data model intro (line 351) [B271]. *Verified by:* database test: deleting a station that has a client fails on the foreign key. *(built, not yet tested: the station foreign keys in the Foundation migration are `ON DELETE RESTRICT`)*
- [ ] **S1-SCHEMA-15** G7: `portal_users` has no station column; a user is linked to stations only through `station_memberships (station_id, portal_user_id, role)` (supersedes blueprint §6 `tenant_id UUID NOT NULL` on `portal_users`). *Source:* plan G7 (line 289); blueprint §6 line 156 [D235, B277]. *Verified by:* database test of both tables' columns. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-SCHEMA-16** `portal_users.email` is `citext` and unique, so two addresses that differ only in letter case are the same account (supersedes blueprint §6 `email VARCHAR(255) UNIQUE`). *Source:* plan §Data model, row portal_users (line 356); blueprint §6 line 157 [D398, B278, T77]. *Verified by:* database test: inserting `A@x.zm` after `a@x.zm` fails on the unique constraint. *(built, not yet tested: matching without regard to case is shown by `portal-sessions-database.test.ts` :: signing in > the right hash opens a session with a refresh token, resets the counter and is audited, but no test inserts a second address differing only in case)*
- [ ] **S1-SCHEMA-17** `portal_users.phone_number_e164` (plan name `phone_e164`) is optional and accepts only E.164 numbers. *Source:* plan §Data model, row portal_users (line 356) [D401, B281, T77]. *Verified by:* database test of `portal_users_phone_number_check`. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-SCHEMA-18** `portal_users.platform_role` is NULL for station users and client users and otherwise only `SUPER_ADMIN` or `SUPPORT` (supersedes blueprint §6, where `role='ADMIN'` still needed a `tenant_id`). *Source:* plan G7 (line 289); plan §Data model, row portal_users (line 356) [D235, D402, B105, T77]. *Verified by:* schema test: the column is nullable and its check accepts only those two values. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-SCHEMA-19** A portal user's account status is `ACTIVE` or `DISABLED`, and every user has a full name (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §3 [T77]. *Verified by:* schema test of the status check. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-SCHEMA-20** `station_memberships` is keyed by (station, user): one membership per person per station. *Source:* plan §Data model, row station_memberships (line 357) [D404, B277, T78]. *Verified by:* database test: a second membership for the same pair fails on the primary key. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-SCHEMA-21** Station staff roles are exactly `OWNER`, `MANAGER` and `ANALYST`, held per station in `station_memberships.role` (supersedes blueprint §6 roles `STATION_MANAGER` and `ADVERTISER` on the user row). *Source:* plan G7 (line 289); plan §Data model, row station_memberships (line 357); blueprint §3.1 line 55 and §6 line 159 [D236, D405, B120, T78]. *Verified by:* database test of `station_memberships_role_check`. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-SCHEMA-22** `clients` rows belong to a station: `station_id` is NOT NULL with a RESTRICT foreign key to `stations`. *Source:* plan §Data model, row clients (line 360) [D416, B287, T79]. *Verified by:* schema test. *(built, not yet tested: NOT NULL is proven by `database-catalogue.test.ts` (the S1-ISOLATION-15 test); the RESTRICT foreign key is not tested)*
- [ ] **S1-SCHEMA-23** `clients.name` is required. *Source:* plan §Data model, row clients (line 360) [D417, B287, T79]. *Verified by:* schema test. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [x] **S1-SCHEMA-24** Client names are unique within a station (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §3 [T79]. *Verified by:* database test: the same name twice at one station fails; at two stations it succeeds. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > clients > a duplicate name is refused with a plain message ; apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > clients > two stations may each have a client with the same name.
- [ ] **S1-SCHEMA-25** `clients.logo_object_key` (plan name `logo_key`) holds the client's logo storage key. *Source:* plan §Data model, row clients (line 360) [D418, B287]. *Verified by:* schema test. *(built, not yet tested)*
