- [x] **S1-SCHEMA-26** `clients.default_action_card_id` (plan name `default_card_id`) can only point at an action card of the same station (composite foreign key). *Source:* plan §Data model, row clients (line 360); plan G5 [D420, B222, B287, T79, T46]. *Verified by:* database test: setting it to another station's card fails. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > composite keys (enforced even where row-level security is bypassed) > S1-ISOLATION-11: station A's client → default card cannot reference station B's row.
- [ ] **S1-SCHEMA-27** `clients` has `UNIQUE (id, station_id)`, the target of composite foreign keys from ads and action cards. *Source:* plan §Data model, row clients (line 360) [D421, B287]. *Verified by:* database catalogue test: the constraint `clients_id_station_unique` exists. *(built, not yet tested: the composite foreign keys that target it are proven by `station-isolation-matrix.test.ts` (the S1-ISOLATION-11 tests), but no test names `clients_id_station_unique`)*
- [x] **S1-SCHEMA-28** `action_cards` rows carry a NOT NULL `station_id`, and a station sees only its own cards. *Source:* plan §Data model, row action_cards (line 361); blueprint §6 lines 189–195 [D422, B307]. *Verified by:* schema test; row-level-security test on `action_cards`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > row-level security > S1-ISOLATION-15: every station-owned table has a mandatory station_id ; apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > row-level security (lookup_api) > S1-TESTS-13, S1-ISOLATION-16: station A's context sees its own action_cards rows and none of station B's.
- [ ] **S1-SCHEMA-29** `action_cards` has a NOT NULL `schema_version` and records its author. *Source:* plan §Data model, row action_cards (line 361); status-built.md §3 (author) [D426, B307, T80]. *Verified by:* schema test. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-SCHEMA-30** `action_cards.content` (plan name `card`) stores the card as `jsonb`, NOT NULL, and must be a JSON object. *Source:* plan §Data model, row action_cards (line 361) [D427, B307, T80]. *Verified by:* schema test: a JSON array or string is refused. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-SCHEMA-31** A stored action card must be smaller than 16 kilobytes. *Source:* plan §Data model, row action_cards (line 361) [D429, B256, T80]. *Verified by:* database test of `action_cards_content_check`: a card of 16 KB or more fails. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-SCHEMA-32** A card is attached through the campaign (`campaigns.action_card_id`) or as a client's default, never directly to the ad: `action_cards` has no ad column (supersedes blueprint §6 `actionable_payloads.media_item_id`). *Source:* blueprint §6 line 191; plan §Data model, rows campaigns and clients [B309]. *Verified by:* database test of the column list. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-SCHEMA-33** Every action card belongs to one client of the same station: `action_cards.client_id` is `uuid NOT NULL` and references `clients (id, station_id)` through the composite foreign key `action_cards_client_foreign_key`; a client viewer's card access (P5C) depends on this column. *Source:* plan §Data model, row action_cards "`station_id`, `client_id`" (line 361). *Verified by:* schema test: the column is NOT NULL and the composite foreign key exists; database test: inserting a card without a client fails. *(built, not yet tested)* *Built in:* Foundation migration (`action_cards.client_id uuid NOT NULL`, constraint `action_cards_client_foreign_key`); the cross-station half of the key is tested under S1-ISOLATION-12.

### Database schema: fingerprint store

- [ ] **S1-FINGERPRINT-STORE-01** G8: ad audio is stored as global, de-duplicated `fingerprints.audio_assets`, and each station-owned ad points at one asset through `ads.audio_asset_id` (supersedes blueprint §6 `media_items`). *Source:* plan G8 (line 290) [D238]. *Verified by:* schema test: `ads.audio_asset_id` references `audio_assets`. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-FINGERPRINT-STORE-02** G3: fingerprints and audio assets are global: no table in the `fingerprints` schema has a station or client column (supersedes blueprint §3 "row-level security hides fingerprints across tenants"). *Source:* plan G3 (line 285); plan §Data model, row fp.audio_assets (line 362) [D221, D441]. *Verified by:* schema test: no column named `station_id` or `client_id` in the schema. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-FINGERPRINT-STORE-03** `audio_assets.asset_number` (plan name `fp_key`) is a compact integer identity key (`integer GENERATED ALWAYS AS IDENTITY`), unique. *Source:* plan §Data model, row fp.audio_assets (line 362); plan G13 [D434, B315, T92]. *Verified by:* schema test. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-FINGERPRINT-STORE-04** `audio_assets.content_sha256` (plan name `sha256`) is unique, so a second asset with the same content is refused. *Source:* plan §Data model, row fp.audio_assets (line 362); plan G8 [D435, D238, B315, T92]. *Verified by:* database test. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-FINGERPRINT-STORE-05** `audio_assets.duration_milliseconds` (plan name `duration_ms`) is NOT NULL and greater than zero. *Source:* plan §Data model, row fp.audio_assets (line 362) [D436, B315, T92]. *Verified by:* schema test. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-FINGERPRINT-STORE-06** `audio_assets.fingerprint_version` (plan name `fp_version`) records the algorithm version used. *Source:* plan §Data model, row fp.audio_assets (line 362) [D439, B315, T92]. *Verified by:* schema test (S3 ingest test: it equals the engine's current version). *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-FINGERPRINT-STORE-07** `audio_assets.hash_count` records how many hashes the asset produced. *Source:* plan §Data model, row fp.audio_assets (line 362) [D440, B315, T92]. *Verified by:* schema test (S3 ingest test: it equals the asset's rows in the hash table). *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-FINGERPRINT-STORE-08** The hash table stores each hash as `bigint` (supersedes blueprint §6 `hash BYTEA`). *Source:* plan §Data model, row fp.fingerprints_v1 (line 367) [D486, T92]. *Verified by:* schema test: column `hash` is `bigint` NOT NULL. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-FINGERPRINT-STORE-09** G13: each hash row references its audio asset by the small integer `audio_asset_number` (plan name `asset_key`) referencing `audio_assets.asset_number`, not by a UUID (supersedes blueprint §6 `media_item_id UUID`). *Source:* plan G13 (line 295); plan §Data model, row fp.fingerprints_v1 (line 367) [D262, D488, B312, T92]. *Verified by:* schema test: `integer` NOT NULL with that reference. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-FINGERPRINT-STORE-10** G1: each hash row holds its time offset as the integer `time_offset_frames` (plan name `t_off`), never a column named `offset` (supersedes blueprint §6 `fingerprints.offset`). *Source:* plan G1 (line 283); plan §Data model, row fp.fingerprints_v1 (line 367) [D215, D489, B311, T92]. *Verified by:* migration test: the table is created without error and has `time_offset_frames integer NOT NULL`. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-FINGERPRINT-STORE-11** The hash table has exactly three columns: no per-row id and no `created_at` (supersedes blueprint §6 `id BIGSERIAL` and `created_at`). *Source:* plan §Data model, row fp.fingerprints_v1 (line 367); blueprint §6 lines 198–204 [D491, B311, T92]. *Verified by:* schema test of the column list. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-FINGERPRINT-STORE-12** The hash table has one covering b-tree index on `(hash) INCLUDE (audio_asset_number, time_offset_frames)`, so a lookup is answered from the index alone (supersedes blueprint §6 `USING hash(hash)`). *Source:* plan §Data model, row fp.fingerprints_v1 (line 367); blueprint §6 line 207 [D490, B313, T92]. *Verified by:* database test reads the index definition; `EXPLAIN` of the lookup shows an index-only scan. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [x] **S1-FINGERPRINT-STORE-13** G13: there is one hash table per algorithm version, with the version in its name (`hashes_version_1`; plan name `fp.fingerprints_v1`), so re-indexing builds a new table beside the live one without downtime. *Source:* plan G13 (line 295); plan §Standing requirements; conventions §Scaling rules [D263, B314, T92]. *Verified by:* schema test of the table name (re-index tooling is tested in P8). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > names > S1-NAMING-05, S1-MIGRATIONS-10: the slice tables exist under their domain names; the blueprint names do not.

### Database schema: ads, campaigns and time windows

- [ ] **S1-ADS-CAMPAIGNS-01** `ads` rows are station-owned (`station_id` NOT NULL) and protected by row-level security. *Source:* plan §Data model, row ads (line 363); blueprint §6 lines 164–171 [D446, B285]. *Verified by:* isolation test: station A's context sees none of station B's ads and cannot insert, update or delete them. *(built, not yet tested: reading and inserting are proven by `station-isolation-matrix.test.ts` (the S1-TESTS-13 tests, `ads`); no test updates or deletes another station's ad)*
- [ ] **S1-ADS-CAMPAIGNS-02** G5: the brand is a row in `clients` (the station's advertiser), referenced by a mandatory `ads.client_id` through a composite foreign key, so an ad can never point at another station's client (supersedes blueprint §6 free-text `artist_or_brand`). *Source:* plan G5 (line 287); plan §Data model, row ads (line 363); blueprint §6 line 168 [D447, B286, T46]. *Verified by:* database test: an ad without a client, or with another station's client, is refused. *(built, not yet tested: another station's client is refused (`station-isolation-matrix.test.ts`, S1-ISOLATION-11 ad → client), but no test inserts an ad without a client)*
- [ ] **S1-ADS-CAMPAIGNS-03** `ads.title` is required. *Source:* plan §Data model, row ads (line 363) [D448, B285]. *Verified by:* schema test. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-ADS-CAMPAIGNS-04** `ads.upload_object_key` holds the storage key of the uploaded file. *Source:* plan §Data model, row ads (line 363) [D449, B291]. *Verified by:* schema test. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-ADS-CAMPAIGNS-05** `ads.upload_content_sha256` (plan name `upload_sha256`) holds the raw SHA-256 of the uploaded file. *Source:* plan §Data model, row ads (line 363) [D451, B291]. *Verified by:* schema test. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-ADS-CAMPAIGNS-06** An ad records its audio duration (new: the blueprint had no duration). *Source:* plan G8 [B291, T81]. *Verified by:* schema test. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-ADS-CAMPAIGNS-07** `ads.status` is one of `AWAITING_UPLOAD`, `PROCESSING`, `READY`, `NEEDS_REVIEW` or `FAILED`, starting at `AWAITING_UPLOAD` (new: the blueprint had no status). *Source:* plan §Data model, row ads (line 363); plan G8 [D454, B290, T81]. *Verified by:* database test of `ads_status_check` and of the default. *(built, not yet tested: the default is shown by `ads-and-uploads.test.ts` :: creating an ad and its upload URL > signs exactly Content-Type and Content-Length for 10 minutes, with no checksum parameters, and audits it; no test of `ads_status_check` refusing other values)*
- [x] **S1-ADS-CAMPAIGNS-08** `ads.archived_at` is a nullable `timestamptz`; archiving sets it instead of deleting the row. *Source:* plan §Data model, row ads (line 363) [D456, B291, T81]. *Verified by:* schema test (P5B archive test: the row remains and leaves live lists). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/ad-history-and-changes.test.ts :: ad history, rename and archive > removing an ad > removes a draft from every list and page, keeps it for the record, and audits it.
- [x] **S1-ADS-CAMPAIGNS-09** `ads.processing_error_code` (plan name `error_code`) holds a short code, never exception text. *Source:* plan §Data model, row ads (line 363); global rules §Two layers [D457, B291, T81]. *Verified by:* schema test: the column exists and is nullable text. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > completing an upload > a file that is not really audio is refused, deleted from storage, and the ad marked FAILED with the reason ; apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > completing an upload > an object that differs from what was declared (written around the signed URL) is refused.
- [ ] **S1-ADS-CAMPAIGNS-10** `ads` has no media-type column; version 1 handles advertisements only (supersedes blueprint §6 `media_type` 'ADVERTISEMENT', 'MUSIC', 'PROMOTION'). *Source:* blueprint §6 line 169; plan O8 [B289]. *Verified by:* database test of the column list. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-ADS-CAMPAIGNS-11** Ads are indexed by `(station_id, updated_at DESC)` (replaces blueprint §6 `idx_media_items_tenant`). *Source:* blueprint §6 line 174; conventions §Names in the database (`ads_station_updated_at_index`) [B299, T97]. *Verified by:* database test finds the index. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [x] **S1-ADS-CAMPAIGNS-12** `campaigns` rows carry `station_id` and are protected by row-level security (the blueprint's `campaign_schedules`). *Source:* plan §Data model, row campaigns (line 364); blueprint §6 lines 177–186 [D464, B301]. *Verified by:* isolation test: station A's context sees none of station B's campaigns. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > row-level security (lookup_api) > S1-TESTS-13, S1-ISOLATION-16: station A's context sees its own campaigns rows and none of station B's ; apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > row-level security > S1-ISOLATION-15: every station-owned table has a mandatory station_id.
- [x] **S1-ADS-CAMPAIGNS-13** `campaigns.ad_id` references an ad of the same station (composite foreign key). *Source:* plan §Data model, row campaigns (line 364) [D465, B301, T46]. *Verified by:* database test: a campaign pointing at another station's ad is refused. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > composite keys (enforced even where row-level security is bypassed) > S1-ISOLATION-11: station A's campaign → ad cannot reference station B's row.
- [x] **S1-ADS-CAMPAIGNS-14** `campaigns.action_card_id` (plan name `card_id`) references an action card of the same station. *Source:* plan §Data model, row campaigns (line 364) [D466, B301, T46]. *Verified by:* database test: a campaign pointing at another station's card is refused. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > composite keys (enforced even where row-level security is bypassed) > S1-ISOLATION-11: station A's campaign → card cannot reference station B's row.
- [ ] **S1-ADS-CAMPAIGNS-15** `campaigns.name` is required. *Source:* plan §Data model, row campaigns (line 364) [D467, B301]. *Verified by:* schema test. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-ADS-CAMPAIGNS-16** `campaigns.status` is one of `DRAFT`, `ACTIVE`, `PAUSED` or `ENDED`. *Source:* plan §Data model, row campaigns (line 364); blueprint §6 line 185 [D468, B303, T82]. *Verified by:* database test of `campaigns_status_check`. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-ADS-CAMPAIGNS-17** A new campaign starts as `DRAFT`, so nothing is live until it is published (supersedes blueprint §6 `is_active BOOLEAN DEFAULT TRUE`). *Source:* blueprint §6 line 185; plan §Data model, row campaigns (line 364) [B303]. *Verified by:* database test of the default. *(built, not yet tested: the Foundation migration defaults `campaigns.status` to DRAFT; the API inserts DRAFT explicitly (`campaigns.service.ts`), shown by `cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts` :: schedules > creates a draft campaign in the station time zone and replaces its windows when saved again, but the database default itself is not tested)*
- [ ] **S1-ADS-CAMPAIGNS-18** "Live now" and "Scheduled" are never stored as campaign statuses. *Source:* plan §Data model, row campaigns (line 364) [D474]. *Verified by:* schema test: the status check has only the four stored values. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-ADS-CAMPAIGNS-19** `campaigns.active_period` is a NOT NULL `tstzrange` holding the campaign's start and end, and cannot be empty (supersedes blueprint §6 `start_date` and `end_date`). *Source:* plan §Data model, row campaigns (line 364); blueprint §6 lines 180–181 [D469, B302, T82]. *Verified by:* database test of `campaigns_active_period_check`. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-ADS-CAMPAIGNS-20** `campaigns.engagement_limit` (plan name `engagement_cap`) is optional and must be above zero when set (NULL means no limit). *Source:* plan §Data model, row campaigns (line 364) [D470, B301, T82]. *Verified by:* schema test: zero and negative numbers are refused. *(built, not yet tested: NULL as no limit is shown by `cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts` :: schedules > treats a missing engagement limit as no limit, and the API input refuses zero (`packages/contracts/test/schedule-schemas.test.ts` :: schedule input > refuses a tap limit of zero); the database check is not tested)*
- [ ] **S1-ADS-CAMPAIGNS-21** `campaigns.time_window_grace_period` (plan name `window_grace`) is a NOT NULL `interval`, 10 minutes by default. *Source:* plan §Data model, row campaigns (line 364); plan O3 (line 658) [D472, B301, T82]. *Verified by:* schema test of the default. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-ADS-CAMPAIGNS-22** An ad has at most one live (`ACTIVE` or `PAUSED`) campaign for any moment: a GiST exclusion constraint refuses overlapping active periods for the same ad, while an overlapping `DRAFT` is allowed. *Source:* plan §Data model, row campaigns (line 364); plan §Step 1 Migration [D473, B304, T83]. *Verified by:* database test: a second overlapping live campaign fails on `campaigns_one_live_campaign_per_ad_exclusion`; a non-overlapping one succeeds; an overlapping draft is allowed. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [x] **S1-ADS-CAMPAIGNS-23** `campaign_time_windows` rows (plan name `campaign_windows`) reference their campaign and carry the same `station_id` (composite foreign key). *Source:* plan §Data model, row campaign_windows (line 365) [D476, T46]. *Verified by:* database test: a window whose station differs from its campaign's is refused. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > composite keys (enforced even where row-level security is bypassed) > S1-ISOLATION-11: station A's time window → campaign cannot reference station B's row.
- [x] **S1-ADS-CAMPAIGNS-24** Daily times live in `campaign_time_windows` (campaign, station, days of the week, local start time, local end time), and a campaign may have several windows (supersedes blueprint §6 `daily_start_time`, `daily_end_time` and `days_of_week` on the schedule row). *Source:* blueprint §6 lines 182–184; plan §Data model, row campaign_windows (line 365) [B305]. *Verified by:* database test: two windows for one campaign are stored (S3: both are honoured by the resolve function). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts :: buttons, schedules and publishing > schedules > creates a draft campaign in the station time zone and replaces its windows when saved again.
- [ ] **S1-ADS-CAMPAIGNS-25** G6: a window's `days_of_week` (plan name `iso_days`) is an array of 1 to 7 distinct ISO day numbers, 1 for Monday to 7 for Sunday; an empty array, a 0 or an 8 is refused (supersedes blueprint §6 `days_of_week INT[]` with no stated convention). *Source:* plan G6 (line 288); plan §Data model, row campaign_windows (line 365); blueprint §4 line 100 and §6 line 184 [D231, D477, B211, B306, T84]. *Verified by:* database test of `campaign_time_windows_days_of_week_check`. *(built, not yet tested: the API input refuses day 0 and a repeated day (`packages/contracts/test/schedule-schemas.test.ts` :: schedule input > refuses a day that is not 1 to 7 ; refuses a day listed twice); `campaign_time_windows_days_of_week_check` is not tested)*
- [ ] **S1-ADS-CAMPAIGNS-26** A window has `local_start_time` and `local_end_time` (plan names `local_start`, `local_end`), both NOT NULL `time` without date or zone, read in the station's time zone. *Source:* plan §Data model, row campaign_windows (line 365) [D478, B305, T84]. *Verified by:* schema test. *(built, not yet tested: reading in the station time zone is shown by `campaign-time-windows-database.test.ts` :: campaign status as a station sees it > an active campaign is live only inside a window, in the station time zone (Lusaka is UTC+2); the column types and NOT NULL are not tested)*
- [x] **S1-ADS-CAMPAIGNS-27** A window whose end is before its start is valid and means it runs past midnight (for example 22:00 to 02:00). *Source:* plan §Data model, row campaign_windows (line 365) [D479, T84]. *Verified by:* database test: a 22:00–02:00 window is accepted. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts :: buttons, schedules and publishing > schedules > creates a draft campaign in the station time zone and replaces its windows when saved again ; apps/api/test/campaign-time-windows-database.test.ts :: time windows > a window past midnight: still open early the next day (Saturday 01:59).
- [ ] **S1-ADS-CAMPAIGNS-28** A window's start and end times must differ (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §3 [T84]. *Verified by:* database test: an 08:00–08:00 window is refused. *(built, not yet tested: the API input refuses it (`packages/contracts/test/schedule-schemas.test.ts` :: schedule input > refuses a window that starts and ends at the same time); the database check is not tested)*

### Database schema: listener installs and recognition events

- [x] **S1-RECOGNITION-EVENTS-01** `listener_installs.install_id_hash` (plan name `install_hash`) is unique: a second install row with the same hash is refused. *Source:* plan §Data model, row installs (line 369) [D497, T85]. *Verified by:* database test: a duplicate hash is refused (today proved indirectly: the leak test provokes this unique violation and finds "duplicate key" in the private log line; a dedicated test of the constraint `listener_installs_install_id_hash_unique` would prove it directly). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > database errors become a generic 500, with the real error and stack in the log.
- [ ] **S1-RECOGNITION-EVENTS-02** A listener install is identified in the database only by `install_id_hash`: `listener_installs` has no column that holds the raw install UUID. *Source:* plan §Data model, row installs (line 369) [D497]. *Verified by:* schema test of the column list of `listener_installs`. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-RECOGNITION-EVENTS-03** `listener_installs.platform` is NOT NULL and one of `ANDROID` or `IOS`. *Source:* plan §Data model, row installs (line 369) [D499, T86]. *Verified by:* schema test. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-RECOGNITION-EVENTS-04** `listener_installs.app_version` is NOT NULL. *Source:* plan §Data model, row installs (line 369) [D500]. *Verified by:* schema test (S3: the version sent at registration is stored). *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-RECOGNITION-EVENTS-05** `listener_installs.status` is NOT NULL and one of `ACTIVE` or `BLOCKED`. *Source:* plan §Data model, row installs (line 369) [D502, T86]. *Verified by:* schema test (S3: a blocked install's token is refused). *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-RECOGNITION-EVENTS-06** Every recognition event names the install that sent it: `listener_install_id` (plan name `install_id`) is NOT NULL with a foreign key to `listener_installs`; the platform is kept on the install, not on each event (supersedes blueprint §6 `device_platform` on each log row). *Source:* plan §Data model, rows installs and recognition_events (lines 369–370); blueprint §6 line 214 [D508, B321]. *Verified by:* schema test. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-RECOGNITION-EVENTS-07** `recognition_events.request_id` is NOT NULL. *Source:* plan §Data model, row recognition_events (line 370) [D510]. *Verified by:* schema test. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [x] **S1-RECOGNITION-EVENTS-08** Recognitions are stored in `recognition_events`, which records every attempt (supersedes blueprint §6 `recognition_logs`; the event id as public UUIDv7 `recognition_id` is delivered by S3). *Source:* blueprint §6 line 211; plan §Data model, row recognition_events (line 370) [B317]. *Verified by:* schema test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > names > S1-NAMING-05, S1-MIGRATIONS-10: the slice tables exist under their domain names; the blueprint names do not.
- [ ] **S1-RECOGNITION-EVENTS-09** `recognition_events.captured_at` is a NOT NULL `timestamptz`. *Source:* plan §Data model, row recognition_events (line 370) [D512, B326]. *Verified by:* schema test. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-RECOGNITION-EVENTS-10** `recognition_events.received_at` is the NOT NULL time of first receipt and the partition key. *Source:* plan §Data model, row recognition_events (line 370) [D514, B326]. *Verified by:* schema test. *(built, not yet tested: monthly partitioning on `received_at` is shown by `database-scaling.test.ts` :: recognition event partitions > S1-SCALING-05: an old month can be detached whole without touching the other months; NOT NULL is not tested)*
- [ ] **S1-RECOGNITION-EVENTS-11** A check constraint keeps `captured_at` between `received_at` minus 24 hours and `received_at` plus 2 minutes (supersedes blueprint §6 single `matched_at`). *Source:* plan §Data model, row recognition_events (line 370); plan O12 [D540, B326, T88]. *Verified by:* database test of `recognition_events_captured_at_check`: rows 24 hours and one second early, or 2 minutes and one second late, are refused; rows exactly on the bounds are accepted. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-RECOGNITION-EVENTS-12** `recognition_events.is_deferred` is a NOT NULL boolean, default false. *Source:* plan §Data model, row recognition_events (line 370) [D518, T87]. *Verified by:* schema test. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-RECOGNITION-EVENTS-13** `recognition_events.attempt_count` is NOT NULL, default 1. *Source:* plan §Data model, row recognition_events (line 370) [D519, T87]. *Verified by:* schema test. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-RECOGNITION-EVENTS-14** `recognition_events.completed_at` is a nullable `timestamptz`, set when the outcome is final. *Source:* plan §Data model, row recognition_events (line 370) [D520]. *Verified by:* schema test (S3: NULL while pending, set once a terminal outcome is stored). *(built, not yet tested)*

- [ ] **S1-RECOGNITION-EVENTS-21** `recognition_events.match_margin` records the match margin. *Source:* plan §Data model, row recognition_events (line 370) [D532, T87]. *Verified by:* schema test: numeric type. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-RECOGNITION-EVENTS-22** `recognition_events.match_offset_milliseconds` (plan name `match_offset_ms`) records the match offset, with the unit in its name. *Source:* plan §Data model, row recognition_events (line 370) [D533, T87]. *Verified by:* schema test. *(built, not yet tested)*

### Database schema: audit trail

- [x] **S1-AUDIT-01** Audit records live in their own table, `audit_events`, separate from recognition analytics (supersedes blueprint §6 combined "Audit & Analytics Recognition Logs"). *Source:* blueprint §6 line 209; plan §Data model, row audit_events (line 374) [B334, T90]. *Verified by:* database test: audit rows are written to and read from `audit_events`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > signing in > a wrong hash is refused, counted and audited.
- [x] **S1-AUDIT-02** An audit row records the actor (`actor_portal_user_id`, referencing `portal_users`) and the action. *Source:* plan §Data model, row audit_events (line 374); status-built.md §3 (action) [D561, T90]. *Verified by:* database test reads audit rows back by actor and action. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > signing in > a wrong hash is refused, counted and audited.
- [x] **S1-AUDIT-03** An audit row records the station (`station_id` referencing `stations`), and a station reads only its own audit rows. *Source:* plan §Data model, row audit_events (line 374) [D562, T90]. *Verified by:* schema test; row-level-security test on `audit_events`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > clients > creating a client is audited with who, which station, the request id and when ; apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > row-level security (lookup_api) > S1-TESTS-13, S1-ISOLATION-16: station A's context sees its own audit_events rows and none of station B's.
- [ ] **S1-AUDIT-04** An audit row records the entity: `entity_type` NOT NULL and `entity_id`. *Source:* plan §Data model, row audit_events (line 374) [D563, T90]. *Verified by:* schema test. *(built, not yet tested: the entity type and id are recorded (`stations-and-clients.test.ts` :: clients > creating a client is audited with who, which station, the request id and when), but NOT NULL on `entity_type` is not tested)*
- [x] **S1-AUDIT-05** An audit row records the difference made as `jsonb` (`changes`; plan name `diff`). *Source:* plan §Data model, row audit_events (line 374) [D564, T90]. *Verified by:* schema test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > clients > creating a client is audited with who, which station, the request id and when ; apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > changing where the station is > an owner sets the province and city, and the profile, the audit trail and the next read all agree.
- [x] **S1-AUDIT-06** An audit row records the `request_id` and the time. *Source:* plan §Data model, row audit_events (line 374) [D565, T90]. *Verified by:* schema test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > clients > creating a client is audited with who, which station, the request id and when.

### Scaling

- [x] **S1-SCALING-01** High-volume event tables are partitioned by month: `recognition_events` is range-partitioned on its arrival time, with partitions for the current month and the next three plus a default partition (supersedes G12 "monthly partitions later" and blueprint §6 `recognition_logs`, which grew without bound). *Source:* plan §Standing requirements (line 21); plan G12 (line 294); conventions §Scaling rules [D28, D261, X422, B318, T93]. *Verified by:* `database-scaling.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-scaling.test.ts :: recognition event partitions > has a partition for this month and the next three, plus a default.
- [x] **S1-SCALING-02** `app.create_recognition_event_partitions()` creates missing future months, and running it twice creates nothing new; the API role may call it. *Source:* conventions §Scaling rules [D29, B318, T94]. *Verified by:* `database-scaling.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-scaling.test.ts :: recognition event partitions > the scheduler can top up future months, and running it twice creates nothing new.
- [x] **S1-SCALING-03** The partition function refuses a number of months outside 0 to 24. *Source:* conventions §Scaling rules [D29, T95]. *Verified by:* `database-scaling.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-scaling.test.ts :: recognition event partitions > refuses an unreasonable number of months.
- [ ] **S1-SCALING-04** A scheduled call to `app.create_recognition_event_partitions()` keeps future months' partitions in place. *Source:* conventions §Scaling rules [D29]. *Verified by:* job test: the scheduled run tops up partitions (no scheduler exists yet — see gaps).
- [ ] **S1-SCALING-05** Old months can be detached or dropped whole, without touching other months. *Source:* plan G12 (line 294); conventions §Scaling rules [D261]. *Verified by:* database test: detaching an old month leaves the other months' rows untouched. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-SCALING-06** Recognition idempotency keys live in a separate, small, unpartitioned table (`recognition_idempotency_keys`) whose primary key is (listener install, idempotency key), so uniqueness per install holds across months (supersedes the master data model's `UNIQUE(install_id, idempotency_key)` on `recognition_events`). *Source:* plan §Standing requirements (line 21); plan §Data model, row recognition_events (line 370); conventions §Scaling rules [D30, D509, X425, B320, T89]. *Verified by:* database test: the table is not partitioned; a second insert of the same pair fails whatever month the event falls in. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-SCALING-07** Recognition events have a block-range index on arrival time and an index on station and capture time (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §3 [T97]. *Verified by:* database test finds both indexes. *(built, not yet tested)*

### Start-up checks

- [x] **S1-STARTUP-01** The API refuses to start on a superuser database connection ("Refusing to start"), because row-level security would not apply. *Source:* baseline §1 row 2; plan G2 (line 284); plan §Step 1 Database (line 114) [X214, D220, B146, T34]. *Verified by:* `startup-checks.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/startup-checks.test.ts :: startup refuses an unsafe database role > rejects a superuser connection (row-level security would not apply).
- [ ] **S1-STARTUP-02** The API refuses to start when its database role can bypass row-level security. *Source:* baseline §1 row 2; plan G2 (line 284) [X215, D220, B146, T35]. *Verified by:* `startup-checks.test.ts` with a bypass role. *(built, not yet tested: `apps/api/src/infrastructure/database/database-role-check.ts` refuses `rolbypassrls`; `startup-checks.test.ts` tries only a superuser and `lookup_api`)*
- [ ] **S1-STARTUP-03** The API refuses to start when its database role owns tables (the boot check trips when connected as the owner). *Source:* baseline §1 row 2; plan §Verified facts, Postgres (line 262); plan §Step 1 tests (line 128); plan §Phases row 1 Verify (line 643) [X216, D183, D220, D636, X495, B146, T35]. *Verified by:* `startup-checks.test.ts` with a table-owning role. *(built, not yet tested: `apps/api/src/infrastructure/database/database-role-check.ts` refuses a role that owns application tables; no test connects as a table owner)*
- [x] **S1-STARTUP-04** Starting as `lookup_api` passes the role check. *Source:* plan G2 (line 284) [D220, T23]. *Verified by:* `startup-checks.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/startup-checks.test.ts :: startup refuses an unsafe database role > accepts the restricted lookup_api role.
- [ ] **S1-STARTUP-05** The role check runs automatically when the application boots (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §1 [T36]. *Verified by:* a boot test with an unsafe role fails before serving any request. *(built, not yet tested: `DatabaseModule.onApplicationBootstrap` in `apps/api/src/infrastructure/database/database.module.ts`, so every API test boots through the check as `lookup_api`; no boot test with an unsafe role)*

### Errors (two layers)

- [x] **S1-ERRORS-01** G9: every error response from every endpoint has exactly the shape `{ "error": { "code", "message", "request_id" } }`, with no extra keys (new: blueprint §7 defined only the success response). *Source:* plan §Step 1 Errors (line 108); plan G9 (line 291); baseline §2 row 2 [X84, D245, B362, X251, T107]. *Verified by:* `error-responses.test.ts`: 400, 404, 413, 429, 500 and 503 bodies each have exactly that shape (once S2 adds the error-envelope schema, they also validate against it — S2-CONTRACTS-02). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > (every case, through expectErrorEnvelope).
- [x] **S1-ERRORS-02** `AppError` carries a code, an HTTP status, a public message and a private internal detail; the internal detail goes only to the log, never to the response. *Source:* plan §Step 1 Errors (line 108) [D612, T113]. *Verified by:* API test: an error with a private detail answers with the catalogue message only. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > a message written for the user is relayed; its private detail is not.
- [x] **S1-ERRORS-03** `AppError` also carries an optional cause, and the filter logs both the internal detail and the cause. *Source:* plan §Cross-cutting, error model (line 612) [X85]. *Verified by:* unit test: an `AppError` built with a cause reaches the filter, which answers with the public message only and logs the detail and the cause. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/health-when-database-is-down.test.ts :: health when the database is down > returns a generic 503 with a request id, and logs which dependency failed, with the stack ; apps/api/test/log-privacy.test.ts :: logged errors never carry personal values > a refusal caused by a database error logs the cause, without its values.
- [ ] **S1-ERRORS-04** `ERROR_CATALOG` maps every error code the API can return to its HTTP status and a public message written for people, and a response can only use a code that is in the catalogue. *Source:* plan §Step 1 Errors (line 108); plan §Cross-cutting (line 612) [D613, X86, T118]. *Verified by:* unit test: each entry has a status and a non-empty message; a scan of `apps/api/src` finds no code missing from the catalogue; leak test: each body's `code` is a catalogue entry. *(built, not yet tested: `apps/api/src/common/errors/error-catalog.ts`; `AppError` and the filter take only the `ErrorCode` type, so the compiler refuses an unknown code, but no unit test checks the entries, and the contracts' `ErrorEnvelope` checks a code's shape, not that it is in the catalogue)*
- [ ] **S1-ERRORS-05** The catalogue defines 13 codes with plain-language messages: `BAD_REQUEST`, `INVALID_JSON`, `VALIDATION_FAILED`, `UNAUTHENTICATED`, `FORBIDDEN`, `NOT_FOUND`, `METHOD_NOT_ALLOWED`, `CONFLICT`, `PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE`, `UNSUPPORTED_MEDIA_TYPE`, `RATE_LIMITED`, `INTERNAL` and `SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE` (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §4 [T118]. *Verified by:* API tests reach each code (`UNAUTHENTICATED`, `METHOD_NOT_ALLOWED` and `UNSUPPORTED_MEDIA_TYPE` are not reached yet). *(built, not yet tested: `apps/api/src/common/errors/error-catalog.ts`, which now holds 18 codes: the 13 listed plus `INVALID_CREDENTIALS`, `STATION_NOT_ACTIVE`, `AD_NOT_READY_TO_PUBLISH`, `UPLOAD_NOT_RECEIVED` and `UPLOAD_REJECTED`; `UNAUTHENTICATED` is now reached by `authentication.test.ts`, but no test reaches `METHOD_NOT_ALLOWED` or `UNSUPPORTED_MEDIA_TYPE`)*
- [x] **S1-ERRORS-06** One global exception filter (`AllExceptionsFilter`) handles every error that leaves an HTTP handler, including unknown routes, body-parser failures and thrown errors. (That no controller builds its own error body is S1-ERRORS-22, proved by the scanner of S1-TESTS-14; both are open.) *Source:* plan §Step 1 Errors (line 108); plan §Cross-cutting, chokepoints (line 617); global rules §Fix it centrally [D614, X111, T117]. *Verified by:* API tests: unknown route → 404 envelope; malformed JSON → 400 envelope; thrown error → 500 envelope. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > unknown routes get a 404 envelope ; apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > malformed JSON gets a plain 400 without the parser message ; apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > programming errors become a generic 500.
- [x] **S1-ERRORS-07** The filter recognises database errors (`QueryFailedError` and driver errors) first, before any general case, always answers them as `INTERNAL`, and never relays their text — including a malformed id that reaches the database. *Source:* plan §Step 1 Errors (line 108); plan §Cross-cutting (line 615); global rules §Fix it centrally ("Catch the technical type first") [D615, X91, T109, T110, T117]. *Verified by:* leak test: a forced unique violation and a malformed id each return the generic 500 with no SQLSTATE, constraint or table name. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > database errors become a generic 500, with the real error and stack in the log ; apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > a malformed id reaching the database gives no driver text back.
- [x] **S1-ERRORS-08** The error filter never relays a thrown exception's `message` to the caller (Tonse-hub's filter leaks it at `global-exception.filter.ts:28-29` and must not be copied); framework exception text is never passed through either, a framework 400 answering with the catalogue's `BAD_REQUEST` message. *Source:* plan §Reused from Tonse-hub, Do differently (line 90); plan §Verified facts (line 270); global rules §Two layers [D104, X92, T112, T114]. *Verified by:* leak tests for database, programming and framework errors. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > programming errors become a generic 500 ; apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > framework exception text is never passed through.
- [ ] **S1-ERRORS-09** The generic 500 answer is identical in every environment (development, test, production): no environment relays exception text. *Source:* plan §Cross-cutting "in every environment" (line 614); plan §Step 1 Errors (line 108); plan §Verified facts, Tonse-hub filter (line 270) [X89, D104, D616]. *Verified by:* API test run with the environment set to development and to production: the 500 body is the same apart from the request id. *(built, not yet tested: `apps/api/src/common/errors/all-exceptions.filter.ts` has no environment branch; the tests run only with `NODE_ENV=test`)*
- [x] **S1-ERRORS-10** The filter never passes an exception's `detail` (or any other internal field) through to the response. *Source:* plan §Verified facts, Tonse-hub filter "passes detail through" (line 270) [D193, X93, T109, T113]. *Verified by:* leak test: a driver error with a `detail` naming a column answers without that text. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > database errors become a generic 500, with the real error and stack in the log ; apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > a message written for the user is relayed; its private detail is not.
- [x] **S1-ERRORS-11** Any error that is not an application error with a user-written message becomes 500 `INTERNAL` with the generic catalogue message. *Source:* plan §Cross-cutting (line 614) [X88, T112]. *Verified by:* API test: a route throwing a plain `Error("secret detail")` answers 500 `INTERNAL` with no "secret detail". *Evidence:* apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > programming errors become a generic 500.
- [x] **S1-ERRORS-12** Every 5xx response carries the generic catalogue message, even when the thrown exception carries its own message. *Source:* plan §Step 1 Errors "5xx responses always use the generic message" (line 108) [X90, D616, T109, T102]. *Verified by:* API tests: a database error and a dependency failure each answer with the catalogue text only. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > database errors become a generic 500, with the real error and stack in the log ; apps/api/test/health-when-key-value-store-is-down.test.ts :: health when the key-value store is down > returns a generic 503 with a request id, and logs the real cause privately.
- [x] **S1-ERRORS-13** A message written for users is relayed to the caller as written (for example `409 CONFLICT` "This conflicts with something that already exists."; later "This code has expired", "Please update the app"). *Source:* plan §Cross-cutting (line 613); global rules §Fix it centrally ("Relay the first") [X87, T113]. *Verified by:* API test: an `AppError` with a catalogue user message returns that exact message. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > a message written for the user is relayed; its private detail is not.
- [x] **S1-ERRORS-14** No public response contains an exception class name (`QueryFailedError`, `TypeError`, `SyntaxError`, `HttpException`, `Exception`). *Source:* global rules §Two layers; baseline §2 row 2 [X94, T108]. *Verified by:* the shared internal-detail scanner runs on every error case. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > (every case, through assertNoInternalDetails in apps/api/test/support/internal-detail-patterns.ts).
- [x] **S1-ERRORS-15** No public response contains a stack trace. *Source:* global rules §Two layers; baseline §2 row 2 [X95, T108]. *Verified by:* the scanner rejects `at \S+ \(` and indented `at ` lines. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > (every case, through assertNoInternalDetails in apps/api/test/support/internal-detail-patterns.ts).
- [x] **S1-ERRORS-16** No public response contains a SQLSTATE code or database driver text (for example "duplicate key", "violates", `ECONNREFUSED`). *Source:* global rules §Two layers; baseline §2 row 2 [X96, T108, T102]. *Verified by:* the scanner rejects `SQLSTATE`, `23505`, `duplicate key` and `violates`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > (every case, through assertNoInternalDetails in apps/api/test/support/internal-detail-patterns.ts).
- [x] **S1-ERRORS-17** No public response contains a table, column, constraint or relation name. *Source:* global rules §Two layers [X97, T108, T109]. *Verified by:* forced unique and foreign-key violations answer with no table or constraint name (only the unique case is tested today). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > database errors become a generic 500, with the real error and stack in the log.
- [x] **S1-ERRORS-18** No public response contains a file path (`node_modules`, `/app/`, `/src/`, Windows drive paths, `.ts:<line>`). *Source:* global rules §Two layers; plan §Cross-cutting regex (line 634) [X98, T108]. *Verified by:* the scanner's path patterns. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > (every case, through assertNoInternalDetails in apps/api/test/support/internal-detail-patterns.ts).
- [ ] **S1-ERRORS-19** No public response contains a configuration value (for example the database host or a secret). *Source:* global rules §Two layers; baseline §6 row 1 [X100]. *Verified by:* leak test: a forced configuration error answers without any configured value. *(built, not yet tested: `AllExceptionsFilter` answers catalogue text only; `health-when-database-is-down.test.ts` checks the database port is absent from its 503 and `startup-checks.test.ts` ('names the bad variable without echoing values') checks configuration errors, but no test forces a configuration error into a response)*
- [x] **S1-ERRORS-20** For every 5xx answer the server logs, privately, the full message, the stack trace, the route and the request id. *Source:* global rules §Two layers ("Private layer"); baseline §2 row 2 [X101, T122]. *Verified by:* log-capture test: a forced error produces one log line with message, stack, route and the response's request id. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > database errors become a generic 500, with the real error and stack in the log.
- [x] **S1-ERRORS-21** Every 5xx `HttpException` is logged with its full detail, not only unexpected errors. *Source:* plan §Reused from Tonse-hub, Do differently (line 96) [X102, D117, T123]. *Verified by:* log-capture test: a 503 produces an error line with the stack and the request id. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/health-when-key-value-store-is-down.test.ts :: health when the key-value store is down > returns a generic 503 with a request id, and logs the real cause privately.
- [x] **S1-ERRORS-22** One central helper (the `sendServerError`-style helper) logs the detail and returns the clean message, and every API catch block that answers a caller uses it or rethrows to the global filter. *Source:* plan §Cross-cutting, chokepoints (line 617); global rules §Fix it centrally [X103, T117]. *Verified by:* whole-file code scan of `apps/api/src` (S1-TESTS-14). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/source-scan.test.ts :: source scan > only the exception filter writes HTTP responses ; apps/api/test/source-scan.test.ts :: source scan > an error's own message never becomes a public message, on one line or across several.
- [x] **S1-ERRORS-23** A database connection failure at boot goes through the central error path: it is logged as one structured JSON line with its stack trace, and no connection string or password appears in the log or console. *Source:* plan §Cross-cutting, chokepoints "The DB connection-failure path, at boot" (line 618); global rules §Fix it centrally [X112, T130]. *Verified by:* start-up test with an unreachable database host: one structured error line with a stack, no configured password or URL. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/process-failures.test.ts :: process failures are logged as one structured line, then the process exits > an unreachable database at start-up: one fatal JSON line with the stack, no password, nothing else, exit 1.
- [x] **S1-ERRORS-24** Start-up failures outside any request are logged at fatal level with the error, and the process exits (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §4 [T130]. *Verified by:* process test: a start-up failure produces one fatal JSON line and a non-zero exit code. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/process-failures.test.ts :: process failures are logged as one structured line, then the process exits > an unreachable database at start-up: one fatal JSON line with the stack, no password, nothing else, exit 1.
- [x] **S1-ERRORS-25** A Node `unhandledRejection` writes one fatal structured log line with the stack, then the process exits. *Source:* plan §Step 1 Startup (line 106); plan §Cross-cutting chokepoints (line 619); global rules §Fix it centrally [X115, D611, T130]. *Verified by:* process test: an unhandled rejection in a child process logs one fatal JSON line and exits non-zero. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/process-failures.test.ts :: process failures are logged as one structured line, then the process exits > an unhandled-rejection: one fatal JSON line with the stack and personal values masked, exit 1.
- [x] **S1-ERRORS-26** A Node `uncaughtException` writes one fatal structured log line with the stack, then the process exits. *Source:* plan §Step 1 Startup (line 106); plan §Cross-cutting chokepoints (line 619) [X116, D611, T130]. *Verified by:* process test: a throw outside any handler in a child process logs one fatal JSON line and exits non-zero. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/process-failures.test.ts :: process failures are logged as one structured line, then the process exits > an uncaught-exception: one fatal JSON line with the stack and personal values masked, exit 1.
- [x] **S1-ERRORS-27** An unknown route answers 404 `NOT_FOUND` in the error envelope, with a request id. *Source:* plan §Step 1 tests (line 125) [D633, X197, T116]. *Verified by:* API test: `GET /api/v1/does-not-exist`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > unknown routes get a 404 envelope.
- [x] **S1-ERRORS-28** Broken percent-encoding in the URL gets a plain 400 `BAD_REQUEST` (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §4 [T111]. *Verified by:* API test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > broken percent-encoding in the URL gets a plain 400.

### Request ids (correlation)

- [x] **S1-REQUEST-ID-01** Every response, success or error, carries an `X-Request-Id` header; without a caller id the server generates a UUID. *Source:* plan §Cross-cutting, correlation id (line 621); baseline §2 row 1 [X123, X250, T104]. *Verified by:* API test: health, 404 and 500 responses carry the header. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/health-and-request-ids.test.ts :: health and request ids > reports ok when the database and key-value store answer.
- [x] **S1-REQUEST-ID-02** Every error body carries `request_id`, equal to the `X-Request-Id` header of the same response (Tonse-hub's filter had none). *Source:* plan §Cross-cutting (line 621); plan §Verified facts (line 270); global rules §Correlation id [X124, D194, T107]. *Verified by:* API test for 400, 404 and 500. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > (every case, through expectErrorEnvelope).
- [x] **S1-REQUEST-ID-03** A caller's `X-Request-Id` that is a valid UUID is kept and echoed back. *Source:* plan §Step 1 Request context (line 109); plan §Step 1 tests (line 127); plan §Recognition flow step 2 (line 479) [X126, D617, D635, B346, T105]. *Verified by:* API test: send a UUID; the response header carries the same UUID. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/health-and-request-ids.test.ts :: health and request ids > echoes a well-formed request id from the caller.
- [x] **S1-REQUEST-ID-04** A malformed or hostile `X-Request-Id` (not a UUID, forged log JSON, markup, path tricks, a 2,000-character value, a UUID with extra text, newlines) is replaced with a fresh server-generated UUID. *Source:* plan §Step 1 Request context (line 109); baseline §2 row 1 ("hostile ids replaced") [X127, D617, B346, X250, T106]. *Verified by:* API test per hostile value; no log line contains the sent value. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/health-and-request-ids.test.ts :: health and request ids > replaces a hostile request id (<case>) with a fresh one.
- [ ] **S1-REQUEST-ID-05** The request context (nestjs-cls) carries the request id for the whole request, and the same id tags every log line written while handling it. *Source:* plan §Roles and RLS, Context in NestJS (line 392); plan §Step 1 Request context (line 109); plan §Do differently (line 91); global rules §Correlation id; baseline §2 row 1 [X125, D587, D105]. *Verified by:* log-capture test: all lines produced by one request share its id. *(built, not yet tested: `apps/api/src/common/http/assign-request-id.ts` sets the id, nestjs-pino's `genReqId` (`infrastructure/logging/logging.module.ts`) puts it on request lines, and `ClsModule` (`app.module.ts`) carries it to the audit trail; tests find single lines by the id, but none checks that every line of one request carries it)*
- [x] **S1-REQUEST-ID-06** Searching the logs for the request id of an error response finds the full error: message, stack trace and route. *Source:* global rules §Correlation id ("The user quotes the id; you grep it") [X128, T122]. *Verified by:* log-capture test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > database errors become a generic 500, with the real error and stack in the log.

### Logging

- [x] **S1-LOGGING-01** API logs are structured JSON, one object per line (the plan names nestjs-pino). *Source:* plan §Step 1 Logging (line 110); plan §Cross-cutting, structured logs (line 622); plan §Do differently (line 91); global rules §Logging; baseline §7 row 1 [X139, D618, D105, X339, T122]. *Verified by:* log-capture test: each captured line parses as one JSON object. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > database errors become a generic 500, with the real error and stack in the log.
- [ ] **S1-LOGGING-02** The API writes logs only to standard output (or standard error), in every environment, and never depends on a local log file being writable. *Source:* plan §Step 1 Logging (line 110); global rules §Logging ("Write where the platform already captures it") [X144, D618, T126]. *Verified by:* start-up test with a read-only working directory: the API starts and logs normally; no log file is written. *(built, not yet tested: `LOG_DESTINATION` is `process.stdout` in `apps/api/src/config/config.module.ts`, and the process logger writes to stdout; `process-failures.test.ts` shows normal and failed starts write JSON only to stdout, but no test starts with a read-only working directory)*
- [ ] **S1-LOGGING-03** Every log line carries a timestamp and a level. *Source:* global rules §Logging ("timestamp, level, …") [X140]. *Verified by:* log-capture test: each line has `time` and `level`. *(built, not yet tested: pino's default `time` and `level` fields (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/logging/logging.module.ts`, `apps/api/src/process-failures.ts`); tests read `level` on error and fatal lines, but none checks `time`)*
- [x] **S1-LOGGING-04** Every request log line carries the method, the route path (without query string) and the status. *Source:* global rules §Logging ("route"); status-built.md §4 [X141, T128]. *Verified by:* log-capture test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/log-privacy.test.ts :: logged errors never carry personal values > S1-LOGGING-15: by default a request line holds only id, method and path: no headers, no query string.
- [x] **S1-LOGGING-05** Error log lines carry the error message and the full stack trace. *Source:* global rules §Logging ("Log the stack trace, not just the message"); plan §Cross-cutting (line 622); baseline §7 row 1 [X143, X339, T122]. *Verified by:* log-capture test: a forced error's line has a non-empty stack field. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > database errors become a generic 500, with the real error and stack in the log.
- [ ] **S1-LOGGING-06** A newline in caller-supplied input (path, header, body field) never produces a forged extra log line; each event stays one JSON line. *Source:* global rules §Logging ("JSON-encoding also prevents attacker-supplied newlines from forging a fake log line") [X145]. *Verified by:* log-capture test: a request whose path and user agent contain an encoded newline and a fake JSON object yields exactly the expected number of lines. *(built, not yet tested: each event is one JSON-encoded line (pino, `infrastructure/logging/logging.module.ts`), and Node refuses raw line breaks in headers (noted in `health-and-request-ids.test.ts`); no test sends an encoded newline in a path or user agent and counts the lines)*
- [x] **S1-LOGGING-07** The authorization header is redacted from every log line. *Source:* plan §Step 1 Logging (line 110); plan §Cross-cutting (line 623); baseline §7 row 2 [X147, D619, D105, X340, T127]. *Verified by:* log-capture test: a request with `Authorization: Bearer secret-value` leaves no trace of `secret-value`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/log-request-details.test.ts :: request logging with LOG_REQUEST_DETAILS on > logs headers but redacts cookies, authorization and set-cookie.
- [x] **S1-LOGGING-08** Cookies (request `Cookie` and response `Set-Cookie`) are redacted from every log line. *Source:* plan §Step 1 Logging (line 110); baseline §7 row 2 [X148, D619, X340, T127]. *Verified by:* log-capture test with a session cookie. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/log-request-details.test.ts :: request logging with LOG_REQUEST_DETAILS on > logs headers but redacts cookies, authorization and set-cookie ; apps/api/test/log-request-details.test.ts :: request logging with LOG_REQUEST_DETAILS on > redacts the session cookies a successful sign-in sets ; apps/api/test/log-privacy.test.ts :: logged errors never carry personal values > S1-LOGGING-15: by default a request line holds only id, method and path: no headers, no query string.
- [x] **S1-LOGGING-09** Passwords and password hashes are redacted from every log line. *Source:* plan §Step 1 Logging (line 110); baseline §7 row 2 [X149, D619, X340, T127]. *Verified by:* log-capture test with request-detail logging on: a sign-in body's password never appears. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/log-request-details.test.ts :: request logging with LOG_REQUEST_DETAILS on > logs headers but redacts cookies, authorization and set-cookie ; apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > signing in > never puts the password or the email in a response or in the logs ; apps/api/test/log-privacy.test.ts :: logged errors never carry personal values > a failed query keeps its SQLSTATE, SQL text and stack, but not its parameters or the quoted value.
- [x] **S1-LOGGING-10** Tokens (access, refresh, install and invitation tokens) are redacted from every log line. *Source:* baseline §7 row 2 [X150, X340, T127]. *Verified by:* log-capture test: token values sent in headers, cookies and bodies never appear. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/log-request-details.test.ts :: request logging with LOG_REQUEST_DETAILS on > logs headers but redacts cookies, authorization and set-cookie ; apps/api/test/client-errors.test.ts :: client error reports > accepts a report without signing in and logs it at error level, scrubbed, with the related request id.
- [x] **S1-LOGGING-11** Email addresses are redacted from every log line. *Source:* plan §Step 1 Logging (line 110); plan §Cross-cutting (lines 623, 635); baseline §7 row 2; global rules §Logging [X151, X199, D619, X340, T127]. *Verified by:* log-capture test: an email in a header and a body, with request-detail logging on, is absent from every line. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/log-request-details.test.ts :: request logging with LOG_REQUEST_DETAILS on > logs headers but redacts cookies, authorization and set-cookie ; apps/api/test/log-privacy.test.ts :: logged errors never carry personal values > S1-LOGGING-15: by default a request line holds only id, method and path: no headers, no query string ; apps/api/test/log-privacy.test.ts :: logged errors never carry personal values > a failed query keeps its SQLSTATE, SQL text and stack, but not its parameters or the quoted value ; apps/api/test/client-errors.test.ts :: client error reports > accepts a report without signing in and logs it at error level, scrubbed, with the related request id.
- [x] **S1-LOGGING-12** Phone numbers are redacted from every log line. *Source:* plan §Step 1 Logging (line 110); plan §Cross-cutting (lines 623, 635); baseline §7 row 2; global rules §Logging [X152, X200, D619, X340, T127]. *Verified by:* log-capture test with an E.164 number. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/log-privacy.test.ts :: logged errors never carry personal values > a check violation keeps the constraint and table, but not the failing row ; apps/api/test/client-errors.test.ts :: client error reports > accepts a report without signing in and logs it at error level, scrubbed, with the related request id ; apps/api/test/log-privacy.test.ts :: serializeErrorForLog > masks personal values in messages that are not database errors.
- [ ] **S1-LOGGING-13** Audio never appears in a log line (bytes or base64). *Source:* plan §Step 1 Logging (line 110); plan §Cross-cutting (line 623); baseline §7 row 2; blueprint §8 line 281 [X153, B384, D619, X340, T127]. *Verified by:* log-capture test: a multipart audio upload's bytes and base64 never appear. *(built, not yet tested: audio never passes through the API (uploads go straight to storage by presigned PUT, design S2-D2), and `REDACTED_LOG_PATHS` in `infrastructure/logging/logging.module.ts` covers `*.audio` and `*.audioBase64`; no test checks a log for audio)*
- [x] **S1-LOGGING-14** Personal identifiers appear in logs only as HMAC(pepper, id) prefixes, never raw; the rate limiter logs and stores only a hash of the client address. *Source:* plan §Cross-cutting (line 624); plan §Do differently (line 91); baseline §7 row 3 ("Built (rate limiting)") [X157, T160]. *Verified by:* log-capture test: a rate-limited request logs the hashed client key, not the raw address. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/log-privacy.test.ts :: logged errors never carry personal values > every line of a signed-in request names the actor by a keyed hash, never by id or email ; apps/api/test/client-errors.test.ts :: client error reports > limits reports to 30 a minute per client address (the counter is kept under a keyed hash of the address; the address is neither in the store nor in the logs).
- [x] **S1-LOGGING-15** Request bodies and query strings are logged only when `LOG_REQUEST_DETAILS` is on, and it is off by default (the plan's `LOG_DEBUG=false`). *Source:* baseline §7 row 2; plan §Cross-cutting (line 624); global rules §Logging ("Verbose request dumps go behind a debug flag that is off by default") [X158, X340, T128]. *Verified by:* log-capture test with the default configuration: no body or query string; with the flag on, they appear redacted. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/log-privacy.test.ts :: logged errors never carry personal values > S1-LOGGING-15: by default a request line holds only id, method and path: no headers, no query string ; apps/api/test/log-request-details.test.ts :: request logging with LOG_REQUEST_DETAILS on > logs headers but redacts cookies, authorization and set-cookie.
- [ ] **S1-LOGGING-16** Health checks are not written to the request log (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §4 [T129]. *Verified by:* log-capture test. *(built, not yet tested: `autoLogging.ignore` for `/api/v1/health` in `apps/api/src/infrastructure/logging/logging.module.ts`)*
- [x] **S1-LOGGING-17** Caller mistakes (4xx) are logged at info level without a stack; server faults (5xx) at error level with the stack (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §4 [T125]. *Verified by:* log-capture test of a 400 and a 500 (the info-level path is untested). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/log-privacy.test.ts :: logged errors never carry personal values > S1-LOGGING-17: a plain caller mistake is logged at info level, without a stack ; apps/api/test/log-privacy.test.ts :: logged errors never carry personal values > a failed query keeps its SQLSTATE, SQL text and stack, but not its parameters or the quoted value.
- [x] **S1-LOGGING-18** A refused request is logged with its private reason (for example the cross-site check) under its request id (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §4 [T124]. *Verified by:* log-capture test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: request-layer security > cross-site request forgery (cookie requests) > refuses a cookie-carrying write from another site, and logs why.
- [ ] **S1-LOGGING-19** Every log line is tagged with the service name (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §4 [T126]. *Verified by:* log-capture test. *(built, not yet tested: `base: { service: 'lookup-api' }` in `apps/api/src/infrastructure/logging/logging.module.ts` and `apps/api/src/process-failures.ts`; only the start-up fatal line's service is checked (`process-failures.test.ts`))*

### HTTP requests

- [x] **S1-REQUESTS-01** API responses carry `Strict-Transport-Security` with a max-age, so browsers use HTTPS only. *Source:* baseline §2 row 3; blueprint §3.1 line 67 (kept) [X252, B155, T137]. *Verified by:* `request-security.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: request-layer security > response headers > sends security headers, forbids caching, and hides the framework.
- [x] **S1-REQUESTS-02** API responses carry a content security policy with `default-src 'self'`. *Source:* baseline §2 row 3 [X253, T138]. *Verified by:* `request-security.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: request-layer security > response headers > sends security headers, forbids caching, and hides the framework.
- [x] **S1-REQUESTS-03** API responses carry `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`. *Source:* baseline §2 row 3 [X254, T136]. *Verified by:* `request-security.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: request-layer security > response headers > sends security headers, forbids caching, and hides the framework.
- [x] **S1-REQUESTS-04** API responses carry frame options that forbid framing (`X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN`). *Source:* baseline §2 row 3 [X255, T139]. *Verified by:* `request-security.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: request-layer security > response headers > sends security headers, forbids caching, and hides the framework.
- [x] **S1-REQUESTS-05** Security headers are set (the plan names helmet), and no response reveals the framework or language version: no `X-Powered-By` header and no version strings. *Source:* plan §Step 1 Startup (line 106); baseline §2 row 3; global rules §Two layers [D608, X256, X99, T140]. *Verified by:* `request-security.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: request-layer security > response headers > sends security headers, forbids caching, and hides the framework.
- [x] **S1-REQUESTS-06** API responses are never cached: every response carries `Cache-Control: no-store`. *Source:* baseline §2 row 3 [X257, T135]. *Verified by:* `request-security.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: request-layer security > response headers > sends security headers, forbids caching, and hides the framework.
- [x] **S1-REQUESTS-07** An allowed portal origin gets `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` set to itself, with credentials allowed. *Source:* baseline §2 row 4 [X258, T141]. *Verified by:* `request-security.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: request-layer security > cross-origin requests (CORS) > allows the portal origin with credentials, and nobody else.
- [x] **S1-REQUESTS-08** Any origin that is not exactly listed gets no `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`. *Source:* baseline §2 row 4 [X258, T142]. *Verified by:* `request-security.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: request-layer security > cross-origin requests (CORS) > allows the portal origin with credentials, and nobody else.
- [x] **S1-REQUESTS-09** Error responses carry the cross-origin headers too, so the portal can read the request id (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §5 [T143]. *Verified by:* `request-security.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: request-layer security > cross-origin requests (CORS) > error responses carry CORS headers too, so the portal can show the request id.
- [ ] **S1-REQUESTS-10** Browsers may cache the cross-origin preflight answer for 600 seconds (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §5 [T144]. *Verified by:* API test of `Access-Control-Max-Age`. *(built, not yet tested: `maxAge: 600` in `enableCors`, `apps/api/src/configure-http-application.ts`)*
- [x] **S1-REQUESTS-11** Cross-site request forgery: a write request that carries cookies from a site that is not an allowed origin is refused with 403 `FORBIDDEN`, and the reason is logged. *Source:* baseline §2 row 5 [X260, T145]. *Verified by:* `request-security.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: request-layer security > cross-site request forgery (cookie requests) > refuses a cookie-carrying write from another site, and logs why.
- [x] **S1-REQUESTS-12** A cookie-carrying write with neither `Origin` nor `Referer` is refused with 403 `FORBIDDEN` (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §5 [T146]. *Verified by:* `request-security.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: request-layer security > cross-site request forgery (cookie requests) > refuses a cookie-carrying write with no origin at all.
- [x] **S1-REQUESTS-13** Cookie-carrying writes from the portal are allowed, identified by `Origin` or by `Referer` (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §5 [T147]. *Verified by:* `request-security.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: request-layer security > cross-site request forgery (cookie requests) > allows cookie writes from the portal (Origin or Referer).
- [x] **S1-REQUESTS-14** Writes without cookies (the mobile app) and reads (GET) with cookies are not blocked by the cross-site check (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §5 [T148]. *Verified by:* `request-security.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: request-layer security > cross-site request forgery (cookie requests) > does not affect the mobile app (no cookies) or methods without side effects.
- [x] **S1-REQUESTS-15** Every input is validated by a strict schema, and valid input reaches the handler parsed. *Source:* baseline §2 row 7 [X265, T149]. *Verified by:* `request-security.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: request-layer security > input validation > passes valid input through, parsed.
- [x] **S1-REQUESTS-16** Unknown input fields are refused (never passed through to handlers) with 400 `VALIDATION_FAILED` and reason `unrecognized_keys`, without echoing the unknown field's name (supersedes the plan's "stripped or refused" `whitelist` option: the baseline's zod pipe refuses). *Source:* baseline §2 row 7; plan §Reused from Tonse-hub (line 86) [X266, D100, T150]. *Verified by:* `request-security.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: request-layer security > input validation > rejects unknown fields without echoing their names.
- [x] **S1-REQUESTS-17** A validation error names the field path and a reason code, never the submitted value. *Source:* baseline §2 row 7 [X267, T151]. *Verified by:* `request-security.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: request-layer security > input validation > names the bad field and a reason code, never the submitted value.
- [x] **S1-REQUESTS-18** A missing body is rejected with 400 `VALIDATION_FAILED` (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §5 [T152]. *Verified by:* `request-security.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: request-layer security > input validation > rejects a missing body.
- [ ] **S1-REQUESTS-19** A validation answer reports at most 20 issues, each path cut to 120 characters (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §5 [T153]. *Verified by:* API test with 25 bad fields and a 200-character path. *(built, not yet tested: `MAXIMUM_REPORTED_ISSUES` and `MAXIMUM_PATH_LENGTH` in `apps/api/src/common/validation/zod-validation.pipe.ts`; the contracts' `ErrorEnvelope` also allows at most 20 fields and 120-character paths, but no test sends 25 bad fields or a long path)*
- [x] **S1-REQUESTS-20** JSON request bodies are limited to 100 KB; a larger body gets 413 `PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE`. *Source:* baseline §2 row 8 [X268, T119]. *Verified by:* `error-responses.test.ts`: 101 KB → 413. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > oversized JSON bodies get a 413.
- [x] **S1-REQUESTS-21** Body-parser failures are answered in the error envelope: malformed JSON gets 400 `INVALID_JSON` without the parser's own wording. *Source:* baseline §2 row 8 [X269, T115]. *Verified by:* `error-responses.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > malformed JSON gets a plain 400 without the parser message.
- [x] **S1-REQUESTS-22** A valid JSON body still reaches its handler intact (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §4 [T120]. *Verified by:* `error-responses.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > valid JSON still works.
- [x] **S1-REQUESTS-23** The server cuts slow clients off with a header timeout of 20 seconds. *Source:* baseline §2 row 9 [X270, T161]. *Verified by:* `request-security.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: request-layer security > slow-client protection > sets header, request and keep-alive timeouts.
- [x] **S1-REQUESTS-24** The server has a whole-request timeout of 60 seconds. *Source:* baseline §2 row 9 [X271, T161]. *Verified by:* `request-security.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: request-layer security > slow-client protection > sets header, request and keep-alive timeouts.
- [x] **S1-REQUESTS-25** The server closes idle keep-alive connections after 65 seconds. *Source:* baseline §2 row 9 [X272, T161]. *Verified by:* `request-security.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: request-layer security > slow-client protection > sets header, request and keep-alive timeouts.
- [ ] **S1-REQUESTS-26** A request may carry at most 100 headers (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §5 [T162]. *Verified by:* API test with 101 headers. *(built, not yet tested: `server.maxHeadersCount = 100` in `applyServerTimeouts`, `apps/api/src/configure-http-application.ts`)*
