### Rate limits

- [x] **S1-RATE-LIMITS-01** Every URL, unknown ones included, has a baseline request limit per client address, applied before the route handler runs; once exceeded even health answers 429 in the envelope, while other addresses are unaffected. *Source:* baseline §2 row 6; blueprint §3 line 29 ("Rate Limiting") (kept) [X261, B79, T155]. *Verified by:* `request-security.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: request-layer security > rate limits > the baseline limit covers every URL, unknown ones included.
- [x] **S1-RATE-LIMITS-02** Routes can carry stricter limits than the baseline; past its maximum a route answers 429 `RATE_LIMITED` with a `Retry-After` of 1 to 60 seconds, counted per client address. *Source:* baseline §2 row 6 [X262, T154]. *Verified by:* `request-security.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: request-layer security > rate limits > a route limit returns 429 with Retry-After, per client.
- [ ] **S1-RATE-LIMITS-03** Rate-limit counters live in the shared key-value store, so a limit holds across two or more API instances. *Source:* baseline §2 row 6; plan §Target architecture ("2+ instances") [X263, B80, T160]. *Verified by:* test with two application instances sharing the store: requests split across them count against one limit. *(built, not yet tested: counters live in Valkey (`apps/api/src/common/rate-limiting/rate-limiter.ts`); no test runs two application instances)*
- [x] **S1-RATE-LIMITS-04** When the counter store is unreachable, a limit marked sensitive (for example sign-in) refuses the request with `SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE`, never lets it pass. *Source:* baseline §2 row 6 and §3 [X264, B81, T158]. *Verified by:* `request-security.test.ts` with the store down. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: rate limiter when its store is unreachable > refuses requests for counters marked to refuse (e.g. sign-in).
- [x] **S1-RATE-LIMITS-05** When the counter store is unreachable, other limits let the request through and log one warning (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §5 [T159]. *Verified by:* `request-security.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: rate limiter when its store is unreachable > allows other requests through and logs a warning.
- [x] **S1-RATE-LIMITS-06** `trust proxy` is set so limits see the real client address: the address is taken from `X-Forwarded-For` through exactly `TRUSTED_PROXY_COUNT` proxy hops (default 1, at most 5), so two clients behind one proxy are counted separately (the Render hop count is checked at first deploy, P8). *Source:* plan §Reused from Tonse-hub, Do differently (line 96); baseline §2 row 10 [X273, D118, B82, T157]. *Verified by:* request test with one trusted proxy. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: request-layer security > rate limits > a route limit returns 429 with Retry-After, per client.
- [ ] **S1-RATE-LIMITS-07** A forged `X-Forwarded-For` entry beyond the trusted hop count is ignored, so a client cannot choose its own rate-limit key. *Source:* plan §Reused from Tonse-hub, Do differently (line 96); baseline §2 row 10 [X273, D118, B82]. *Verified by:* request test: with the count set to 1, a spoofed extra entry does not change the counted address. *(built, not yet tested: `application.set('trust proxy', TRUSTED_PROXY_COUNT)` in `apps/api/src/configure-http-application.ts`; no test sends a spoofed extra `X-Forwarded-For` entry)*
- [ ] **S1-RATE-LIMITS-08** The baseline is 300 requests per client address per minute by default, configurable from 10 to 100,000 (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §5 [T156]. *Verified by:* configuration test of the default. *(built, not yet tested: `RATE_LIMIT_REQUESTS_PER_MINUTE` in `apps/api/src/config/app-config.ts` (default 300, 10 to 100,000); `request-security.test.ts` overrides it to 20)*
- [ ] **S1-RATE-LIMITS-09** Counters are counted atomically in fixed windows and store only a hash of the client address (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §5 [T160]. *Verified by:* key-value store inspection after a request: the key holds no raw address. *(built, not yet tested: one Lua script counts and starts the window (`INCREMENT_COUNTER_SCRIPT`), and keys are hashed through `DerivedKeys.hashIdentifier`, in `apps/api/src/common/rate-limiting/rate-limiter.ts`; the rate-limiter tests replace the hash with the raw value)*

### Health and key-value store

- [x] **S1-HEALTH-01** `GET /api/v1/health` checks both the database and the key-value store and answers 200 `{ "status": "ok" }` only when both answer. *Source:* plan §Step 1 Health (line 115) [D620, T101]. *Verified by:* `health-and-request-ids.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/health-and-request-ids.test.ts :: health and request ids > reports ok when the database and key-value store answer.
- [x] **S1-HEALTH-02** When the database is down, health answers 503 `SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE` in the generic envelope with a request id and no driver text, and logs the real cause privately. *Source:* plan §Step 1 Health (line 115); plan §Cross-cutting chokepoints "in the health check" (line 618); global rules §Fix it centrally [X113, D621, T103]. *Verified by:* API test with the database unreachable. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/health-when-database-is-down.test.ts :: health when the database is down > returns a generic 503 with a request id, and logs which dependency failed, with the stack.
- [x] **S1-HEALTH-03** When the key-value store (Valkey) is down, health answers 503 `SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE` in the generic envelope with a request id and no driver text such as `ECONNREFUSED`, and logs which dependency failed, with the stack and request id. *Source:* plan §Step 1 Health (line 115) [X114, D621, T102, T123]. *Verified by:* `health-when-key-value-store-is-down.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/health-when-key-value-store-is-down.test.ts :: health when the key-value store is down > returns a generic 503 with a request id, and logs the real cause privately.
- [ ] **S1-HEALTH-04** The key-value store connection fails fast while disconnected (no offline queue, one retry, 3-second connect timeout) and closes cleanly on shutdown (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §5 [T163]. *Verified by:* integration test with the store stopped: a request fails within the timeout. *(built, not yet tested: `enableOfflineQueue: false`, `maxRetriesPerRequest: 1`, `connectTimeout: 3_000` and `quit` on shutdown in `apps/api/src/infrastructure/key-value-store/key-value-store.module.ts`; the store-down tests replace the failing commands with stubs)*
- [ ] **S1-HEALTH-05** The API connects as `lookup_api` with a pool of at most 10 connections, a client-side statement timeout (default 5,000 milliseconds, 100 to 60,000 allowed) and the configured encryption mode (`disable`, `require`, or `verify-full`, which also checks the certificate) (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §5 [T164]. *Verified by:* configuration test of the pool size, timeout range and encryption modes. *(built, not yet tested: pool `max: 10` and `statement_timeout` in `apps/api/src/infrastructure/database/database.module.ts`, the timeout range and TLS modes in `apps/api/src/config/app-config.ts`, certificate checking in `infrastructure/database/data-source-options.ts`; `startup-checks.test.ts` ('refuses an unencrypted database connection') covers only production refusing `disable`)*

- [x] **S1-TESTS-05** The leak suite sends an oversize JSON body (over 100 KB) and checks for the 413 envelope. *Source:* plan §Cross-cutting ("oversize", line 632); baseline §2 row 8 [X191, T119]. *Verified by:* `error-responses.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > oversized JSON bodies get a 413.

### Phase 0 tools (built before Step 1; the engine core is what Step 3 wraps)

The product requirements for the engine (D9, G14 and the worker) are in the S3 section; the items below record the Phase 0 tools that `status-built.md` codes S1, so their tested behaviour stays covered.

- [x] **S1-PHASE-ZERO-01** The Phase 0 tools exist: the fingerprint engine, the benchmark, Docker Postgres, the design tokens and the capture app; the user confirmed the capture app records and plays back on their phone. *Source:* plan §Context (line 4); memory/look-up-project-decisions.md [X439, T337, T358]. *Verified by:* pytest suite for the engine and benchmark green; user confirmation recorded in the plan's Context. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_bench.py :: test_gate_passes_on_clean_corpus ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_matching.py :: test_phone_clip_matches_right_ad_and_offset. The capture app's part rests on the user's confirmation recorded in the plan's Context; its widget tests have no recorded run on the current commit (see S1-CAPTURE-APP).
### Fingerprint engine (Phase 0 core)

- [x] **S1-ENGINE-01** Phone AAC audio (16 kHz, 24 kbps, `.m4a`) decodes to mono 32-bit float samples at the canonical rate of 11,025 Hz, with the expected length (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §9 [T314]. *Verified by:* pytest. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_decode.py :: test_decodes_phone_aac_to_canonical_rate.
- [x] **S1-ENGINE-02** Bytes that are not audio are refused with a stable code (`DECODE_FAILED` or `UNSUPPORTED_FORMAT`) (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §9 [T315]. *Verified by:* pytest. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_decode.py :: test_garbage_bytes_are_rejected_with_a_code.
- [x] **S1-ENGINE-03** A decode error's text is only its code, never ffmpeg's internals (status-built.md only; global rules §Two layers). *Source:* status-built.md §9 [T316]. *Verified by:* pytest. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_decode.py :: test_error_text_is_only_the_code.
- [x] **S1-ENGINE-04** Audio larger than the byte cap is refused with `AUDIO_TOO_LARGE` (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §9 [T317]. *Verified by:* pytest. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_decode.py :: test_byte_cap.
- [x] **S1-ENGINE-05** Audio longer than the duration cap is refused with `AUDIO_TOO_LONG` (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §9 [T318]. *Verified by:* pytest. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_decode.py :: test_duration_cap.
- [x] **S1-ENGINE-06** Only whitelisted containers are decoded (MP4/M4A/3GP, Ogg, WebM, WAV, MP3, AAC, FLAC); others such as AIFF are refused with `UNSUPPORTED_FORMAT` (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §9 [T319]. *Verified by:* pytest. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_decode.py :: test_container_whitelist.
- [ ] **S1-ENGINE-07** Decoding also stops on a wall-clock deadline (`DECODE_TIMEOUT`), on a file with no audio stream (`NO_AUDIO_STREAM`) and on empty audio (`EMPTY_AUDIO`); only the first audio stream is read; default caps are 150 seconds and 25 MB, and a recognition query is capped at 20 seconds (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §9 [T320]. *Verified by:* pytest per code and cap. *(built, not yet tested: `services/fingerprinter/lookup_fingerprint/decode.py` (the three codes; 150-second and 25 MB defaults) and `lookup_fingerprint/matcher.py` (20-second query cap); `tests/test_decode.py` covers only the byte and duration caps, the container list and garbage bytes)*
- [x] **S1-ENGINE-08** Fingerprinting is deterministic: the same audio gives the same landmarks (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §9 [T321]. *Verified by:* pytest. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_matching.py :: test_fingerprint_is_deterministic.
- [x] **S1-ENGINE-09** An 8-second phone clip (16 kHz AAC, 15 dB signal-to-noise) is matched to the right ad and to its position within two frames, and the recognition reports timings for decode, fingerprint, lookup, alignment and total (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §9 [T322, T323]. *Verified by:* pytest. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_matching.py :: test_phone_clip_matches_right_ad_and_offset.
- [x] **S1-ENGINE-10** Every input is resampled to one canonical rate; skipping that step breaks matching (regression for the Dejavu sample-rate flaw). *Source:* status-built.md §9; plan §Verified facts, sample-rate trap (line 247) [T324]. *Verified by:* pytest. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_matching.py :: test_skipping_resampling_breaks_matching.
- [x] **S1-ENGINE-11** Audio that is not in the index never returns `MATCH` (10 unrelated clips). *Source:* status-built.md §9; plan G14 (negative set) [T325]. *Verified by:* pytest. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_matching.py :: test_unrelated_audio_is_rejected.
- [x] **S1-ENGINE-12** When two assets share the same audio, the answer is `AMBIGUOUS`, never a confident wrong brand. *Source:* status-built.md §9; plan G14 [T326]. *Verified by:* pytest. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_matching.py :: test_shared_audio_is_ambiguous_not_a_wrong_brand.
- [x] **S1-ENGINE-13** The decision rule: no candidate or too few aligned landmarks gives `NO_MATCH`; enough landmarks with no runner-up, or a margin of at least the minimum, gives `MATCH`; a smaller margin gives `AMBIGUOUS`. *Source:* status-built.md §9; plan D9 and G14 (top-2 margin) [T327]. *Verified by:* pytest (five cases). *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_matching.py :: test_decide_threshold_and_margin[<five cases>].
- [x] **S1-ENGINE-14** Duplicate-audio check: a re-encoded copy of an indexed ad links to that asset with high coverage; an unrelated ad has coverage under 0.1 (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §9 [T328]. *Verified by:* pytest. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_matching.py :: test_duplicate_check_links_reencoded_copy_only.
- [x] **S1-ENGINE-15** The in-memory index refuses to index the same asset twice (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §9 [T329]. *Verified by:* pytest. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_matching.py :: test_memory_index_rejects_double_add.
- [ ] **S1-ENGINE-16** The Postgres index returns exactly what the in-memory index returns (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §9 [T330]. *Verified by:* `services/fingerprinter/tests/test_postgres_index.py :: test_postgres_lookup_equals_memory_lookup`, which runs only when `LOOKUP_FINGERPRINT_TEST_DATABASE_URL` is set; whether it ran is not recorded. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-ENGINE-17** Re-ingesting an asset into the Postgres index replaces its rows (idempotent) (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §9 [T331]. *Verified by:* `services/fingerprinter/tests/test_postgres_index.py :: test_postgres_reingest_is_idempotent`, which skips without the database variable. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-ENGINE-18** Postgres lookups run under a per-query statement timeout, 800 milliseconds by default (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §9 [T332]. *Verified by:* pytest with a slow lookup. *(built, not yet tested: `SET LOCAL statement_timeout`, default 800 milliseconds, in `services/fingerprinter/lookup_fingerprint/index.py`)*
- [ ] **S1-ENGINE-19** Landmarks are packed exactly into a 64-bit integer (no truncated hash, no collisions); default parameters are 11,025 Hz, a 1,024-sample window, 50 % overlap, 5 peaks per frame, fan-out 5 and at most 200 frames between paired peaks; default thresholds are 20 aligned landmarks and a margin of 2. *Source:* status-built.md §9; plan D9 ("hashes are exact packed integers") [T333]. *Verified by:* pytest of the packing and of the defaults (tests use calibrated thresholds of 7 and 2). *(built, not yet tested: packing in `services/fingerprinter/lookup_fingerprint/fingerprint.py`, defaults in `lookup_fingerprint/config.py`)*

### Benchmark harness (Phase 0)

- [x] **S1-BENCHMARK-01** The benchmark gate passes (exit code 0) on a clean synthetic corpus: 8-second accuracy 100 %, no false positives, duplicates separable (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §10 [T337]. *Verified by:* pytest. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_bench.py :: test_gate_passes_on_clean_corpus.
- [x] **S1-BENCHMARK-02** The gate can fail: impossible thresholds exit with code 1 and mark the accuracy check FAIL in the report (global rules §Tests: a step that cannot fail is worth nothing). *Source:* status-built.md §10 [T338]. *Verified by:* pytest. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_bench.py :: test_gate_can_fail.
- [x] **S1-BENCHMARK-03** Synthetic distractor ads pad the index to production size without breaking matches; the report counts them, adds their seconds to the indexed total, shows no wrong matches and a throughput above zero (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §10 [T339]. *Verified by:* pytest. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_bench.py :: test_distractors_pad_the_index_without_breaking_matches.
- [x] **S1-BENCHMARK-04** The corpus manifest loader reports every problem at once: two references for one ad, a missing file, a query whose ad has no reference, an unknown role (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §10 [T340]. *Verified by:* pytest. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_bench.py :: test_manifest_reports_every_problem.
- [x] **S1-BENCHMARK-05** Field captures are cut into random clips per length, per take and per codec (AAC and WAV controls), labelled query or negative, written to disk, and re-running adds nothing (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §10 [T341]. *Verified by:* pytest. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_prepare.py :: test_prepare_cuts_encodes_and_is_rerunnable.
- [x] **S1-BENCHMARK-06** A capture-app session is merged into the corpus manifest: old headers are widened, old column names renamed, paths made relative to the manifest, and re-running adds nothing (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §10 [T342]. *Verified by:* pytest. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_import_captures.py :: test_import_merges_session_and_widens_old_header.
- [x] **S1-BENCHMARK-07** A clip that ran longer than its labelled length is cut back to it (and noted); a clip of the right length is left untouched (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §10 [T343]. *Verified by:* pytest. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_import_captures.py :: test_overlong_clip_is_cut_to_its_labelled_length.
- [x] **S1-BENCHMARK-08** A session folder outside the corpus folder is refused (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §10 [T344]. *Verified by:* pytest. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_import_captures.py :: test_session_outside_the_corpus_is_refused.
- [x] **S1-BENCHMARK-09** A session recorded by an older capture-app build imports into an old or a current corpus, and its old clip-length column still drives the trim (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §10 [T349]. *Verified by:* pytest. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_legacy_column_names.py :: test_import_session_reads_an_old_session[old corpus] ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_legacy_column_names.py :: test_import_session_reads_an_old_session[current corpus].
- [x] **S1-BENCHMARK-10** Old start and end columns still bound where the ad plays inside a capture (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §10 [T350]. *Verified by:* pytest. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_legacy_column_names.py :: test_prepare_honours_old_start_and_end_columns.
- [ ] **S1-BENCHMARK-11** The proposed exit criteria are encoded in the benchmark: 8-second accuracy at least 90 %, false positives at most 0.5 %, wrong-ad matches at most 0.5 %, 95th-percentile end-to-end time at most 1,000 milliseconds, 95th-percentile upload of an 8-second clip at most 32 KB (the pilot measurement itself is P8). *Source:* status-built.md §10; plan §Phases row 0 Verify (line 641); plan O1 [T351]. *Verified by:* pytest per criterion (only accuracy pass and fail are tested). *(built, not yet tested: the exit criteria in `services/fingerprinter/bench/run.py`; `tests/test_bench.py` checks only the accuracy pass and fail)*
- [ ] **S1-BENCHMARK-12** The benchmark report also gives per-stage latency (median and 95th percentile), upload sizes, accuracy by clip length, scene, device, codec and microphone setting, calibrated thresholds, the duplicate-detection cut-off, a parameter sweep and a Postgres-index option (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §10 [T352]. *Verified by:* pytest of the report sections. *(built, not yet tested: `summarize` and `render_markdown` in `services/fingerprinter/bench/run.py`)*
- [ ] **S1-BENCHMARK-13** A synthetic dry run at one virtual CPU with a 1,010-ad index measured 33 to 126 milliseconds end to end at the 95th percentile (target 1,000) and Postgres lookups of 17 to 34 milliseconds (status-built.md only; a documented run, not an automated test). *Source:* status-built.md §10; `spike/README.md` (30 September 2026 note) [T353]. *Verified by:* re-running the benchmark at one CPU and keeping the report. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S1-BENCHMARK-14** The fingerprinter container image runs Python 3.12 as a non-root user, with ffmpeg bundled in the audio library (no system packages) (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §10 [T354]. *Verified by:* `docker run … id -u` is not 0; the image has no ffmpeg system package. *(built, not yet tested: `services/fingerprinter/Dockerfile`: `python:3.12-slim`, `USER fingerprinter`, ffmpeg from the PyAV wheels)*

## S2 — Step 2 — Station side, basic (sign-in, clients, ads and upload, action cards, schedule, publish, audit, seed, portal: sign in, ads list, new ad, ad detail)

### Pinned stack (Step 2)

- [ ] **S2-STACK-01** `@nestjs/jwt` is pinned at 12.0.2 (design S2-D3 uses `jose` 6.2.12 instead — see conflicts). *Source:* plan §Pinned stack, API row (line 67) [D47]. *Verified by:* dependency check in `apps/api/package.json` and the lockfile. *Built differently:* tokens are signed and checked with `jose` 6.2.12 (design S2-D3 and S2-D21); `@nestjs/jwt` is not a dependency in apps/api/package.json or the lockfile.
- [x] **S2-STACK-02** Request bodies of the Step 2 endpoints are validated by the strict zod validation pipe (supersedes the Pinned stack's class-validator / class-transformer data-transfer classes, per baseline §2 and the built foundation). *Source:* plan §Pinned stack, API row (line 67); baseline §2 row 7 [D57]. *Verified by:* request-validation test on each Step 2 route: unknown fields and bad values get 400 `VALIDATION_FAILED`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: request-layer security > input validation > rejects unknown fields without echoing their names ; apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: request-layer security > input validation > names the bad field and a reason code, never the submitted value ; apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > clients > names must be 1 to 80 characters, and unknown fields are refused ; apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > changing where the station is > only the ten provinces, a city of 1 to 80 characters, and nothing else about the station ; apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > creating an ad and its upload URL > refuses an unknown client, a client of another station, files over 20 MiB and unsupported types, naming the field ; apps/api/test/ad-history-and-changes.test.ts :: ad history, rename and archive > renaming > refuses an empty or over-long title, an unknown field, an analyst and another station ; apps/api/test/action-card-fixtures.test.ts :: action card fixtures through PUT …/card > refuses %s with the expected fields and reasons ; apps/api/test/cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts :: buttons, schedules and publishing > schedules > refuses an end before the start with the rule named (no route test sends a bad sign-in body or a bad upload-address request, and most route tests check the 400 and the fields rather than the code).
- [ ] **S2-STACK-03** Passwords are hashed with the `argon2` package pinned at 0.45.1. *Source:* plan §Pinned stack, API row (line 67) [D58]. *Verified by:* dependency check; unit test: stored hashes start with `$argon2id$`. *(built, not yet tested: the 0.45.1 pin is in apps/api/package.json and the lockfile, which no test reads. That passwords are stored as argon2id with the agreed cost is proven by apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts, test "stores passwords as argon2id with 19 MiB, 2 passes and 1 lane")*
- [x] **S2-STACK-04** The action-card JSON Schema compiles in `ajv` strict mode (draft 2020-12) and agrees with the zod contract on every shared fixture. (Whether run-time validation follows the plan's ajv or design S2-D8's zod is the open item S2-STACK-12.) *Source:* plan §Pinned stack, API row (line 67); design S2-D8 [D59, T270]. *Verified by:* card validation test runs the shared JSON Schema through ajv. *Evidence:* packages/contracts/test/action-card-json-schema.test.ts :: action card JSON Schema (for the listener app) > (strict compilation runs before every case) ; packages/contracts/test/action-card-json-schema.test.ts :: action card JSON Schema (for the listener app) > agrees with zod that <each valid fixture> is valid.

### Local infrastructure (Step 2)

- [ ] **S2-INFRASTRUCTURE-01** `infra/docker-compose.yml` provides local S3-compatible object storage standing in for Cloudflare R2, with its API on host port 59000 and its console on 59001 (the plan names MinIO; design S2-D1, newer, uses versitygw v1.8.0 because MinIO images can no longer be pulled). *Source:* plan §Pinned stack, Local infra row (line 71); plan §Repo layout, infra (line 345); plan §Step 1 Monorepo (line 104); design S2-D1 [D86, D352, B71]. *Verified by:* compose file check; the upload round-trip test (S2-TESTS-05) reaches the store on 59000. *Built differently:* `infra/docker-compose.yml` runs versitygw v1.8.0 (design S2-D1) on 127.0.0.1:39000, below the Windows dynamic port range (design, Scripts and infrastructure), with no separate console port. The API's upload tests reach it, but no test checks the compose file.
- [ ] **S2-INFRASTRUCTURE-02** A one-shot bucket-initialisation step creates the bucket `lookup-ads` and leaves it private. *Source:* plan §Pinned stack, Local infra row (line 71) [D87, B71]. *Verified by:* after `docker compose up`, the bucket exists and an unsigned GET of an object in it is refused. *Built differently:* the bucket is `lookup-ad-uploads`, created with its CORS rule by `npm run object-storage:prepare` (which the API tests also run before they start, in apps/api/test/setup/prepare-test-database.ts) instead of a one-shot compose step; no test makes an unsigned GET to show it is private.
- [ ] **S2-INFRASTRUCTURE-03** The portal development server listens on port 5180, including for the slice walk-through. *Source:* plan §Pinned stack, Ports (line 73); plan §Verification at the end of the slice, step 2 (line 194) [D90, P190]. *Verified by:* Vite configuration check; Playwright's base URL uses 5180. *(built, not yet tested: apps/portal/vite.config.ts serves on 5180 and apps/portal/playwright.config.ts uses http://localhost:5180 as its base address, but no test asserts the port)*

### Sign-in

- [x] **S2-SIGN-IN-01** The API has a sign-in endpoint (plan: "login") under `/api/v1` that accepts a portal user's email and password; the seeded owner's credentials succeed and set the session cookies. *Source:* plan §Step 2 API modules, auth (line 134); plan §Phases row 1 (line 643) [P45, X483]. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > signing in > sets session cookies with exactly the safe attributes and returns the user and stations, never a token (a test user stands in for the seeded owner) ; apps/portal/e2e/sign-in.spec.ts :: signing in and out > the station owner signs in to the Overview, and signing out returns to Sign in (the seeded owner, in the browser).
- [x] **S2-SIGN-IN-02** Passwords are hashed with argon2id; the database refuses to store a password hash that is not argon2id in the standard format, so a bcrypt or plain value is never written. *Source:* baseline §3 row 1; plan §Data model, row portal_users (line 356); plan §Step 2 auth (line 134); blueprint §6 line 158 (kept) [X278, D400, B168, D58, T178]. *Verified by:* database test of stored hashes. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > privileges > stored hashes must be argon2id in the standard format.
- [x] **S2-SIGN-IN-03** Sign-in verifies the password against its argon2id hash: the right password signs in, a wrong one does not. *Source:* plan §Step 2 auth (line 134); baseline §3 [P46]. *Verified by:* API test with the seeded owner. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > signing in > sets session cookies with exactly the safe attributes and returns the user and stations, never a token ; apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > signing in > answers an unknown email, a wrong password and a disabled account identically, each for exactly one password hash.
- [ ] **S2-SIGN-IN-04** Long passwords are allowed: a password well beyond typical lengths (for example 100 characters) can be set and used to sign in, and it is never silently truncated. *Source:* baseline §3 row 1 [X279, P47]. *Verified by:* API test: a 100-character password signs in; the same password with its last character changed does not. *(built, not yet tested: the password policy accepts 15 to 128 characters (apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > password policy (NIST SP 800-63B revision 4) > counts characters, not bytes: 15 to 128 after NFC normalisation) and argon2 does not truncate, but no test signs in with a 100-character password and is then refused with its last character changed)*
- [x] **S2-SIGN-IN-05** Common and breached passwords are refused wherever a password is set, using a list bundled with the API; checking a password never calls an outside service. *Source:* baseline §3 row 1 [X280, P48]. *Verified by:* unit test with outbound network blocked: "password123" and other entries from the bundled list are refused. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > password policy (NIST SP 800-63B revision 4) > refuses common passwords, repeats and plain sequences (the list of over 3,000 passwords ships inside apps/api and the check is synchronous, so it cannot call out, but the test does not block the network) ; apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > changing the password > names the problem: a wrong current password, or a new password that breaks the policy (the same policy guards a password change) ; apps/api/test/development-seed.test.ts :: development seed > refuses a weak owner password and refuses to run in production (and the seeded owner's password).
- [x] **S2-SIGN-IN-06** Sign-in reaches the stored password hash only through SECURITY DEFINER functions; the API role cannot read the hash column (design S2-D4 is stricter than the baseline: the function returns only the hash settings and the comparison happens inside the database). *Source:* baseline §3 row 2 and §1 (column-level privileges) [X281, P49, B280, T179, T53]. *Verified by:* database tests: direct read denied; the function returns only settings. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > hash settings > returns the algorithm, parameters and salt, never the hash itself; email matches without regard to case ; apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts :: database privileges of the API role > portal users > cannot read password hashes, even its own.
- [x] **S2-SIGN-IN-07** An unknown email and a wrong password get exactly the same response: same status, same error code, same message (only the request id differs). *Source:* baseline §3 row 2 [X282, P50, B280]. *Verified by:* API test comparing the two responses (the database layer already gives both `INVALID_CREDENTIALS`, S2-SIGN-IN-15). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > signing in > answers an unknown email, a wrong password and a disabled account identically, each for exactly one password hash.
- [x] **S2-SIGN-IN-08** An unknown email and a wrong password take about the same time to answer (a password check is still performed for an unknown email). *Source:* baseline §3 row 2 [X283, P51, B280]. *Verified by:* API timing test: median response time over 20 attempts of each kind differs by less than the tolerance written in the test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > signing in > answers an unknown email, a wrong password and a disabled account identically, each for exactly one password hash (equal work is proved by counting the password hash computations, one on every path, rather than by timing the answers).
- [x] **S2-SIGN-IN-09** The hash-settings lookup returns only the algorithm, parameters and salt — never the hash itself — and matches the email without regard to case (status-built.md; design S2-D4). *Source:* status-built.md §6 [T179]. *Verified by:* database test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > hash settings > returns the algorithm, parameters and salt, never the hash itself; email matches without regard to case.
- [x] **S2-SIGN-IN-10** The hash-settings lookup returns nothing for an unknown email or a disabled account (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §6 [T180]. *Verified by:* database test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > hash settings > returns nothing for an unknown email or a disabled account.
- [x] **S2-SIGN-IN-11** A wrong password hash is refused as `INVALID_CREDENTIALS`, adds one to the account's consecutive failure count and is audited as `sign_in_failed` (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §6 [T181]. *Verified by:* database test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > signing in > a wrong hash is refused, counted and audited.
- [x] **S2-SIGN-IN-12** The right password hash returns `SIGNED_IN` with the user id and a live session, with the email in any letter case (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §6 [T182]. *Verified by:* database test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > signing in > the right hash opens a session with a refresh token, resets the counter and is audited.
- [x] **S2-SIGN-IN-13** A successful sign-in resets the failure count to zero and is audited as `signed_in` (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §6 [T184]. *Verified by:* database test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > signing in > the right hash opens a session with a refresh token, resets the counter and is audited.
- [x] **S2-SIGN-IN-14** Tonse-hub's JSON Web Token pattern is reused for the API's tokens: the signing secret is validated at start-up, expiry is short and the token is verified on every request. *Source:* plan §Verified facts (line 271) [D197]. *Verified by:* code review against Tonse-hub's token configuration; start-up test with a missing or short secret. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/startup-checks.test.ts :: production configuration fails closed > refuses a missing, short or placeholder authentication secret, in any environment ; apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > signing in > sets session cookies with exactly the safe attributes and returns the user and stations, never a token (the access cookie lives 900 seconds) ; apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > sessions > signing out ends the session at once: the same access cookie is refused on the next request (the token is checked against its live session on every request, design S2-D3).
- [x] **S2-SIGN-IN-15** A disabled account and an unknown email get the same refusal (`INVALID_CREDENTIALS`) from the database layer (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §6 [T185]. *Verified by:* database test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > signing in > a disabled account and an unknown email both give the same refusal.
- [x] **S2-SIGN-IN-16** A re-hash with new parameters is stored only after a successful comparison, never after a failed one (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §6 [T186]. *Verified by:* database test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > signing in > applies a re-hash with new parameters only after a successful comparison.
- [x] **S2-SIGN-IN-17** After 20 consecutive failures an account gets at most one attempt a minute (`THROTTLED`, even with the right password) until 60 seconds after the last failure (status-built.md; design S2-D7 database backstop). *Source:* status-built.md §6 [T187]. *Verified by:* database test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > signing in > after 20 straight failures an account gets at most one attempt a minute, even with the right password.
- [x] **S2-SIGN-IN-18** Password hashes are compared through SHA-256 digests of both strings, so comparison time does not depend on where they differ, and failed sign-ins never raise, so the failure count is kept (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §6 [T190]. *Verified by:* database test that a failed comparison commits its count; review of the comparison. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > signing in > a wrong hash is refused, counted and audited (the count is read back on a second connection, so it was committed, and the SHA-256 comparison was confirmed by reading migration 1791100000000-StationPortal).
- [x] **S2-SIGN-IN-19** Only `lookup_api` may call the seven sign-in functions (find hash settings, sign in, rotate refresh token, end session, find active session, change password, delete expired sessions); `lookup_admin_api` and `lookup_fingerprint_worker` cannot (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §6 [T175]. *Verified by:* database test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > privileges > only lookup_api may call the sign-in functions; the internal helpers are callable by nobody.
- [x] **S2-SIGN-IN-20** The internal helpers (issue a session, check the sign-in backstop) are callable by no application role (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §6 [T176]. *Verified by:* database test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > privileges > only lookup_api may call the sign-in functions; the internal helpers are callable by nobody.
- [x] **S2-SIGN-IN-21** Authentication routes have stricter rate limits than the baseline: the sign-in endpoint is limited per client address and answers 429 `RATE_LIMITED` with `Retry-After` well before the baseline limit would. *Source:* plan §Step 2 auth "throttling" (line 134); plan §Reused from Tonse-hub (line 87); baseline §3 row 3 [D101, X284, P52]. *Verified by:* API test: repeated sign-ins from one address hit 429 before a non-auth route would. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > limits on guessing > limits sign-in requests to 20 a minute per client address (the baseline default is 300 a minute) ; apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > limits on guessing > after 10 wrong passwords an account is refused with 429, and an unknown email behaves the same (RATE_LIMITED and Retry-After on the sign-in route).
- [x] **S2-SIGN-IN-22** The sign-in endpoint is also rate-limited per account, so attempts against one email from many addresses are limited too. *Source:* baseline §3 row 3 [X285, P53]. *Verified by:* API test: attempts for one email from different forwarded addresses are refused after the per-account limit. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > limits on guessing > after 10 wrong passwords an account is refused with 429, and an unknown email behaves the same (every attempt comes from a different forwarded address).
- [x] **S2-SIGN-IN-23** When the shared rate-limit counter store is unreachable, the sign-in endpoint refuses requests (503 in the envelope) and no password check runs. *Source:* baseline §3 row 3 and §2 row 6 [X287, P54]. *Verified by:* API test with the key-value store stopped. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/authentication-when-key-value-store-is-down.test.ts :: authentication when the key-value store is down > sign-in answers 503 with a request id and no internals (that no password check runs is not asserted, though the limit is counted before the sign-in work) ; apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: rate limiter when its store is unreachable > refuses requests for counters marked to refuse (e.g. sign-in).
- [x] **S2-SIGN-IN-24** A cookie-carrying write to the sign-in, refresh or sign-out endpoint from an origin that is not on the allowed list is refused, and the session stays valid. *Source:* baseline §2 row 5 [P71]. *Verified by:* API test: sign-out with valid cookies and `Origin: https://evil.example` is refused. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > cross-site requests > sign-in, refresh and sign-out from another site, or with no origin at all, are refused even without cookies (stricter than asked, but the session is not re-checked afterwards) ; apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: request-layer security > cross-site request forgery (cookie requests) > refuses a cookie-carrying write from another site, and logs why.

### Sessions

- [x] **S2-SESSIONS-01** O9: the portal signs in with httpOnly cookie sessions, not OAuth2 bearer tokens; the sign-in response body holds no token (supersedes blueprint §3.1 "OAuth2 with JWTs"). *Source:* plan O9 (line 664); plan §Step 2 auth (line 134); plan §Phases row 1 (line 643); blueprint §3.1 line 71 [B161, X615, P567, X483]. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: sign-in sets cookies and the body holds no token. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > signing in > sets session cookies with exactly the safe attributes and returns the user and stations, never a token.
- [x] **S2-SESSIONS-02** A successful sign-in sets an access cookie that expires after 15 minutes. *Source:* plan §Step 2 auth (line 134); baseline §3 row 4 [X288, B162, P55]. *Verified by:* API test: `Max-Age` 900 (or `Expires` 15 minutes ahead); with a fake clock the cookie is accepted at 14 minutes and refused (401) at 16. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > signing in > sets session cookies with exactly the safe attributes and returns the user and stations, never a token (checks Max-Age=900, with no fake-clock test of the token at 14 and 16 minutes).
- [x] **S2-SESSIONS-03** A successful sign-in sets a refresh cookie that expires after 7 days (604,800 seconds). *Source:* plan §Step 2 auth (line 134); baseline §3 row 4 [X289, P56, B163]. *Verified by:* API test of the cookie lifetime. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > signing in > sets session cookies with exactly the safe attributes and returns the user and stations, never a token (Max-Age between 604,700 and 604,800 seconds).
- [x] **S2-SESSIONS-04** A new refresh token expires 7 days after it is issued. *Source:* plan §Step 2 auth (line 134) [P56, T183]. *Verified by:* database test: the stored token's expiry is 7 days ahead. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > signing in > the right hash opens a session with a refresh token, resets the counter and is audited.
- [x] **S2-SESSIONS-05** Both session cookies are `HttpOnly`. *Source:* plan §Step 2 auth (line 134); plan §Reused from Tonse-hub (line 92); baseline §3 row 4 [P57, D110, X290, B166]. *Verified by:* API test: both `Set-Cookie` headers contain `HttpOnly`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > signing in > sets session cookies with exactly the safe attributes and returns the user and stations, never a token.
- [x] **S2-SESSIONS-06** Both session cookies are `Secure`. *Source:* baseline §3 row 4 [P58, X290, B166]. *Verified by:* API test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > signing in > sets session cookies with exactly the safe attributes and returns the user and stations, never a token.
- [x] **S2-SESSIONS-07** Both session cookies carry a `SameSite` attribute. *Source:* plan §Reused from Tonse-hub (line 92); baseline §3 row 4 [P59, D110, X290, B166]. *Verified by:* API test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > signing in > sets session cookies with exactly the safe attributes and returns the user and stations, never a token (SameSite=Strict).
- [x] **S2-SESSIONS-08** Both session cookies use the `__Host-` name prefix, with `Path=/` and no `Domain` attribute. *Source:* baseline §3 row 4 [P60, X290, B166]. *Verified by:* API test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > signing in > sets session cookies with exactly the safe attributes and returns the user and stations, never a token (the names are read from the response and must start with __Host-; Path=/ and no Domain on each).
- [ ] **S2-SESSIONS-09** Refresh tokens are stored only as hashes (32-byte), never in clear; the clear token exists only in the cookie. *Source:* plan §Reused from Tonse-hub, Do differently (line 92); plan §Step 2 auth "hashed refresh cookie" (line 134); baseline §3 row 5 [D108, X291, B164, P61, T195]. *Verified by:* database test: after sign-in no stored value equals the cookie value, and the stored value equals the hash of the cookie value. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S2-SESSIONS-10** The refresh endpoint rotates the refresh token on every use: it sets a new refresh cookie and a new access cookie, and the previous refresh token stops working. *Source:* plan §Step 2 auth "rotating" (line 134); plan §Do differently (line 92); baseline §3 row 5 [D109, X292, B163, P62]. *Verified by:* API test: refresh twice in a row with the first cookie; the first call succeeds, the second is refused (see S2-SESSIONS-24 for the built 30-second grace). *Built differently:* rotation is tested (apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > sessions > refresh replaces both cookies, and the new ones work), but a used refresh token keeps working for up to three more uses within 30 seconds (design S2-D3), so the previous token does not stop at once; this waits on the user's decision in S2-SESSIONS-24.
- [ ] **S2-SESSIONS-11** Presenting a refresh token that was already rotated revokes the whole token family, so the newer token from the same sign-in stops working too. *Source:* baseline §3 row 5 [X293, B165, P63]. *Verified by:* API test: sign in, refresh (token B), replay token A, then try token B; both are refused and the family is marked revoked (the built layer revokes only after the grace in S2-SESSIONS-24 — conflict noted). *Built differently:* replaying an old refresh token ends the whole session only after the 30-second, three-use grace of design S2-D3 (tested: apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > sessions > a refresh token used again after its grace (three extra uses) ends the session: every cookie stops working); this waits on the user's decision in S2-SESSIONS-24.
- [x] **S2-SESSIONS-12** An expired or revoked refresh token is refused with 401 in the error envelope. *Source:* plan §Step 2 auth (line 134); baseline §2 row 2 [P64]. *Verified by:* API test with a token past its expiry and one whose revocation time is set. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > sessions > signing out ends the session at once: the same access cookie is refused on the next request (a revoked refresh token gets 401) ; apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > refresh token rotation > an unknown, expired or disabled-user token is invalid (expiry is tested in the database, the API answers INVALID with the same 401, and the 401 bodies are not checked here).
- [x] **S2-SESSIONS-13** The sign-out endpoint (plan: "logout") revokes the current session's refresh token and clears both cookies. *Source:* plan §Step 2 auth (line 134); baseline §3 row 6 [P65, X294]. *Verified by:* API test: after sign-out the response clears both cookies and the old refresh token is refused. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > sessions > signing out ends the session at once: the same access cookie is refused on the next request.
- [x] **S2-SESSIONS-14** A password change revokes all of the user's sessions (every refresh token family), stores the new hash, records when it changed, opens a fresh session for the caller and is audited as `password_changed` (tagged P5A in `slice-plan-portal.md` line 44; baseline §3 marks it Step 2). *Source:* baseline §3 row 6 [X295, P44, T200]. *Verified by:* database test (API test with two sessions once the endpoint is committed). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > changing the password > ends every session of the user and opens a fresh one for the caller.
- [x] **S2-SESSIONS-15** The "me" endpoint returns the signed-in user's own account details and station role; without a valid access cookie it answers 401 with `code`, `message` and `request_id`. *Source:* plan §Step 2 auth (line 134) [P67]. *Verified by:* API test: with cookies → the seeded owner and role OWNER; without → 401. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > sessions > the access cookie opens /auth/me; without it the answer is a plain 401 (checks the user id and the code UNAUTHENTICATED, not the message or request id) ; apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > signing in > sets session cookies with exactly the safe attributes and returns the user and stations, never a token (the station role is asserted on this body, which /auth/me builds with the same function).
- [ ] **S2-SESSIONS-16** Every protected portal route validates the session's access cookie before the handler runs; a missing, expired or tampered cookie answers 401 in the error envelope (supersedes blueprint §3 "JWT Validation" at a gateway). *Source:* blueprint §3 line 29; plan §Step 2 auth; plan O9 [B86]. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test of the three cases on `GET /stations/me`. *(built, partly tested: a missing access cookie answers 401 (apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > who can reach a station > without signing in, station routes answer 401), and a signed-out or disabled session is refused at once, but no test sends an expired or tampered access token; the route is `GET /stations/{stationId}`, design S2-D10)*
- [x] **S2-SESSIONS-17** No response of the sign-in, refresh, sign-out or "me" endpoints contains a password hash, a refresh-token hash or any token in its body. *Source:* baseline §1 and §3; plan §Reused from Tonse-hub (line 92) [P68]. *Verified by:* API test scanning the four response bodies for hash prefixes and token values. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > signing in > sets session cookies with exactly the safe attributes and returns the user and stations, never a token (the body is asserted exactly and holds no cookie value) ; apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > sessions > refresh replaces both cookies, and the new ones work (204, no body) ; apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > sessions > signing out ends the session at once: the same access cookie is refused on the next request (204, no body, and /auth/me shares the sign-in body's builder but is not scanned).
- [ ] **S2-SESSIONS-18** Session tokens are never placed in `localStorage`, `sessionStorage`, a URL or a query string; the portal relies on the httpOnly cookies. *Source:* plan §Reused from Tonse-hub, Do differently (line 92); plan O9 (line 664) [D111, B167, P159]. *Verified by:* Playwright: after sign-in, both storages hold no token and no URL in the history contains one; a scan of portal code finds no token written to storage or a URL. *(built, not yet tested: the portal source never uses localStorage or sessionStorage and relies on the cookies, but no browser test or source scan checks it)*
- [x] **S2-SESSIONS-19** The request context carries the signed-in user's id, role and current station id (playing the part of the blueprint's "tenant_id and role permissions" in the token), and two concurrent requests never see each other's values. *Source:* plan §Roles and RLS, Context in NestJS (line 392); blueprint §3.1 line 71 [D588, B169]. *Verified by:* API test: a handler reading the context sees the session's user, role and station; `GET /stations/me` returns the member's station. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > who can reach a station > a member reads the station profile, with their role ; apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > clients > creating a client is audited with who, which station, the request id and when (the actor, station and request id come from the request context, and no test runs two requests at once).
- [x] **S2-SESSIONS-20** Only narrow SECURITY DEFINER functions create, rotate and revoke refresh tokens: the API role has no direct access to portal sessions or refresh tokens. *Source:* baseline §1 row 7 ("functions: Next") [X229, T174]. *Verified by:* `portal-sessions-database.test.ts` and `database-privileges.test.ts`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > privileges > the API role cannot read sessions or refresh tokens directly ; apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts :: database privileges of the API role > portal users > has no direct access to refresh tokens.
- [x] **S2-SESSIONS-21** A fresh refresh token rotates (`ROTATED`, same session), and the token it returns rotates too (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §6 [T191]. *Verified by:* database test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > refresh token rotation > a fresh token rotates, and the new token works too.
- [x] **S2-SESSIONS-22** A used refresh token presented after 31 seconds revokes the whole session (reason `REFRESH_TOKEN_REUSED`), and every token in it stops working (status-built.md only; the 31-second boundary follows from the grace in S2-SESSIONS-24, which awaits the user's confirmation). *Source:* status-built.md §6 [T193]. *Verified by:* database test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > refresh token rotation > a used token presented after 31 seconds revokes the whole session, and every token in it dies.
- [x] **S2-SESSIONS-23** An unknown refresh token, an expired one, or one belonging to a disabled user is `INVALID`, and a disabled user's session is no longer active (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §6 [T194]. *Verified by:* database test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > refresh token rotation > an unknown, expired or disabled-user token is invalid.
- [ ] **S2-SESSIONS-24** Within 30 seconds a used refresh token may be presented three times in all (lost responses, two tabs); a fourth use ends the session and is audited as `refresh_token_reused` (status-built.md; design S2-D3 — a grace the baseline does not mention; needs the user's acceptance). *Source:* status-built.md §6 [T192]. *Verified by:* database test. *Test that proves the built behaviour:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > refresh token rotation > within 30 seconds a used token may branch three times in all (lost responses, two tabs); a fourth use ends the session. *Awaiting user confirmation* of design S2-D3: the grace departs from baseline §3 ("reuse of an old token revokes the whole token family"), which S2-SESSIONS-11 states. Unticked in review round 1; tick it, record the acceptance date and amend baseline §3 only after the user accepts the grace.
- [x] **S2-SESSIONS-25** A user keeps at most 10 live sessions: the 11th sign-in ends the oldest with reason `SESSION_LIMIT_REACHED`, audited as `sessions_revoked_by_limit` (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §6 [T188]. *Verified by:* database test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > signing in > keeps at most 10 live sessions per user, ending the oldest.
- [ ] **S2-SESSIONS-26** A session lasts at most 30 days from sign-in, and no refresh token outlives its session (status-built.md; design S2-D3). *Source:* status-built.md §6 [T189]. *Verified by:* database test with a fake clock at day 30 and day 31. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [x] **S2-SESSIONS-27** Signing out by refresh token ends the session and its tokens, is audited as `signed_out` exactly once, and is safe to repeat (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §6 [T196]. *Verified by:* database test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > signing out > ends the session by its refresh token, is audited once, and is safe to repeat.
- [x] **S2-SESSIONS-28** Signing out by session id ends the session (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §6 [T197]. *Verified by:* database test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > signing out > ends the session by its id.
- [x] **S2-SESSIONS-29** A password change with a wrong current password is refused (`INVALID_CREDENTIALS`) and audited as `password_change_failed` (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §6 [T198]. *Verified by:* database test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > changing the password > refuses a wrong current password and counts it.
- [ ] **S2-SESSIONS-30** A failed password change also adds to the failure count, and the 20-failure backstop applies to password changes too (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §6 [T199]. *Verified by:* database test asserting the count (the existing test asserts only the audit row). *(built, not yet tested)*
- [x] **S2-SESSIONS-31** A password change needs a live session (`INVALID_SESSION` otherwise) (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §6 [T201]. *Verified by:* database test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > changing the password > needs a live session.
- [ ] **S2-SESSIONS-32** A clean-up function deletes sessions that expired or were revoked more than a day ago (status-built.md only; nothing schedules it yet). *Source:* status-built.md §6 [T202]. *Verified by:* database test of the function; job test once scheduled. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S2-SESSIONS-33** Session revocation reasons are `SIGNED_OUT`, `REFRESH_TOKEN_REUSED`, `PASSWORD_CHANGED`, `ACCOUNT_DISABLED` and `SESSION_LIMIT_REACHED`, and a revoked session always has both a time and a reason (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §6 [T203]. *Verified by:* database test of the check constraints. *(built, not yet tested)*

### Station access and authorisation

- [x] **S2-AUTHORIZATION-01** Role checks per station (`OWNER`, `MANAGER`, `ANALYST`) run in the API on top of row-level security on every station route. *Source:* baseline §3 row 7; plan §C heading (line 427) [X297, B121, P72]. *Verified by:* API test matrix: each Step 2 station route called as OWNER, MANAGER and ANALYST. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > clients > an analyst can list clients but not create them ; apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > who can see and change ads > an analyst can list and read ads but not create one or complete an upload ; apps/api/test/cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts :: buttons, schedules and publishing > who may change buttons, schedules and publishing > an analyst may read the ad but not change any of it ; apps/api/test/ad-history-and-changes.test.ts :: ad history, rename and archive > renaming > refuses an empty or over-long title, an unknown field, an analyst and another station ; apps/api/test/ad-history-and-changes.test.ts :: ad history, rename and archive > removing an ad > is for owners and managers; an analyst, another station and a station under review are refused ; apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > changing where the station is > a manager may, an analyst may not, and people from another station see nothing (a MANAGER changes the station profile and removes an ad; an ANALYST is refused every change).
- [x] **S2-AUTHORIZATION-02** `ANALYST` is read-only: an ANALYST calling any station write route (client create, ad create and complete, card, schedule, publish) gets 403 and nothing is written. *Source:* plan §C heading "ANALYST is read-only" (line 427); plan §Phases 5b Verify (line 648) [P73, X297]. *Verified by:* API test per write route. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > clients > an analyst can list clients but not create them ; apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > who can see and change ads > an analyst can list and read ads but not create one or complete an upload ; apps/api/test/cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts :: buttons, schedules and publishing > who may change buttons, schedules and publishing > an analyst may read the ad but not change any of it ; apps/api/test/ad-history-and-changes.test.ts :: ad history, rename and archive > removing an ad > is for owners and managers; an analyst, another station and a station under review are refused ; apps/api/test/ad-history-and-changes.test.ts :: ad history, rename and archive > renaming > refuses an empty or over-long title, an unknown field, an analyst and another station (the title is unchanged afterwards) ; apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > changing where the station is > a manager may, an analyst may not, and people from another station see nothing (that nothing was written is checked on the title and profile routes, and the guard refuses the others before the route runs).
- [x] **S2-AUTHORIZATION-03** A request for an id that belongs to another station answers 404, never 403, with the same body as a random id, so the id's existence is not revealed; a member of station A only cannot act in station B's context. *Source:* baseline §3 row 7; plan §Step 2 tests IDOR (line 146); blueprint §3 line 44 (kept) [X298, B96, B121, P74]. *Verified by:* API test: station B calls every `:id` route with station A's ad ids and gets 404 each time. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > who can reach a station > another station's id answers 404, exactly like a station that does not exist or an id that is not a UUID ; apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > who can see and change ads > another station gets 404 on every one of this station's ads ; apps/api/test/cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts :: buttons, schedules and publishing > who may change buttons, schedules and publishing > another station gets 404 on this station's ad, whatever it tries ; apps/api/test/ad-history-and-changes.test.ts :: ad history, rename and archive > history > is read-only for analysts, and another station's ad, an unknown ad or a station under review are refused (the identical body is compared for station ids, and the ad routes check the 404).
- [x] **S2-AUTHORIZATION-04** A station whose status is not `ACTIVE` can reach only its application-status and profile endpoints; every other station route refuses it with 403 (the plan's own check is in Phase 5a; placed here because the routes ship in Step 2). *Source:* plan §Roles and RLS, Guards (line 396); plan §Phases row 5a (line 647) [D594]. *Verified by:* API test: a member of a pending, rejected or suspended station gets 403 on client, ad, card, schedule and publish routes and 200 on the application-status and profile routes. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > who can reach a station > a %s station reaches its profile and none of its content (pending verification, under review, rejected and suspended: 403 STATION_NOT_ACTIVE on all 15 content routes, 200 on the profile, which is the application-status page until phase 5a) ; apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > changing where the station is > works while the station is under review, so a new station can say where it is.
- [x] **S2-AUTHORIZATION-05** Every portal request for station data runs inside `StationScopedTransaction`, under row-level security scoped to the signed-in user's station. *Source:* plan §Target architecture diagram (line 314) [D293]. *Verified by:* API test: every station route's queries run with the station setting present; station B's session gets 404 for station A's ids. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/source-scan.test.ts :: source scan > S2-AUTHORIZATION-08: feature code reaches the database only through StationScopedTransaction (no repositories, no direct data source) ; apps/api/test/step-two-route-failures.test.ts :: Step 2 routes when something underneath fails > a %s error inside the route > %s (every station route fails when its own StationScopedTransaction fails, so each runs inside one) ; apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > who can see and change ads > another station gets 404 on every one of this station's ads.
- [ ] **S2-AUTHORIZATION-06** The portal's station endpoints (and later the listener endpoints) connect as `lookup_api` and as no other role. *Source:* plan §Roles and RLS (line 379) [D574]. *Verified by:* integration test: `current_user` inside station requests is `lookup_api`. *(built, not yet tested: the API refuses to start as a superuser, a role that bypasses row-level security or a table owner (apps/api/test/startup-checks.test.ts :: startup refuses an unsafe database role > rejects a superuser connection (row-level security would not apply)), but no test reads `current_user` inside a station request)*
- [x] **S2-AUTHORIZATION-07** Station feature code gets its repositories only from the station-scoped transaction; it never uses a repository bound to the bare connection. *Source:* plan §Roles and RLS, Context in NestJS (line 394) [D592, B93]. *Verified by:* code review plus the lint rule of S2-AUTHORIZATION-08. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/source-scan.test.ts :: source scan > S2-AUTHORIZATION-08: feature code reaches the database only through StationScopedTransaction (no repositories, no direct data source) (authentication and health, which run before any station context, are the only exceptions).
- [x] **S2-AUTHORIZATION-08** A lint rule bans direct `@InjectRepository` in station feature modules, and the lint step is blocking. *Source:* plan §Roles and RLS, Context in NestJS (line 394) [D593, B93]. *Verified by:* adding `@InjectRepository` to a file inside `features/` makes the lint command fail. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/source-scan.test.ts :: source scan > S2-AUTHORIZATION-08: feature code reaches the database only through StationScopedTransaction (no repositories, no direct data source) (a source-scan test in the blocking test suite instead of an ESLint rule, design S2-D19).

### Stations

- [x] **S2-STATIONS-01** `GET /api/v1/stations/me` returns the signed-in user's station. *Source:* plan §Step 2 API modules, stations (line 135) [P79]. *Verified by:* API test: as the seeded owner it returns "Station A" with frequency "98.1 FM". *Evidence:* apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > who can reach a station > a member reads the station profile, with their role (built as GET /api/v1/stations/{stationId} because a person may belong to several stations, design S2-D10).
- [x] **S2-STATIONS-02** `GET /api/v1/stations/me` never returns another station's data and contains no internal review fields or secrets. *Source:* plan §Step 2 (line 135); baseline §1 [P80]. *Verified by:* API test with two stations: each user sees only their own station; the body has no review or status-internal fields. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > who can reach a station > a member reads the station profile, with their role (the body is asserted exactly, with no review fields) ; apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > who can reach a station > another station's id answers 404, exactly like a station that does not exist or an id that is not a UUID.

### Clients

- [x] **S2-CLIENTS-01** D3 and G5: advertisers are modelled as a station's clients — rows the station owns (`clients.station_id` NOT NULL, `ads.client_id` NOT NULL referencing a client of the same station, no free-text brand column) — not as tenants or account owners (supersedes blueprint §6 `media_items.artist_or_brand` and the `ADVERTISER` user role). *Source:* plan D3 (line 233); plan G5 (line 287) [D131, D228]. *Verified by:* schema test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > row-level security > S1-ISOLATION-15: every station-owned table has a mandatory station_id ; apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > composite keys (enforced even where row-level security is bypassed) > S1-ISOLATION-11: station A's ad → client cannot reference station B's row ; apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > names > S1-NAMING-05, S1-MIGRATIONS-10: the slice tables exist under their domain names; the blueprint names do not (that ads.client_id is NOT NULL and that ads has no brand column are not asserted).
- [x] **S2-CLIENTS-02** The clients module lists the signed-in station's clients. *Source:* plan §Step 2 API modules, clients (line 136) [P81]. *Verified by:* API test: the seeded owner lists exactly the two seeded clients; a second station's clients never appear. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > clients > lists alphabetically, ignoring case (the list is exactly the station's own clients) ; apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > clients > a member of another station can neither list nor add this station's clients.
- [x] **S2-CLIENTS-03** The clients module creates a client for the signed-in station; the station comes from the session, and a station id sent in the body is refused as an unknown field. *Source:* plan §Step 2 clients (line 136); baseline §2 row 7 [P82]. *Verified by:* API test: create succeeds without a station id; a body with `station_id` is refused with a validation error naming the field. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > clients > creating a client is audited with who, which station, the request id and when (the station comes from the path and is checked against the session's memberships, design S2-D10) ; apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > clients > names must be 1 to 80 characters, and unknown fields are refused (a station_id in the body is refused as unrecognized_keys without naming the field, so a sent name is never echoed).
- [x] **S2-CLIENTS-04** Creating a client whose name already exists at the station returns a clean error in the envelope, with no database or constraint text. *Source:* global rules §Two layers; plan §Cross-cutting, leak regression suite (line 632) [P83]. *Verified by:* API test: the second create has a catalogue `code` and `request_id` and matches none of the leak patterns. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > clients > a duplicate name is refused with a plain message (checks the 409, the message and the leak patterns, not the code or the request id).
- [x] **S2-CLIENTS-05** Creating a client writes an `audit_events` row with actor, station, entity "client", the new values and the request id. *Source:* plan §Step 2 Audit (line 140) [P84]. *Verified by:* API test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > clients > creating a client is audited with who, which station, the request id and when.

### Ads

- [x] **S2-ADS-01** D3: every ad is owned by exactly one station (`ads.station_id` NOT NULL, foreign key to `stations`); there is no advertiser-owned ad (supersedes blueprint §1 and §3.1, where advertisers were tenants managing their own ads). *Source:* plan D3 (line 233) [D129]. *Verified by:* schema test: no table links an ad to an advertiser account as owner. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > row-level security > S1-ISOLATION-15: every station-owned table has a mandatory station_id ; apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > composite keys (enforced even where row-level security is bypassed) > S1-ISOLATION-11: station A's ad → client cannot reference station B's row ; apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > names > S1-NAMING-05, S1-MIGRATIONS-10: the slice tables exist under their domain names; the blueprint names do not (no tenants or media_items table exists, and the foreign key from ads to stations is not asserted on its own).
- [x] **S2-ADS-02** Only a signed-in member of a station can create or upload an ad: `POST /ads` without a station member session answers 401 in the error envelope (supersedes blueprint §1 "Radio Stations / Advertisers … register audio ads"). *Source:* blueprint §1 line 9; plan D3 [B39]. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > who can reach a station > without signing in, station routes answer 401 ; apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > changing where the station is > needs signing in ; apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > who can see and change ads > another station gets 404 on every one of this station's ads (the sign-in guard covers every route, but creating an ad is not itself tried without a session).
- [ ] **S2-ADS-03** D3: no ad-creating or upload route exists for advertisers (client viewers); a client-viewer session gets 403 or 404 on each. *Source:* plan D3 (line 233) [D130]. *Verified by:* API route test (client-viewer sessions arrive in P5C; until then, a route inventory shows every ad route requires a station membership). *(built, not yet tested: there is no client-viewer role until phase 5C, every ad route sits under `/stations/{stationId}` behind the station membership guard (design S2-D10), and members of another station get 404 (apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > who can see and change ads > another station gets 404 on every one of this station's ads), but no test lists the routes)*
- [x] **S2-ADS-04** `POST /api/v1/ads` creates an ad as a draft (status `AWAITING_UPLOAD`) for one of the signed-in station's clients, owned by the caller's station. *Source:* plan §Step 2 ads (line 137); plan §Data model, ads (line 363); blueprint §3.1 line 55 ("Create") (kept) [P88, B122]. *Verified by:* API test: the response has the new ad id and status `AWAITING_UPLOAD`; the row's station is the caller's. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > creating an ad and its upload URL > signs exactly Content-Type and Content-Length for 10 minutes, with no checksum parameters, and audits it (the ad is AWAITING_UPLOAD and its upload key carries the caller's station id).
- [x] **S2-ADS-05** `POST /api/v1/ads` with a client id from another station answers 404 and creates nothing. *Source:* plan §Step 2 tests, IDOR (line 146); baseline §1 (composite foreign keys) [P89]. *Verified by:* API test: no `ads` row is added. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > creating an ad and its upload URL > refuses an unknown client, a client of another station, files over 20 MiB and unsupported types, naming the field (answers 400 with clientId unknown_client instead of 404, design S2-D16) ; apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > composite keys (enforced even where row-level security is bypassed) > S1-ISOLATION-11: station A's ad → client cannot reference station B's row (the database refuses an ad that points at another station's client, and the test does not count ad rows after the refusal).
- [x] **S2-ADS-06** After a successful completion check, the ad moves to `PROCESSING`. *Source:* plan §Step 2 ads (line 137); plan §Data model, ads status (line 363) [P100]. *Verified by:* API test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > completing an upload > accepts a real %s file, moves the ad to processing, audits it, and is safe to repeat.
- [ ] **S2-ADS-07** After a successful completion check, one `ingest` job is enqueued (deferral to S3 proposed by design S2-D12 — needs the user's acknowledgement before this item is deferred). *Source:* plan §Step 2 ads (line 137); plan §Phases row 2, Build (line 644) [P100, R361]. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: after `POST /ads/:id/complete` one job is on the `ingest` queue and the ad is `PROCESSING`. *Not built yet* (Step 3 adds the ingest queue and its worker, design S2-D12; the deferral awaits the user's acknowledgement).
- [x] **S2-ADS-08** `GET /api/v1/ads` lists only the signed-in station's ads. *Source:* plan §Step 2 ads (line 137); baseline §1 [P102]. *Verified by:* API test with two stations: each lists only its own. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > listing ads > pages 20 at a time, newest change first, with no ad missing or repeated (the pages hold exactly this station's ads).
