- [x] **S2-ADS-09** `GET /api/v1/ads/:id` returns one of the signed-in station's ads, including its processing status; another station's id answers 404. *Source:* plan §Step 2 ads (line 137) [P103]. *Verified by:* API test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > completing an upload > a file that is not really audio is refused, deleted from storage, and the ad marked FAILED with the reason (the ad's own page shows its status) ; apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > who can see and change ads > another station gets 404 on every one of this station's ads.
- [x] **S2-ADS-10** An ad's API response never shows exception text: a failed ad carries only an error code from the known list. *Source:* plan §Data model, ads (line 363); global rules §Two layers [P104]. *Verified by:* API test: a failed ad's response has a listed code only. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > completing an upload > a file that is not really audio is refused, deleted from storage, and the ad marked FAILED with the reason.
- [ ] **S2-ADS-11** A station's uploaded ad audio is kept in the private bucket after ingestion; only listener clips are discarded (supersedes blueprint §8 line 281 for reference recordings). *Source:* blueprint §8 line 281; plan G8; plan §Data model, row ads (`upload_object_key`) [B385]. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: after an ad is READY (Step 3) its object still exists and is not publicly readable. *Not built yet* (ingestion and the READY status arrive in Step 3, design S2-D12; until then a checked upload stays in the private bucket and plays back through a signed address, apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > playing an ad back > gives a short-lived URL once the upload is verified, and not before).
- [x] **S2-ADS-12** Creating an ad and completing its upload each write an `audit_events` row with actor, station, entity "ad", the change and the request id. *Source:* plan §Step 2 Audit (line 140) [P105]. *Verified by:* API test: two audit rows. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > creating an ad and its upload URL > signs exactly Content-Type and Content-Length for 10 minutes, with no checksum parameters, and audits it (checks the ad_created row and its changes) ; apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > completing an upload > accepts a real %s file, moves the ad to processing, audits it, and is safe to repeat (checks one ad_upload_verified row) ; apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > clients > creating a client is audited with who, which station, the request id and when (the one audit writer fills the actor, station and request id from the request context, checked here).
- [x] **S2-ADS-13** The API may move an ad from `AWAITING_UPLOAD` to `PROCESSING` or to `FAILED` (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §6 [T204]. *Verified by:* database test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: ad status guard > the API may move an ad from awaiting upload to processing or failed.
- [x] **S2-ADS-14** The API may never mark an ad `READY` or `NEEDS_REVIEW`; only the ingest worker may. *Source:* status-built.md §6; plan §Data model, row ads (line 363) (the ad's processing status is copied from its audio asset) [T205]. *Verified by:* database test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: ad status guard > the API may never mark an ad READY or NEEDS_REVIEW — only the ingest worker does.
- [x] **S2-ADS-15** A `FAILED` ad may go back to `AWAITING_UPLOAD` so the upload can start again (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §6 [T206]. *Verified by:* database test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: ad status guard > a failed upload may start again.
- [ ] **S2-ADS-16** Ads record their upload details: content type limited to `audio/mpeg`, `audio/wav` or `audio/mp4`; size from 1 byte to 20 MB (20,971,520 bytes); original file name of 1 to 255 characters; upload expiry, entity tag and upload time (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §6 [T207]. *Verified by:* database test of each check. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S2-ADS-17** Text lengths are enforced in the database: portal user full name 1 to 120, ad title 1 to 120, client name 1 to 80, campaign name 1 to 120 characters (status-built.md only; the sources give no limits — see gaps). *Source:* status-built.md §6 [T208]. *Verified by:* database test at each limit and one over. *(built, not yet tested)*

### Uploads

- [x] **S2-UPLOADS-01** `POST /api/v1/ads` returns a presigned PUT URL to the private object store for the ad's raw audio. *Source:* plan §Step 2 ads (line 137); blueprint §2 line 20 ("raw audio assets") (kept); baseline §4 row 1 [P90, B72, X305]. *Verified by:* API test: the URL accepts a PUT of a small valid audio file. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > creating an ad and its upload URL > signs exactly Content-Type and Content-Length for 10 minutes, with no checksum parameters, and audits it ; apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > what the storage itself refuses > accepts the exact file, and refuses a bigger file, a smaller file and another type.
- [x] **S2-UPLOADS-02** The presigned PUT URL is short-lived: a PUT after its expiry is refused by the store. *Source:* baseline §4 row 1 [P91, X305, B72]. *Verified by:* API test: the URL's expiry is within the configured short lifetime; a PUT after expiry is refused. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > creating an ad and its upload URL > signs exactly Content-Type and Content-Length for 10 minutes, with no checksum parameters, and audits it (the address lasts 600 seconds) ; apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > what the storage itself refuses > refuses an upload once its URL has expired.
- [x] **S2-UPLOADS-03** The presigned PUT fixes the allowed size and content type in its signature, so a larger file or another content type is refused by the store itself (size enforced up front, not after the file is stored and processed). *Source:* baseline §4 row 1; plan §Reused from Tonse-hub, Do differently (line 93) [X306, P92, D113]. *Verified by:* integration test against the local store: a PUT over the size limit and a PUT with a different `Content-Type` are both refused. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > what the storage itself refuses > accepts the exact file, and refuses a bigger file, a smaller file and another type.
- [x] **S2-UPLOADS-04** The upload object key is generated by the server and stored on the ad; nothing the user types (file name, title) becomes part of the key. *Source:* plan §Data model, row ads (line 363); baseline §4 row 1 [D450, X307, P93, B72]. *Verified by:* API test: an ad with a title containing `../` gets a key containing neither the title nor the file name. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > creating an ad and its upload URL > signs exactly Content-Type and Content-Length for 10 minutes, with no checksum parameters, and audits it (the key is ad-uploads, the station id, the ad id and a random id, with nothing typed) ; apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > creating an ad and its upload URL > keeps only a clean last path segment of the file name, for display.
- [ ] **S2-UPLOADS-05** The bucket that holds ad audio is private: an object cannot be read without a signed URL. *Source:* baseline §4 row 1; plan §Pinned stack, local infra "lookup-ads, private" (line 71) [P94, X305, B71, D87]. *Verified by:* integration test: an unsigned GET of an uploaded object is refused. *(built, not yet tested: the bucket is prepared private by `npm run object-storage:prepare` and the portal plays audio through 5-minute signed addresses, but no test makes an unsigned GET)*
- [ ] **S2-UPLOADS-06** Ad audio goes from the browser straight to object storage; the audio bytes never pass through the API, and no API route accepts ad audio in its body. *Source:* plan §Target architecture diagram (line 314); plan §Step 2 ads (line 137) [D294, P97]. *Verified by:* Playwright network log: the audio PUT goes to the object-store host; API route inventory. *(built, partly tested: the API hands out a signed storage address and the API tests upload straight to it, and the browser journey reaches only the portal and the storage host (apps/portal/e2e/new-ad-journey.spec.ts :: a station puts an ad on air: client, audio, buttons, schedule, review, publish), but no test pins the audio request to the storage host or lists the API routes)*
- [x] **S2-UPLOADS-07** Only MP3, WAV and M4A audio files are accepted for an ad. *Source:* plan §C New ad wizard step 2 (line 445); NewAdUpload wireframe footnote "MP3, WAV or M4A" [P95, B296]. *Verified by:* API test: MP3, WAV and M4A fixtures pass; a PDF and an OGG renamed to `.mp3` are refused at completion. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > completing an upload > accepts a real %s file, moves the ad to processing, audits it, and is safe to repeat ; apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > completing an upload > a file that is not really audio is refused, deleted from storage, and the ad marked FAILED with the reason (the refused file is HTML text named .mp3, not a PDF or an OGG file) ; apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > creating an ad and its upload URL > refuses an unknown client, a client of another station, files over 20 MiB and unsupported types, naming the field (another content type is refused when the upload is requested).
- [x] **S2-UPLOADS-08** An ad's audio file may be at most 20 MB. *Source:* plan §C New ad wizard step 2 (line 445); NewAdUpload wireframe footnote "up to 20 MB" [P96, B296]. *Verified by:* integration test: a 20 MB file uploads; a file 1 byte over is refused. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > creating an ad and its upload URL > refuses an unknown client, a client of another station, files over 20 MiB and unsupported types, naming the field (20 MiB and one byte is refused when the upload is requested, and no test uploads a full 20 MiB file) ; apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > what the storage itself refuses > accepts the exact file, and refuses a bigger file, a smaller file and another type (the store refuses any size other than the one signed).
- [x] **S2-UPLOADS-09** `POST /api/v1/ads/:id/complete` checks the uploaded object's first bytes (magic bytes) before anything else happens; the file name and declared content type are not trusted. *Source:* plan §Step 2 ads (line 137); plan §Do differently (line 93); baseline §4 row 2 [D112, X308, P98, B295]. *Verified by:* API test: a text file named `ad.mp3` with an audio content type is refused at `complete`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > completing an upload > a file that is not really audio is refused, deleted from storage, and the ad marked FAILED with the reason.
- [x] **S2-UPLOADS-10** `complete` also checks the uploaded object's size before processing. *Source:* baseline §4 row 2; plan §Do differently (line 93) [X309, D113, P98]. *Verified by:* API test: an object over the limit is refused at `complete`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > completing an upload > an object that differs from what was declared (written around the signed URL) is refused (any size other than the declared one is refused, and the declared size cannot be over the limit).
- [ ] **S2-UPLOADS-11** An object whose first bytes are not MP3, WAV or M4A, or whose size is over the limit, is never enqueued for fingerprinting. *Source:* plan §Step 2 ads (line 137); baseline §4 row 2 [P99, B295, D113]. *Verified by:* API test: after the refused completion the ingest queue is empty. *Not built yet* (nothing is enqueued in Step 2, design S2-D12; a refused file is deleted and its ad marked FAILED, apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > completing an upload > a file that is not really audio is refused, deleted from storage, and the ad marked FAILED with the reason).
- [ ] **S2-UPLOADS-12** Object storage is reached through one storage-driver interface (as in Tonse-hub's `modules/storage/*`), so the local store and Cloudflare R2 in production are interchangeable drivers chosen by configuration; no feature code calls a storage client library directly. *Source:* plan §Reused from Tonse-hub (line 85); blueprint §2 line 20 (kept); plan D2 [D99, B70]. *Verified by:* unit test with a fake driver; integration test with the local driver; code scan. *Built differently:* one S3-compatible `ObjectStorage` service (apps/api/src/infrastructure/object-storage/object-storage.ts) serves both stores, which differ only in their endpoint configuration (design S2-D1), instead of separate drivers; it is the only file that imports the AWS SDK, but no source scan checks that.

### Action cards

- [x] **S2-CARDS-01** `PUT /api/v1/ads/:id/card` validates the card against the one shared action-card contract in `packages/contracts` before storing it; an invalid card is never written (the plan names ajv against `action-card.schema.json`; design S2-D8 uses the zod contract that emits that schema — see conflicts). *Source:* plan §Step 2 cards (line 138); plan §Data model, row action_cards (line 361); plan G10 [P109, D428, B237]. *Verified by:* API test: a valid card is saved; an invalid card returns a validation error and no row is inserted. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/action-card-fixtures.test.ts :: action card fixtures through PUT …/card > stores %s and returns it unchanged ; apps/api/test/action-card-fixtures.test.ts :: action card fixtures through PUT …/card > refuses %s with the expected fields and reasons (the zod contract validates the card in the request pipe before the route runs, though card rows are not counted) ; apps/api/test/cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts :: buttons, schedules and publishing > buttons (action cards) > refuses a javascript: link or a local phone number, naming the field and the rule, never echoing the value.
- [x] **S2-CARDS-02** G10: the shared card contract refuses any field it does not define, on the card or on an action (`additionalProperties: false`) (supersedes blueprint §5 and §6 unvalidated `ui_schema_json`). *Source:* plan G10 (line 292); baseline §5 row 1 [D246, X317, P114, B237, T261, T262]. *Verified by:* contract fixtures; API test: a card with an extra `onclick` property is rejected. *Evidence:* packages/contracts/test/action-card-schema.test.ts :: action card schema > refuses unknown-field-on-action for the expected reason ; packages/contracts/test/action-card-schema.test.ts :: action card schema > refuses unknown-field-on-card for the expected reason.
- [x] **S2-CARDS-03** The action dictionary has exactly four types — web link, phone call, WhatsApp message and map location (blueprint `ACTION_LINK`, `ACTION_DIAL`, `ACTION_WHATSAPP`, `ACTION_MAP`; built contract `LINK`, `CALL`, `WHATSAPP`, `MAP`) — and any other type is refused when saving. *Source:* blueprint §5.1 lines 129–133 (kept); blueprint §1 line 9; design S2-D9 [B236, T214, T260]. *Verified by:* contract fixtures. *Evidence:* packages/contracts/test/action-card-schema.test.ts :: action card schema > accepts all-four-action-types ; packages/contracts/test/action-card-schema.test.ts :: action card schema > refuses unknown-action-type for the expected reason.
- [ ] **S2-CARDS-04** G10: only the link action carries a URL; a call, WhatsApp or map action with a `url` field is refused. *Source:* plan G10 (line 292); blueprint §5.1 line 130 (kept, tightened) [D247, B239]. *Verified by:* contract fixture per type with a `url` field (refused today by the unknown-field rule; no dedicated fixture). *(built, not yet tested)*
- [x] **S2-CARDS-05** G10: a link must be `https`; a plain `http` link is refused. *Source:* plan G10 (line 292); plan §Step 2 cards (line 138); baseline §5 row 1 [D248, X318, P110, B239, T229]. *Verified by:* contract fixture. *Evidence:* packages/contracts/test/action-card-schema.test.ts :: action card schema > refuses http-link for the expected reason.
- [x] **S2-CARDS-06** A card containing a `javascript:` link is refused. *Source:* plan G10 (line 292); plan §Step 2 tests (line 146); baseline §5 row 1 [D248, B240, P110, X318, T231]. *Verified by:* contract fixture (the API end-to-end test is S2-TESTS-03). *Evidence:* packages/contracts/test/action-card-schema.test.ts :: action card schema > refuses javascript-scheme-link for the expected reason.
- [x] **S2-CARDS-07** A card containing a `data:` link is refused (reasons `invalid_format` and `link_must_be_canonical_https`). *Source:* plan G10 (line 292) [D248, T227]. *Verified by:* contract fixture. *Evidence:* packages/contracts/test/action-card-schema.test.ts :: action card schema > refuses data-scheme-link for the expected reason.
- [ ] **S2-CARDS-08** A card with an `intent:` link, or any other scheme than `https`, is refused. *Source:* plan G10 (line 292) [D248]. *Verified by:* contract fixture for `intent:` (none exists yet; the canonical-https rule covers it). *(built, not yet tested)*
- [x] **S2-CARDS-09** Call and WhatsApp phone numbers must be stored in E.164 form: a local-form number, a `tel:` prefix and spaces are refused, and a +260 number must have exactly 9 digits after the country code. *Source:* plan G10 (line 292); plan §Step 2 cards (line 138); baseline §5 row 1; blueprint §5.1 line 131 [X319, P111, B242, D249, T248, T254, T255, T264]. *Verified by:* contract fixtures; API test: `+260971234567` accepted; `+26097`, `+260abc` and `tel:+260971234567` refused. *Evidence:* packages/contracts/test/action-card-schema.test.ts :: action card schema > refuses local-phone-number for the expected reason ; packages/contracts/test/action-card-schema.test.ts :: action card schema > refuses phone-tel-scheme for the expected reason ; packages/contracts/test/action-card-schema.test.ts :: action card schema > refuses phone-with-spaces for the expected reason ; packages/contracts/test/action-card-schema.test.ts :: action card schema > refuses zambian-number-ten-digits for the expected reason.
- [x] **S2-CARDS-10** Zambia (ZM) is the default region: the shared phone-number helper turns a Zambian number typed without a country code into `+260…` E.164 (the button builder then saves that form — S2-PORTAL-BUTTONS-03). *Source:* plan G10 (line 292); blueprint §5.1 line 131 [D249, B242, T305]. *Verified by:* phone-number helper tests. *Evidence:* packages/contracts/test/zambian-phone-numbers.test.ts :: phone numbers as stations type them > "<input>" becomes +260977123456.
- [x] **S2-CARDS-11** G10: the WhatsApp action carries its message as a plain text field and the map action carries latitude and longitude as numbers within range; neither carries a ready-made link (map: blueprint "destination coordinates", not a URL). *Source:* plan G10 (line 292); blueprint §5.1 lines 132–133 [D250, B245, B247, T214, T237, T238, T249]. *Verified by:* contract fixtures: text and coordinates pass; coordinates as text, latitude 91 and longitude 181 fail. *Evidence:* packages/contracts/test/action-card-schema.test.ts :: action card schema > accepts all-four-action-types ; packages/contracts/test/action-card-schema.test.ts :: action card schema > refuses latitude-as-string for the expected reason ; packages/contracts/test/action-card-schema.test.ts :: action card schema > refuses latitude-91 for the expected reason ; packages/contracts/test/action-card-schema.test.ts :: action card schema > refuses longitude-181 for the expected reason.
- [x] **S2-CARDS-12** A WhatsApp prefilled first message is at most 500 characters: 500 accepted, 501 refused. *Source:* plan §Mobile B, card content guardrails (line 580); blueprint §5.1 line 132 and §7 lines 268–271 [B245, P115, M217, T220, T256]. *Verified by:* contract fixtures (API test once the route exists). *Evidence:* packages/contracts/test/action-card-schema.test.ts :: action card schema > accepts prefilled-text-exactly-500 ; packages/contracts/test/action-card-schema.test.ts :: action card schema > refuses prefilled-text-501 for the expected reason.
- [x] **S2-CARDS-13** A button label is at most 32 characters (so it fits one line at 320 wide): 32 accepted, 33 refused. *Source:* plan §Step 2 cards (line 138); plan §Mobile B, card content guardrails (line 580); baseline §5 row 1; NewAdButtons wireframe "Label (max 32)" [X321, B254, P112, M214, T217, T232]. *Verified by:* contract fixtures; `Result320` wireframe check. *Evidence:* packages/contracts/test/action-card-schema.test.ts :: action card schema > accepts label-exactly-32-characters ; packages/contracts/test/action-card-schema.test.ts :: action card schema > refuses label-33-characters for the expected reason.
- [x] **S2-CARDS-14** A card holds at most 6 actions: 6 accepted, 7 refused. *Source:* plan §Step 2 cards (line 138); plan §Mobile B (line 580); baseline §5 row 1; NewAdButtons wireframe "4 of 6" [X320, B255, P113, M216, T223, T259]. *Verified by:* contract fixtures. *Evidence:* packages/contracts/test/action-card-schema.test.ts :: action card schema > accepts six-actions ; packages/contracts/test/action-card-schema.test.ts :: action card schema > refuses seven-actions for the expected reason.
- [x] **S2-CARDS-15** Each action has an `id`, a `type`, a `style` and a `label` (kept from blueprint §7 lines 246–249); a missing id or style, or an empty label, is refused. *Source:* blueprint §7 lines 245–272 (kept) [B360, T251, T252, T234]. *Verified by:* contract fixtures. *Evidence:* packages/contracts/test/action-card-schema.test.ts :: action card schema > refuses missing-id for the expected reason ; packages/contracts/test/action-card-schema.test.ts :: action card schema > refuses missing-style for the expected reason ; packages/contracts/test/action-card-schema.test.ts :: action card schema > refuses label-empty for the expected reason.
- [ ] **S2-CARDS-16** The card's layout (`VERTICAL_STACK`) is an enumerated field inside the validated card JSON, not a free table column (supersedes blueprint §6 `layout_type VARCHAR(50)`). *Source:* blueprint §6 line 192; plan §Recognition flow step 9 (`card{schema_version, layout, …}`) [B310, T265]. *Verified by:* schema test: `layout` is an enumerated field of the contract. *(built, not yet tested)*
- [ ] **S2-CARDS-17** The API refuses a card of 16 KB or more with a clean 4xx validation error, not a database error. *Source:* plan §Data model, row action_cards (line 361) [D430, P116]. *Verified by:* API test: a schema-valid card of 16 KB or more is refused with a catalogue error. *(built, not yet tested: the database refuses a card of 16 KB or more, in migration 1790960000000-Foundation, but no API test sends one)*
- [x] **S2-CARDS-18** Saving a card never changes an existing card row: each save creates a new immutable version, the first row stays unchanged and the campaign points at the newer one. *Source:* plan §Data model, row action_cards "Immutable versions" (line 361); baseline §1 row 6 [D425, P117]. *Verified by:* API test: after two saves there are two rows and the first is unchanged. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts :: buttons, schedules and publishing > buttons (action cards) > saves the card as a new version each time and shows the latest on the ad ; apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts :: database privileges of the API role > ads and action cards > action cards are immutable versions: no update, no delete ; apps/api/test/cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts :: buttons, schedules and publishing > publishing > a card saved while the ad is live reaches listeners at once (the live campaign points at the newer version).
- [x] **S2-CARDS-19** A rejected card's error names the field and the reason, never the value sent. *Source:* baseline §2 row 7 [P118]. *Verified by:* API test: a card with `javascript:alert(1)` is rejected and the body does not contain `alert(1)`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts :: buttons, schedules and publishing > buttons (action cards) > refuses a javascript: link or a local phone number, naming the field and the rule, never echoing the value.
- [x] **S2-CARDS-20** `PUT /api/v1/ads/:id/card` for another station's ad answers 404 and adds no card row. *Source:* plan §Step 2 tests, IDOR (line 146) [P119]. *Verified by:* API test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts :: buttons, schedules and publishing > who may change buttons, schedules and publishing > another station gets 404 on this station's ad, whatever it tries ; apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > row-level security (lookup_api) > S1-TESTS-13: station A's context cannot insert rows carrying station B's id (card rows are not counted after the 404, and the database refuses such a row).
- [x] **S2-CARDS-21** Saving a card writes an `audit_events` row whose change shows the fields changed (for example a WhatsApp number). *Source:* plan §Step 2 Audit (line 140); plan §C Ad detail History (line 454) [P120]. *Verified by:* API test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts :: buttons, schedules and publishing > buttons (action cards) > saves the card as a new version each time and shows the latest on the ad (the second save records only the changed phone number's path, never the number).
- [x] **S2-CARDS-22** A station can attach web link, phone call, WhatsApp and map buttons to an ad: a card holding one action of each type is accepted by `PUT /ads/:id/card` and round-trips unchanged. *Source:* blueprint §1 line 9 and §5.1 (kept); plan §Step 2 cards [B41]. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/action-card-fixtures.test.ts :: action card fixtures through PUT …/card > stores all-four-action-types and returns it unchanged.

### Schedule and publish

- [x] **S2-SCHEDULE-01** A station can pair each ad with a schedule: `PUT /api/v1/ads/:id/schedule` saves a start date, an end date, days of the week as ISO numbers (1 = Monday … 7 = Sunday) and one or more daily time windows, and `GET /ads/:id` returns them unchanged. *Source:* blueprint §1 line 9 and §3.1 line 55 ("schedule") (kept); plan §Step 2 campaigns (line 139); plan G6 (line 288); plan §Data model, row campaign_windows (line 365) [B40, B124, P124, D480]. *Verified by:* API test: days `[1,2,3,4,5]` with windows 07:00–09:00 and 17:00–19:00 are saved (two window rows) and read back unchanged. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts :: buttons, schedules and publishing > schedules > creates a draft campaign in the station time zone and replaces its windows when saved again (the saved schedule comes back from the PUT with its days sorted, and the window rows are not counted) ; apps/api/test/cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts :: buttons, schedules and publishing > publishing > goes live with the current card, shows as Live now inside its window, and is audited (the ads list shows the window).
- [x] **S2-SCHEDULE-02** Window times are local times in the station's time zone (default `Africa/Lusaka`), not UTC. *Source:* plan G6 (line 288); plan §Data model, stations timezone (line 355) [P125]. *Verified by:* API test: a 07:00 window for a Lusaka station is stored as local 07:00; database test: "live now" at 05:30 UTC (07:30 Lusaka) is true. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts :: buttons, schedules and publishing > schedules > creates a draft campaign in the station time zone and replaces its windows when saved again (the station's time zone is Africa/Lusaka and the dates are stored as Lusaka midnight) ; apps/api/test/campaign-time-windows-database.test.ts :: campaign status as a station sees it > an active campaign is live only inside a window, in the station time zone (Lusaka is UTC+2).
- [x] **S2-SCHEDULE-03** A window whose end is earlier than its start is accepted and means the window runs past midnight. *Source:* plan G6 (line 288); plan §Data model, campaign_windows (line 365) [P126]. *Verified by:* API test: 22:00–02:00 is accepted. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts :: buttons, schedules and publishing > schedules > creates a draft campaign in the station time zone and replaces its windows when saved again (a 22:00 to 02:00 window is accepted) ; packages/contracts/test/schedule-schemas.test.ts :: schedule input > accepts a normal schedule, including a window that runs past midnight.
- [x] **S2-SCHEDULE-04** A day number outside 1 to 7 is refused with a validation error naming the field. *Source:* plan G6 (line 288) [P127]. *Verified by:* API test: day 0 and day 8 are refused. *Evidence:* packages/contracts/test/schedule-schemas.test.ts :: schedule input > refuses a day that is not 1 to 7 (day 0 is refused with the field named, day 8 is not a case, and the API validates with the same schema).
- [x] **S2-SCHEDULE-05** `POST /api/v1/ads/:id/publish` makes the ad's campaign `ACTIVE`, so schedule then publish leaves an ACTIVE campaign. *Source:* plan §Step 2 campaigns (line 139); plan §Data model, campaigns (line 364); blueprint §3.1 line 55 [P128, B124]. *Verified by:* API test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts :: buttons, schedules and publishing > publishing > goes live with the current card, shows as Live now inside its window, and is audited.
- [ ] **S2-SCHEDULE-06** Publishing a second campaign for the same ad over an overlapping period is refused with a clean error (the database's one-live-campaign rule), with no constraint or SQL text in the response. *Source:* plan §Data model, campaigns GiST exclusion (line 364); global rules §Two layers [P129]. *Verified by:* API test: catalogue code and request id; leak patterns absent. *(built, not yet tested: publishing turns the database's one-live-campaign refusal into a plain 409 in apps/api/src/features/campaigns/campaigns.service.ts, but an ad has one campaign in Step 2, design S2-D23, so no API test can reach it)*
- [x] **S2-SCHEDULE-07** Schedule and publish calls for another station's ad answer 404. *Source:* plan §Step 2 tests, IDOR (line 146) [P130]. *Verified by:* API test on both routes. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts :: buttons, schedules and publishing > who may change buttons, schedules and publishing > another station gets 404 on this station's ad, whatever it tries.
- [x] **S2-SCHEDULE-08** Saving a schedule and publishing each write an `audit_events` row with entity "campaign", the change and the request id. *Source:* plan §Step 2 Audit (line 140) [P131]. *Verified by:* API test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts :: buttons, schedules and publishing > schedules > creates a draft campaign in the station time zone and replaces its windows when saved again (each schedule save is recorded against the campaign) ; apps/api/test/cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts :: buttons, schedules and publishing > publishing > goes live with the current card, shows as Live now inside its window, and is audited (the publish row has entity campaign, and the request id comes from the one audit writer, checked in the client test).

### Audit trail

- [x] **S2-AUDIT-01** G15: every write the portal can make (client create, ad create, upload completion, card save, schedule save, publish) adds exactly one row to `audit_events`; no write route skips it. *Source:* plan §Step 2 Audit (line 140); plan G15 (line 297); plan §Data model, row audit_events (line 374) [D559, D270, B335, P135]. *Verified by:* API test that loops over every write route and asserts one new audit row each. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > clients > creating a client is audited with who, which station, the request id and when ; apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > creating an ad and its upload URL > signs exactly Content-Type and Content-Length for 10 minutes, with no checksum parameters, and audits it ; apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > completing an upload > accepts a real %s file, moves the ad to processing, audits it, and is safe to repeat ; apps/api/test/cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts :: buttons, schedules and publishing > buttons (action cards) > saves the card as a new version each time and shows the latest on the ad ; apps/api/test/cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts :: buttons, schedules and publishing > schedules > creates a draft campaign in the station time zone and replaces its windows when saved again ; apps/api/test/cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts :: buttons, schedules and publishing > publishing > goes live with the current card, shows as Live now inside its window, and is audited (checked route by route rather than by one loop over every write route).
- [x] **S2-AUDIT-02** Each audit row written by the API names the signed-in actor, the station, the entity type and id, the change made (old and new values) and the request id, which equals the response's `X-Request-Id`. *Source:* plan §Data model, row audit_events (line 374); plan §C Ad detail History (line 454); blueprint §3.1 line 71 (the request context records the acting user) [D566, P136, B335, B169]. *Verified by:* API test: changing a WhatsApp number produces an audit row with all five parts. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > clients > creating a client is audited with who, which station, the request id and when ; apps/api/test/cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts :: buttons, schedules and publishing > buttons (action cards) > saves the card as a new version each time and shows the latest on the ad (the card-save row is checked for its entity and the changed field, a phone number on a Call button).
- [ ] **S2-AUDIT-03** No password, token or other secret ever appears in an audit row's recorded changes. *Source:* plan §Data model, row audit_events (line 374) [D566]. *Verified by:* API test: a password change records no password or hash. *(built, not yet tested: no test reads the audit row of a password change for a password or hash; card saves are checked to hold no phone number, apps/api/test/cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts :: buttons, schedules and publishing > buttons (action cards) > saves the card as a new version each time and shows the latest on the ad)*
- [ ] **S2-AUDIT-04** A refused write (validation error, 403, 404) leaves no audit row claiming the change happened. *Source:* plan §Data model, row audit_events "every portal write" (line 374) [P137]. *Verified by:* API test: a rejected card save adds no audit row. *(built, not yet tested: the audit row is written in the same transaction as the change, in apps/api/src/common/audit/audit-trail.ts, but no test counts audit rows after a refused write)*
- [x] **S2-AUDIT-05** Every sign-in and every session event (sign-in, refresh, detected token reuse and family revocation, sign-out) is written to the audit trail with actor, kind and request id. *Source:* baseline §3 row 6 [X296, P69]. *Verified by:* API test: one audit row per event (the database layer already audits all but refresh — S2-AUDIT-06). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > sessions > S2-AUDIT-05: each refresh is recorded in the audit trail with its request id ; apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > signing in > a wrong hash is refused, counted and audited ; apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > signing in > the right hash opens a session with a refresh token, resets the counter and is audited ; apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > refresh token rotation > within 30 seconds a used token may branch three times in all (lost responses, two tabs); a fourth use ends the session ; apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > signing out > ends the session by its refresh token, is audited once, and is safe to repeat (the request id is checked for refresh, and the database tests check the audit actions of the other events).
- [x] **S2-AUDIT-06** The sign-in functions audit `sign_in_failed`, `signed_in`, `refresh_token_reused`, `sessions_revoked_by_limit`, `signed_out`, `password_change_failed` and `password_changed` (status-built.md only). *Source:* status-built.md §6 [T181, T184, T188, T192, T196, T198, T200]. *Verified by:* database tests. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > signing in > a wrong hash is refused, counted and audited ; apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > signing out > ends the session by its refresh token, is audited once, and is safe to repeat ; apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > changing the password > ends every session of the user and opens a fresh one for the caller.

### Logging (Step 2)

- [x] **S2-LOGGING-01** Every log line about a signed-in request carries the actor as a hashed identifier (HMAC(pepper, id) prefix), never the raw user id or email; the error filter likewise never logs a raw user id. *Source:* global rules §Logging ("actor"; "Hash identifiers"); plan §Cross-cutting (line 624); plan §Verified facts, Tonse-hub filter (line 270); baseline §7 row 3 [X142, D195]. *Verified by:* log-capture test: a signed-in request and an error raised in one carry the hashed actor and not the user's id or email. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/log-privacy.test.ts :: logged errors never carry personal values > every line of a signed-in request names the actor by a keyed hash, never by id or email.
- [x] **S2-LOGGING-02** Sign-in and session log lines identify the user only by a hashed identifier, never by email or raw user id. *Source:* baseline §7 row 3 ("Next (sign-in)"); plan §Cross-cutting (line 624); global rules §Logging [P70, X341]. *Verified by:* log-capture test: sign in and sign out; no line contains the email or raw id, and each contains the hashed identifier. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > signing in > never puts the password or the email in a response or in the logs (a refused sign-in is logged with a hashed account) ; apps/api/test/log-privacy.test.ts :: logged errors never carry personal values > every line of a signed-in request names the actor by a keyed hash, never by id or email (sign-out lines are not checked separately).
- [ ] **S2-LOGGING-03** URLs that carry tokens or signatures (for example presigned upload and download URLs) are never logged raw. *Source:* plan §Do differently "never raw ids or URLs carrying tokens" (line 91) [X156]. *Verified by:* log-capture test: `POST /ads` leaves no `X-Amz-Signature` or equivalent query value in any line. *(built, not yet tested: request lines hold only the id, method and path (apps/api/test/log-privacy.test.ts :: logged errors never carry personal values > S1-LOGGING-15: by default a request line holds only id, method and path: no headers, no query string) and response bodies are not logged, but no test looks for a signature in the log after creating an ad)*
- [ ] **S2-LOGGING-04** The `ingest` job carries the request id of the completion request, so worker logs can be matched to it (deferral with S2-ADS-07 proposed by design S2-D12). *Source:* plan §Cross-cutting, correlation id (line 621); baseline §8 row 1 [P101]. *Verified by:* API test: the enqueued job data contains the request id echoed in the completion response. *Not built yet* (the ingest job arrives in Step 3 with S2-ADS-07, design S2-D12).

### Portal: errors and client-side boundaries

- [x] **S2-PORTAL-ERRORS-01** When an API call fails, the portal shows a plain message together with the request id from the error response, so a station user can quote it. *Source:* global rules §Correlation id; plan §C Ads list States "a clear error with the request id" (line 441) [X135, P173]. *Verified by:* component test: a mocked 500 with `request_id` shows the plain message and the id. *Evidence:* apps/portal/src/features/ads/ads-list-page.test.tsx :: Ads list > states > explains a failure in plain words with its reference, and tries again on request ; apps/portal/src/api/api-client.test.ts :: API client > turns the error envelope into an ApiError with the plain message, the fields and the request id ; apps/portal/src/features/ad-buttons/buttons-step.test.tsx :: The buttons step > saving > says a server failure in plain words with the reference, and keeps what was typed.
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-ERRORS-02** The portal never shows raw response text, exception names or stack traces to the user. *Source:* global rules §Two layers; plan §Cross-cutting (lines 612–614) [P174]. *Verified by:* component test: a mocked failure whose body contains `TypeError at …` shows only the portal's own message and the request id. *Evidence:* apps/portal/src/features/ad-review/review-step.test.tsx :: Review and publish > when the API does not agree > says a server failure in plain words, with no technical text ; apps/portal/src/app/portal-routes.test.tsx :: a page that fails to draw > reports the crash to the API and shows plain words, never the stack (a thrown TypeError; the page is checked for exception names, stack frames and file paths) ; apps/portal/src/source-scan.test.ts :: portal source scan > nothing in the portal shows an error's own message to a person: only the API's plain message or ours.
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-ERRORS-03** A failed processing status is shown in plain words (for example "Upload failed" with "The audio file could not be read"), never as a raw error code; an unknown code shows a generic sentence. *Source:* AdsDesktop wireframe File badge "Upload failed"; Dashboard wireframe "Needs attention" "The audio file could not be read" [P175]. *Verified by:* component test: each processing error code maps to a sentence. *Evidence:* apps/portal/src/plain-words/upload-words.test.ts :: why an upload was refused, in words > has its own sentence for every reason the API can give ; apps/portal/src/plain-words/upload-words.test.ts :: why an upload was refused, in words > says something plain for a reason it does not know, never the code itself ; apps/portal/src/features/ads/ad-detail-page.test.tsx :: Ad page > overview > explains a refused upload in words, and offers no player for audio that never arrived (the badge says "Upload refused").
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-ERRORS-04** The portal wraps its routes in a React `ErrorBoundary` for render errors, which shows a fallback screen. *Source:* plan §Cross-cutting, Portal (line 629); global rules §Cover every boundary [X172, P579]. *Verified by:* component test: a throwing component shows the fallback screen. *Evidence:* apps/portal/src/app/error-boundary.test.tsx :: the error boundary around the whole portal > shows plain words when something outside the pages fails to draw, and reports where it happened ; apps/portal/src/app/portal-routes.test.tsx :: a page that fails to draw > reports the crash to the API and shows plain words, never the stack (errors inside a page are caught by the router's error page, which shows the same screen).
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-ERRORS-05** The portal registers a `window.onerror` handler for uncaught errors. *Source:* plan §Cross-cutting, Portal (line 629); global rules §Cover every boundary [X173, P579]. *Verified by:* browser test: an error thrown in an event handler or a timeout reaches the handler. *Evidence:* apps/portal/src/app/error-reporting.test.ts :: client error reporting > reports errors nothing caught and promises nobody was waiting for (in jsdom: an error event dispatched on the window reaches the handler and is reported; not run in a real browser).
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-ERRORS-06** The portal registers an `unhandledrejection` handler for rejected promises. *Source:* plan §Cross-cutting, Portal (line 629); global rules §Cover every boundary [X174, P579]. *Verified by:* browser test: an unhandled rejected promise reaches the handler. *Evidence:* apps/portal/src/app/error-reporting.test.ts :: client error reporting > reports errors nothing caught and promises nobody was waiting for (in jsdom: an unhandledrejection event dispatched on the window reaches the handler and is reported; not run in a real browser).
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-ERRORS-07** The portal never tells the user that an error "has been logged" unless the error report was actually sent; `console.error` alone does not count as logging. *Source:* global rules §Cover every boundary [P580]. *Verified by:* component test of the fallback text with reporting off and on (portal reporting is S2-CLIENT-ERRORS-05; the built fallback says "We've been told about it" only once the API has accepted the report). *Evidence:* apps/portal/src/app/error-boundary.test.tsx :: the error boundary around the whole portal > says it could not tell the team, rather than that it did, when the report is refused ; apps/portal/src/app/portal-routes.test.tsx :: a page that fails to draw > does not say the team was told when the report could not be sent ; apps/portal/src/app/error-reporting.test.ts :: client error reporting > never throws when the report itself cannot be sent, and says it was not sent ; apps/portal/src/app/portal-routes.test.tsx :: a page that fails to draw > reports the crash to the API and shows plain words, never the stack (says "We've been told about it" once the API accepted the report).
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-ERRORS-08** Detailed error display in the portal (exception text, stack, component path) appears only in a development build behind a development-only flag that is off by default and off in production builds. *Source:* global rules §Cover every boundary ("Gate detailed error display behind a dev-only flag") [X106, P582]. *Verified by:* build test: the production bundle never renders the detailed view; a component test shows it only with the flag on. *Evidence:* apps/portal/src/source-scan.test.ts :: portal source scan > nothing in the portal shows an error's own message to a person: only the API's plain message or ours (the portal has no detailed error view in any build, development included, so none can reach production).

### Client error reports (moved from P6 in review round 1; conflict 23)

- [x] **S2-CLIENT-ERRORS-01** `POST /api/v1/client-errors` accepts reports validated against the shared client-error contract in `packages/contracts`; a report with unknown fields or oversized text gets a 400 error envelope. *Source:* plan §Cross-cutting, every boundary "All clients report to `POST /api/v1/client-errors`" (line 630); plan §Repo layout, packages/contracts (lines 341–342); moved from P6-ERRORREPORTING-10. *Verified by:* API test: a valid report is accepted (202); one with an unknown field or a 501-character message gets 400. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/client-errors.test.ts :: client error reports > refuses unknown fields and oversized text ; apps/api/test/client-errors.test.ts :: client error reports > accepts a report without signing in and logs it at error level, scrubbed, with the related request id.
- [x] **S2-CLIENT-ERRORS-02** `POST /api/v1/client-errors` is rate-limited per client address: requests over the limit get 429 `RATE_LIMITED` in the error envelope (built limit: 30 a minute). *Source:* plan §Cross-cutting (line 630, "rate-limited"); moved from P6-ERRORREPORTING-11. *Verified by:* API test: the 31st report in a minute from one address gets 429. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/client-errors.test.ts :: client error reports > limits reports to 30 a minute per client address.
- [x] **S2-CLIENT-ERRORS-03** `POST /api/v1/client-errors` scrubs personal data (emails, phone numbers, tokens, query strings; only the path of a location is kept) from what it receives before it is logged, and logs the report at error level with the related request id. *Source:* plan §Cross-cutting (line 630, "scrubbed"); global rules §Logging and §Correlation id; moved from P6-ERRORREPORTING-12. *Verified by:* API log-capture test: a report carrying an email, a phone number, a token and a query string is logged at error level, with its related request id, and without them. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/client-errors.test.ts :: client error reports > accepts a report without signing in and logs it at error level, scrubbed, with the related request id ; apps/api/test/client-errors.test.ts :: scrubbing > keeps only the path of a location.
- [x] **S2-CLIENT-ERRORS-04** The API has a `client-errors` feature module under `features/`, imported once in `app.module.ts`. *Source:* plan §Repo layout, apps/api (line 336); moved from P6-ERRORREPORTING-13. *Verified by:* structure check. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/client-errors.test.ts :: client error reports > accepts a report without signing in and logs it at error level, scrubbed, with the related request id (the route is served by the application built from app.module.ts; the module is apps/api/src/features/client-errors/).
- [x] **S2-CLIENT-ERRORS-05** The portal's error boundary (render errors) and its global handlers (`window.onerror` and `unhandledrejection`) send what they catch to `POST /api/v1/client-errors`, with the related request id where one exists, and reporting never throws. *Source:* plan §Cross-cutting, Portal (lines 629–630); global rules §Cover every boundary ("Client crashes must actually reach the developer"); moved from P6-ERRORREPORTING-14. *Verified by:* portal unit tests of the reporter, plus an API log-capture check that a portal render error and an unhandled rejection reach the server log with a request id. *Evidence:* apps/portal/src/app/error-reporting.test.ts :: client error reporting > reports errors nothing caught and promises nobody was waiting for ; apps/portal/src/app/error-reporting.test.ts :: client error reporting > sends reports the API contract accepts, trimmed to its limits, and says the API took it ; apps/portal/src/app/error-reporting.test.ts :: client error reporting > never throws when the report itself cannot be sent, and says it was not sent ; apps/portal/src/app/error-boundary.test.tsx :: the error boundary around the whole portal > shows plain words when something outside the pages fails to draw, and reports where it happened ; apps/portal/src/app/portal-routes.test.tsx :: a page that fails to draw > reports the crash to the API and shows plain words, never the stack ; apps/api/test/client-errors.test.ts :: client error reports > accepts a report without signing in and logs it at error level, scrubbed, with the related request id.
- [ ] **S2-CLIENT-ERRORS-06** Portal error reports made while the browser is offline are queued and sent once it is back online. *Source:* plan §Cross-cutting (line 630, "queued offline"); the offline half of P6-ERRORREPORTING-14. *Verified by:* browser test: a report raised with the network off is sent after the network returns. Not built: the built reporter sends once and drops the report on failure.
- [x] **S2-CLIENT-ERRORS-07** `POST /api/v1/client-errors` needs no sign-in and no install token, so a crash before sign-in or before install registration can still be reported; it is protected by its per-address limit (S2-CLIENT-ERRORS-02) and, for cookie-carrying requests, by the cross-site check. *Source:* plan §Cross-cutting (line 630, "All clients report"); built behaviour in `8c69b85`. *Verified by:* API test: a report with no cookie and no token is accepted; a cookie-carrying report from another site is refused. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/client-errors.test.ts :: client error reports > accepts a report without signing in and logs it at error level, scrubbed, with the related request id ; apps/api/test/client-errors.test.ts :: client error reports > a cookie-carrying report from another site is refused (cross-site check).

- [x] **S2-PORTAL-SHELL-01** The portal is a static site (built files only, no server of its own) that talks only to the API and the object store. *Source:* plan §Target architecture diagram (line 314); plan §Render topology (line 325) [D292]. *Verified by:* build check: `vite build` output is static files; a network log shows no request to any other host. *Evidence:* apps/portal/e2e/new-ad-journey.spec.ts :: a station puts an ad on air: client, audio, buttons, schedule, review, publish (its last step checks that every request went to the portal's own address or the file storage, and nowhere else; vite build emits only static files and the portal has no server of its own).

Known differences are listed in their own items (SHELL-02, SCHEDULE-03, SCHEDULE-04, BUTTONS-05, BUTTONS-11).

### Portal: New ad flow

- [x] **S2-PORTAL-NEW-AD-01** The New ad flow runs in this order: choose the client → upload the audio with a progress bar → set the buttons with a live phone preview at 320 and 411 → set the schedule with day chips and times → Publish. *Source:* plan §Step 2 screens, New ad (line 144) [P164]. *Verified by:* Playwright journey at 375 and 1280 px walks the five steps in order. *Evidence:* apps/portal/e2e/new-ad-journey.spec.ts :: a station puts an ad on air: client, audio, buttons, schedule, review, publish ; apps/portal/src/features/ad-buttons/buttons-step.test.tsx :: The buttons step > on a phone > can show the preview at the two phone widths listeners have.
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-NEW-AD-02** The client step lets the user pick one of the station's existing clients or create a new one without leaving the flow. *Source:* plan §C New ad wizard step 1 (line 444); plan §Step 2 clients list and create (line 136) [P165]. *Verified by:* Playwright: create a client inside the step and see it selected. *Evidence:* apps/portal/src/features/new-ad/ad-setup-page.test.tsx :: Setting up an ad > step 1: who the ad is for > adds a new client right there and chooses it (a component test, not Playwright).
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-NEW-AD-03** The upload step shows a progress bar while the file is being sent. *Source:* plan §Step 2 screens "upload with progress" (line 144); plan §C step 2 (line 445) [P166]. *Verified by:* component test with a mocked upload reporting 0 %, 50 % and 100 %. *Evidence:* apps/portal/src/features/new-ad/ad-setup-page.test.tsx :: Setting up an ad > what the review found (each was a real fault) > shows a replacement for a refused file going up: progress, Stop, and Next once it is in ; apps/portal/src/features/new-ad/ad-upload-store.test.ts :: the upload store > sends the file to the signed address with exactly the signed type, then asks the API to check it (0 %, 40 % and 99 % before the check).
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-NEW-AD-04** The upload step shows the three stages "Uploaded", "Preparing it for recognition…" and "Ready for listeners", marking the current one with `aria-current="step"`. *Source:* NewAdUpload wireframe "Upload progress" list; plan §C step 2 "processing indicator" (line 445) [P167]. *Verified by:* component test for each ad status. *Evidence:* apps/portal/src/features/new-ad/ad-setup-page.test.tsx :: Setting up an ad > what the review found (each was a real fault) > shows a replacement for a refused file going up: progress, Stop, and Next once it is in ; apps/portal/src/features/new-ad/ad-setup-page.test.tsx :: Setting up an ad > step 2: the audio > checks the file, names the ad from it, creates the draft and uploads, showing each stage ; apps/portal/e2e/new-ad-journey.spec.ts :: a station puts an ad on air: client, audio, buttons, schedule, review, publish (built stages: "Uploaded", "Checked", then "Ready for listeners", which reads "Not prepared for recognition yet" until recognition is switched on in Step 3, S2-D26; the current stage has aria-current="step").
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-NEW-AD-05** The buttons step shows a live phone preview, drawn from the same shared schema and tokens as the app, that updates as the user types and can switch between 320 and 411 widths. *Source:* plan §Step 2 screens (line 144); plan §Mobile D, Card builder (line 603); NewAdButtons wireframe "Phone preview" [P168, B257]. *Verified by:* component test: typing a label updates the preview at both widths; Playwright: editing a label updates both previews. *Evidence:* apps/portal/src/features/ad-buttons/buttons-step.test.tsx :: The buttons step > adding and changing a button > leaves a button with a problem out of the preview, saying which, until it is fixed ; apps/portal/src/features/ad-buttons/buttons-step.test.tsx :: The buttons step > on a phone > can show the preview at the two phone widths listeners have ; apps/portal/src/components/listener-card-preview.test.tsx :: ListenerCardPreview > shows who the ad is for, its title, and each button in order ; apps/portal/e2e/new-ad-journey.spec.ts :: a station puts an ad on air: client, audio, buttons, schedule, review, publish (the four labels typed appear in the preview).
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-NEW-AD-06** The schedule step uses day chips and start and end time fields. *Source:* plan §Step 2 screens "day chips and times" (line 144); NewAdScheduleMobile wireframe [P169]. *Verified by:* component test: toggling chips and editing times produces the expected schedule request. *Evidence:* apps/portal/src/features/ad-schedule/schedule-step.test.tsx :: The schedule step > the list of times > chooses days one at a time or from the shortcuts, and says what is chosen ; apps/portal/src/features/ad-schedule/schedule-step.test.tsx :: The schedule step > saving > saves exactly what was chosen, and goes back to Drafts.
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-NEW-AD-07** The last step publishes the ad by calling the publish endpoint. *Source:* plan §Step 2 screens "→ Publish" (line 144) [P170]. *Verified by:* Playwright: after Publish, the ad's campaign status is `ACTIVE`. *Evidence:* apps/portal/e2e/new-ad-journey.spec.ts :: a station puts an ad on air: client, audio, buttons, schedule, review, publish (after Publish the ad shows as Scheduled or On air now, which only an ACTIVE campaign does) ; apps/portal/src/features/ad-review/review-step.test.tsx :: Review and publish > an ad that is ready > publishes, says so, and shows the ad.
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-NEW-AD-08** On a phone the flow's Back and forward buttons sit in a bar fixed to the bottom of the screen, at least 52 px tall. *Source:* plan §Mobile D, Shell (line 597); NewAdUpload and NewAdScheduleMobile wireframes, footer [P388]. *Verified by:* Playwright at 375 px: the bar stays visible while the step content scrolls. *Evidence:* apps/portal/e2e/new-ad-journey.spec.ts :: a station puts an ad on air: client, audio, buttons, schedule, review, publish (on the phone, at the buttons, schedule and review steps: the action bar is at least 52 px tall and inside the screen with the page scrolled).
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-NEW-AD-09** Below the `lg` width the flow's Save or Publish control sits in a sticky action bar at the bottom of the screen. *Source:* plan §Mobile D, Shell "Save/Publish sits in a sticky bottom action bar on mobile" (line 597) [P510]. *Verified by:* Playwright at 375 px: the Publish button stays on screen while the review content scrolls. *Evidence:* apps/portal/e2e/new-ad-journey.spec.ts :: a station puts an ad on air: client, audio, buttons, schedule, review, publish (on the phone the review's action bar, which holds Publish, stays inside the screen).
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-NEW-AD-10** The user can play back the audio they uploaded before publishing it. *Source:* memory/tools-must-be-usable.md ("a way to review what was made"); NewAdUpload wireframe "Play the upload"; AdDetail wireframe "Play the ad" [P594]. *Verified by:* Playwright: after upload, the play control plays the uploaded file. *Evidence:* apps/portal/e2e/new-ad-journey.spec.ts :: a station puts an ad on air: client, audio, buttons, schedule, review, publish (the review plays the uploaded audio from storage and pauses it before Publish).
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-NEW-AD-11** Slice walk-through step 3.3: in the portal the user sets Call, WhatsApp and Map buttons, schedules the ad for now, and publishes it. *Source:* plan §Verification at the end of the slice 3.3 (line 198) [X669]. *Verified by:* Playwright step plus a database check of the published campaign. *Evidence:* apps/portal/e2e/new-ad-journey.spec.ts :: a station puts an ad on air: client, audio, buttons, schedule, review, publish (Call, WhatsApp, Directions and Website buttons; times from today; published; the campaign's state is read back through the ads list rather than straight from the database).

### Portal: button builder (New ad, buttons step)

- [x] **S2-PORTAL-BUTTONS-01** Each button row has a type select with "Call", "WhatsApp", "Directions" and "Website", a label field, the fields its type needs ("Goes to") and a "Remove button N" control. *Source:* NewAdButtons wireframe rows and headers "Type", "Label (max 32)", "Goes to"; blueprint §5.1 [P403]. *Verified by:* component test of one row per type. *Evidence:* apps/portal/src/features/ad-buttons/buttons-step.test.tsx :: The buttons step > adding and changing a button > keeps a label the person wrote when the type changes, and follows the type when the label was the starting one ; apps/portal/src/features/ad-buttons/buttons-step.test.tsx :: The buttons step > the order and the number of buttons > removes a button and puts the cursor back at the top of the list ; apps/portal/src/app/accessibility.test.tsx :: accessibility: what axe finds on each screen > the buttons step, with every kind of button and problems showing.
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-BUTTONS-02** The four button types map to the card's action types: Call → dial (`ACTION_DIAL` / `CALL`), WhatsApp → WhatsApp, Directions → map, Website → link. *Source:* blueprint §5.1; plan G10 (line 292); NewAdButtons wireframe type select [P404]. *Verified by:* unit test of the builder's output for each type against the shared contract. *Evidence:* apps/portal/src/features/ad-buttons/validate-button-drafts.test.ts :: turning typed buttons into a card > a good set of buttons > makes a card from what was typed: numbers, links and places tidied, labels trimmed, optional parts left out when empty ; apps/portal/src/features/ad-buttons/validate-button-drafts.test.ts :: turning typed buttons into a card > a good set of buttons > only ever makes a card that the contracts accept, which is what the API and the app accept.
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-BUTTONS-03** A Call button asks for a phone number (example "+260 97 [000 0000]") and the saved card holds it in E.164. *Source:* NewAdButtons wireframe button 1 [P405]. *Verified by:* component test. *Evidence:* apps/portal/src/features/ad-buttons/validate-button-drafts.test.ts :: turning typed buttons into a card > a good set of buttons > makes a card from what was typed: numbers, links and places tidied, labels trimmed, optional parts left out when empty ; apps/portal/src/features/ad-buttons/buttons-step.test.tsx :: The buttons step > saving > saves a card made from what was typed, with each button's own id, and goes back to Drafts.
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-BUTTONS-04** A WhatsApp button asks for a WhatsApp number and an optional first message (example "Hi Brand A, I heard your ad on Station A…"), saved as plain text, not as a link. *Source:* NewAdButtons wireframe button 2; blueprint §7 `prefilled_text` [P406]. *Verified by:* component test. *Evidence:* apps/portal/src/features/ad-buttons/buttons-step.test.tsx :: The buttons step > adding and changing a button > lets a WhatsApp first message run to several lines and counts it ; apps/portal/src/features/ad-buttons/validate-button-drafts.test.ts :: turning typed buttons into a card > a good set of buttons > keeps the first message and place name when they are given.
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-BUTTONS-05** A Directions button sets its destination with a "Pin on map" control that shows when a location is set, and stores latitude and longitude. *Source:* NewAdButtons wireframe button 3; plan G10 "lat/lng" (line 292); blueprint §5.1 `ACTION_MAP` [P407]. *Verified by:* component test: placing a pin stores two numbers within valid ranges. *Evidence:* apps/portal/src/features/ad-buttons/buttons-step.test.tsx :: The buttons step > adding and changing a button > reads a Directions location from a pasted Google Maps link and offers a way to check it ; apps/portal/src/features/ad-buttons/parse-map-location.test.ts :: reading a location for a Directions button > what is refused > accepts the edges of the earth and refuses anything just past them (built as a Location field with a "Check on Google Maps" link instead of a map pin, plan decision 2 and S2-D30).
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-BUTTONS-06** A Website button asks for a web address; one that does not start with `https://` is marked invalid with the message "Web links must start with https://". *Source:* NewAdButtons wireframe button 4 error; plan G10 (line 292) [P408]. *Verified by:* component test: `www.brand-a.example/book` shows the message; `https://brand-a.example/book` does not. *Evidence:* apps/portal/src/features/ad-buttons/read-web-address.test.ts :: reading a web address for a button > refuses an address that is not secure, and says so ; apps/portal/e2e/new-ad-journey.spec.ts :: a station puts an ad on air: client, audio, buttons, schedule, review, publish (http:// is refused with "Addresses must start with https://"; an address typed without a scheme, like brand.co.zm/book, is made https rather than refused, S2-D31).
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-BUTTONS-07** An invalid field is marked `aria-invalid="true"`, tied to its message with `aria-describedby`, and the message is announced (`role="alert"`). *Source:* NewAdButtons wireframe button 4 input and message [P409]. *Verified by:* component test of the attributes; axe pass. *Evidence:* apps/portal/src/features/ad-buttons/buttons-step.test.tsx :: The buttons step > fields a screen reader can tell apart > marks a field with a problem as invalid and ties it to the message, which is announced ; apps/portal/src/app/accessibility.test.tsx :: accessibility: what axe finds on each screen > the buttons step, with every kind of button and problems showing.
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-BUTTONS-08** A button whose target is invalid is left out of the phone preview, which shows a dashed note in its place: "\"<label>\" is hidden until its link is fixed". *Source:* NewAdButtons wireframe preview last item [P410]. *Verified by:* component test with an invalid Website button. *Evidence:* apps/portal/src/features/ad-buttons/buttons-step.test.tsx :: The buttons step > adding and changing a button > leaves a button with a problem out of the preview, saying which, until it is fixed ; apps/portal/src/features/ad-buttons/validate-button-drafts.test.ts :: turning typed buttons into a card > the preview > names the buttons it leaves out by their number, never by what was typed (the note names buttons by number, "Not shown until fixed: button 4.", so typed text never reaches it).
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-BUTTONS-09** An "+ Add button" control adds a new button row and is not available once there are 6 buttons. *Source:* NewAdButtons wireframe "+ Add button"; plan §Step 2 cards "≤ 6 actions" (line 138) [P411]. *Verified by:* component test: the control is disabled or gone at 6. *Evidence:* apps/portal/src/features/ad-buttons/buttons-step.test.tsx :: The buttons step > the order and the number of buttons > holds at most six buttons, and says so.
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-BUTTONS-10** The phone preview is titled "What listeners see" and has a width switch "Small phone · 320" / "Large phone · 411", with the pressed option marked `aria-pressed`. *Source:* NewAdButtons wireframe section "Phone preview" [P412]. *Verified by:* component test of labels and pressed state. *Evidence:* apps/portal/src/features/ad-buttons/buttons-step.test.tsx :: The buttons step > on a phone > can show the preview at the two phone widths listeners have.
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-BUTTONS-11** The preview shows, top to bottom: the header image in a 2:1 box, "<client> · via <station> · <frequency>", the offer title, then the buttons in order, full width. *Source:* NewAdButtons wireframe preview; plan §Mobile B, Result pattern (lines 556–559) [P413]. *Verified by:* component test of the order; the image box keeps 2:1 at both widths. *Evidence:* apps/portal/src/components/listener-card-preview.test.tsx :: ListenerCardPreview > shows who the ad is for, its title, and each button in order (no header image or offer title: they are not in the Step 2 card, plan decision 3; the ad title is the heading).
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-BUTTONS-12** In the preview the first button is filled (primary), the second a soft tonal button (secondary) and the third an outlined button. *Source:* NewAdButtons wireframe preview buttons; plan §Mobile A, Schema renderer style mapping (line 539) [P414]. *Verified by:* component test of the style per position. *Evidence:* apps/portal/src/features/ad-buttons/validate-button-drafts.test.ts :: turning typed buttons into a card > the preview > styles a button by its place in the list, so fixing a hidden first button brings back the main one ; apps/portal/src/features/ad-buttons/validate-button-drafts.test.ts :: turning typed buttons into a card > a good set of buttons > makes a card from what was typed: numbers, links and places tidied, labels trimmed, optional parts left out when empty.
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-BUTTONS-13** Each field in a button row has an accessible name that includes the button's number: "Button N type", "Button N label", "Button N phone number", "Button N WhatsApp number", "Button N first message" and "Button N web address". *Source:* NewAdButtons wireframe `aria-label`s ("Button 1 type", "Button 1 label", "Button 1 phone number", "Button 2 WhatsApp number", "Button 2 first message", "Button 4 web address"). *Verified by:* Testing Library `getByLabelText` finds each field by these names for buttons 1 to 4. *Evidence:* apps/portal/src/features/ad-buttons/buttons-step.test.tsx :: The buttons step > fields a screen reader can tell apart > names each field after its button, so "Phone number" in button 2 is not mistaken for the one in button 1 (built names read "Button N Type", "Button N Label", "Button N Phone number" and so on).
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-BUTTONS-14** The phone preview's 320/411 width switch is a button group with the accessible name "Preview width". *Source:* NewAdButtons wireframe `role="group" aria-label="Preview width"`. *Verified by:* component test: `getByRole('group', {name: 'Preview width'})` contains the two buttons with their pressed states. *Evidence:* apps/portal/src/features/ad-buttons/buttons-step.test.tsx :: The buttons step > on a phone > can show the preview at the two phone widths listeners have.

### Portal: Ad detail screen

- [x] **S2-PORTAL-AD-DETAIL-01** The Ad detail screen shows the ad's processing status and polls the API while the ad is `PROCESSING`, changing the badge to "Ready" when the API says `READY`. *Source:* plan §Step 2 screens "Ad detail (status polling while processing)" (line 144) [P171]. *Verified by:* component test with a fake timer. *Evidence:* apps/portal/src/features/ads/ad-detail-page.test.tsx :: Ad page > keeping up with the ad > keeps asking while the audio is being prepared, says when it is ready for listeners, then stops asking ; apps/portal/src/features/ads/ad-detail-page.test.tsx :: Ad page > keeping up with the ad > asks again by itself while the audio is still being received, until it has arrived ; apps/portal/src/features/ads/choose-ad-refresh-interval.test.ts :: chooseAdRefreshInterval > checks an ad whose audio is still being received every 5 seconds at first, then less often, settling at once a minute.
- [x] **S2-PORTAL-AD-DETAIL-02** The Ad detail screen stops polling once the ad leaves `PROCESSING` (`READY`, `NEEDS_REVIEW` or `FAILED`). *Source:* plan §Step 2 screens (line 144); plan §C "Polling … while any row is processing" (line 440) [P172]. *Verified by:* component test: no further status requests after a `READY` response. *Evidence:* apps/portal/src/features/ads/ad-detail-page.test.tsx :: Ad page > keeping up with the ad > keeps asking while the audio is being prepared, says when it is ready for listeners, then stops asking ; apps/portal/src/features/ads/choose-ad-refresh-interval.test.ts :: chooseAdRefreshInterval > does not check an ad that only changes when the person changes it (a published ad is still checked once a minute so its ON AIR badge stays true, S2-D26).

### Tests required by Step 2

- [x] **S2-TESTS-01** An API end-to-end test proves the sign-in, refresh and sign-out cookie behaviour: cookies set, rotated and cleared, and it fails if any cookie attribute or the rotation is removed. *Source:* plan §Step 2 tests (line 146) [P179]. *Verified by:* the test exists in the API suite and is green. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > signing in > sets session cookies with exactly the safe attributes and returns the user and stations, never a token ; apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > sessions > refresh replaces both cookies, and the new ones work ; apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > sessions > a refresh token used again after its grace (three extra uses) ends the session: every cookie stops working ; apps/api/test/authentication.test.ts :: portal sign-in and sessions > sessions > signing out ends the session at once: the same access cookie is refused on the next request.
- [x] **S2-TESTS-02** An API end-to-end IDOR test proves station B gets 404 on every route that takes one of station A's ad ids: `GET /ads/:id`, `/complete`, `/card`, `/schedule` and `/publish`. *Source:* plan §Step 2 tests (line 146) [P180]. *Verified by:* the test exists and is green. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > who can see and change ads > another station gets 404 on every one of this station's ads ; apps/api/test/cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts :: buttons, schedules and publishing > who may change buttons, schedules and publishing > another station gets 404 on this station's ad, whatever it tries.

- [x] **S2-TESTS-04** An API end-to-end test proves a card with a bad phone number is rejected by `PUT /ads/:id/card`. *Source:* plan §Step 2 tests (line 146) [P182]. *Verified by:* the test exists and is green. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts :: buttons, schedules and publishing > buttons (action cards) > refuses a javascript: link or a local phone number, naming the field and the rule, never echoing the value.
- [x] **S2-TESTS-05** An API end-to-end test proves the upload round trip against the local object store: create the draft → PUT to the presigned URL → complete → `PROCESSING` (with an `ingest` job, see S2-ADS-07). *Source:* plan §Step 2 tests (line 146) [P183]. *Verified by:* the test runs green against the compose store. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > completing an upload > accepts a real %s file, moves the ad to processing, audits it, and is safe to repeat (MP3, WAV and M4A through the presigned URL to the compose object store; the ingest job is S2-ADS-07, deferred to Step 3 by S2-D12).
